Often he felt a slight irritation at the careless person who had made notes in his margins and dog-eared the pages.

And Henry Morgan had got himself a great plantation and a great power. Under his captaincy the earth flowered and increased. He was making the land give four times as much as it had before. The slaves worked deliriously under the whips which followed them to the fields, but there was nothing personal in the whips. The old overseer had delighted in punishment, but Henry Morgan was not cruel. He was merciless. He merely speeded the wheels of his factory. One could not think of being kind to a sprocket or a flywheel, and no more could this boy think of pampering his slaves.

Henry was forcing money out of the ground, and from it was adding to his hoard in the box under his bed﻿—a little from the season's sale of cane and a trifle in the buying of new cattle. It was not stealing, but only a kind of commission for his success. The little pile of golden coins grew and grew for the time when Henry Morgan should go a-buccaneering and take a Spanish town.

===Chinese===
---

亨利来了两年之后，那监工刑满获释了。他发现自己的自由对那颗早已习惯被外力管束的脑子来说，是一剂太猛的药。那根弦断了，狂怒涌进他心里，他沿路喊着、打着每一个过路人。到了夜里，他的癫狂变得可怕而暴烈。他在自己的绞架下在地上打滚，嘴里吐着带血的泡沫，奴隶们恐惧地看着。终于他爬起来，头发散乱，眼里是疯子的、烈火般的目光。他抓起一支火把冲进了田里。亨利·摩根在他冲进密密的甘蔗行时，一枪把他打死了。

「谁有我更懂这儿的活儿，您又能更信得过谁呢，先生？」年轻的亨利问种植园主，「我在书里学到的、还有我留心观察到的，能让这座种植园的出产翻上一百倍。」

于是他成了远不止监工的人。

亨利把绞架从广场上挪走了，从那以后，绞刑都改在夜里偷偷进行。这不是出于仁慈。他凭自己的推演知道：未知之物永远成不了寻常之物；那些看不见的刑罚，对剩下的奴隶来说，远比在日光下看见的更为可怖。

亨利在和奴隶打交道中学到了许多。他知道绝不能让他们看穿他在想什么，因为一旦那样，他们就以某种说不清道不明的方式抓住了他的把柄，难以摆脱。他必须对下面的人冷淡、疏远、刻薄。除了少数例外，他们会把刻薄当作他高人一等的标志。人们总相信他看起来的样子，而他几乎能装成任何样子。

一个人若是穿得鲜亮，所有人都当他有钱有势，待他也便恭恭敬敬。当他说话像是有意为之时，几乎所有人都当他真是有意为之时。而他所学的诸课之中最重要的一课是——假如他一连九笔交易都干干净净、账目严明，那么第十次他尽可以想偷多少偷多少，谁也想不到怀疑他，只要他先前那九次足够强硬地让所有人都看在眼里。

床下箱子里越堆越高的一摞金币，充分证明了这最后一课的道理。而他身体力行着自己所有的教诲。他从不给任何人留下哪怕一丝把柄，也不让人窥见他的动机、手段、本事和短处。既然大多数人自己都不相信自己，他们便也不会相信一个他们看穿与自己无二的人。

这些规矩是他从生活里一点点捡拾来的，直到他成了种植园的主人，直到詹姆斯·弗劳尔凄然地倚仗他的主意和决断，直到加勒比人、黑人和犯了罪的白人都又恨又怕他，却撼不动他分毫——抓不到任何把柄来伤他。

詹姆斯·弗劳尔快活极了——比他这辈子任何时候都快活——因为这个男孩把种植园那可怕的担子从他肩上卸了下来。他再也不必操心耕种的事。他越来越深地埋进他的书堆里。而如今他已渐入老境，同一本书他读了一遍又一遍却浑然不觉。他常常对那个在他书页边上批注、折角的粗心家伙生出一丝恼怒。

而亨利·摩根为自己挣下了一座大种植园和一种大权柄。在他的统率下，土地开花、丰产。他让这片地的出产翻到了从前的四倍。奴隶们在跟随他们下田的皮鞭下癫狂地干活，可那鞭子不带半点私人恩怨。老监工以惩罚为乐，亨利·摩根却不残忍。他只是无情。他不过是给他的工厂加了点速。人不会去想对一只链轮或飞轮行善，这男孩同样也不会想去娇纵他的奴隶。

亨利把金钱从地皮里榨出来，再用它添进床下箱子里那笔积蓄——从这一季卖甘蔗的钱里抠一点，买新牲口时再捞一点。这不算偷，不过是他成功的一种佣金罢了。那一小摞金币越攒越多，为了有朝一日亨利·摩根要去当海盗、攻下一座西班牙城。

### V / V

===Original===
Henry had served three years, and, though he was only eighteen, he was grown and strong. His crisp black hair seemed to curl more tightly to his head, and his mouth, from dealing with the slaves, was more firm than ever. He gazed about him and knew that he should be satisfied, but his eyes had never lost the trick of looking out beyond distance and over the edge of the present. A little hectoring wish ran through his waking and dreaming like a thin red line. He must get back to the sea and ships. The sea was his mother and his mistress, and the goddess who might command and find him ready and alert for service. Why, his very name, in the ancient Briton tongue, signified one who lived by the sea. Yes, the ships were calling to him cruelly now. His heart sailed out, away from him with every passing merchantman.

In the big house he had studied and considered what navigation there was in books, and in the plantation's little sloop he had gone cruising in the near waters. But this was the play of a child, he thought, and it was not preparing him to be an expert sailor. It was necessary for him to learn avidly, for in the near future he must go a-buccaneering and take a Spanish town. This was the silver throne of all his desire.

And so, one evening﻿—

"There is a thing I should like to be speaking of, sir."

James Flower raised his eyes from his book and laid his head back in the chair.

"If we had a ship to carry our produce to Jamaica," Henry continued, "we should be saving a large deal in freightage. The cost of such a ship would soon enough be eaten away by the profits. Too, we might carry the produce of the other plantations at a smaller fee than the merchantmen ask."

"But where might one come on such a ship?" James Flower inquired.

"There is one in the harbor now, one of two masts and﻿—"

"Then buy her; buy her, and see to it. You know more about these things than I do. By the way, here is an interesting conjecture on the inhabitants of the moon. 'They may be totally unlike human beings,' he read. 'Their necks could easily be﻿—'"

"It will be seven hundred pounds, sir."

"What will be seven hundred pounds? You seem not to pay attention as you used, Henry. Do listen to this paragraph; it is both entertaining and instructive﻿—"

===Chinese===
亨利已服了三年役，虽说才十八岁，却已长得壮实。他那头乌黑的鬈发似乎贴得更紧了，而他的嘴，因为常年和奴隶打交道，比从前更加坚毅。他环顾四周，知道自己该知足了，可他的眼睛始终没改掉那个毛病——总望向远方之外、望过眼前的边界。一种挥之不去的、咄咄逼人的心愿，像一条细细的红线，穿过他醒时和梦里。他必须回到大海和船上去。大海是他的母亲、他的情人，是那位可以发号施令而发现他早已整装待发、随时听命的女神。要知道，他的姓氏，用古不列颠语来说，正意味着「居于海上之人」。是的，此刻船正残忍地呼唤着他。他的心随每一艘驶过的商船出航，离他而去。

在大宅子里，他研读了书里所能找到的一切航海术，又驾着种植园那条小单桅快船在近海巡游。可他觉得这不过是孩子气的玩意儿，并不能让他成为一名内行的水手。他必须如饥似渴地学，因为不久的将来他就要去当海盗、攻下一座西班牙城。这是他全部欲望的银色宝座。

于是，一天傍晚——

「有件事我想跟您说说，先生。」

詹姆斯·弗劳尔从书上抬起眼，把头靠回椅背。

「要是我们有条船把我们的物产运到牙买加，」亨利接着说，「就能省下一大笔运费。那条船的本钱，很快就会被赚回来的利润抵消掉。再说，我们还可以用比商船更低的价钱，替别的种植园运货。」

「可上哪儿弄这么一条船呢？」詹姆斯·弗劳尔问。

「眼下港里就有一条，双桅的，而且——」

「那就买下来；买下来，这事你去办。这些事你比我在行。对了，这儿有一段关于月球居民的有趣推测。『他们也许与人类截然不同，』」他念道，「『他们的脖子很可能——』」

「这要七百镑，先生。」

「什么要七百镑？亨利，你好像不如从前专心了。你听听这一段；既有趣又有教益——」

===Original===
---

Henry careened the ship, and when he had scraped and painted her, he named her *Elizabeth* and put to sea. He had what is known as "hands" to a horseman, a warm feeling of the personality of his boat. He must learn the rules of navigation, of course; but even before that something of the spirit of the ship crept into his soul, and part of him went back to her. It was a steadfast love, a steady understanding of the sea. By the thrill of her deck and the smooth touch of the wheel, he knew instinctively how close he could bring her into the wind. He was like a man who, laying his head on his mistress' breast, reads the flux of her passions in her breathing.

Now he could have run away from Barbados and gone to plundering in the staunch *Elizabeth*, but there was no need. His hoard was not great enough and he was too young; and in addition, he felt a curious, shamefaced love for James Flower.

Henry was content for a little while. The lust that all men have in varying degrees﻿—some for the flash of cards, and some for wine, and some for the bodies of women﻿—was in Henry Morgan, satisfied with the deck's lunge and pitch and the crack of canvas. The wind, blowing out of a black, dreadful sky, was a cup of wine to him, and a challenge, and a passionate caress.

He sailed to Jamaica with the crops and beat about among the islands. The returns from the plantation mounted, and Henry's box of coins was growing heavy.

But after a few months, a dull, torturing desire came to him. It was the yearning of the little boy, revivified and strong. The *Elizabeth* had sated his old lust and left a new one. He thought it was plunder that called him: the beautiful things of silk and gold and the admiration of men, and on these his heart set more zealously than ever.

Henry went to the brown women and the black in the slave huts, striving to dull his hungering if he could not satisfy it; and they received him, cow-eyed and passive, anxious to please. They hoped that of his favor they might receive more food or a jug of rum as a gift. Each time, he came away with disgust and a little pity for their poor, hopeful prostitution.

===Chinese===
---

亨利把船倾侧过来检修，刮净、刷上漆之后，给她取名「伊丽莎白号」（Elizabeth），下了海。他身上有一种骑马人所说的「手感」——一种对他这条船脾性的亲切体会。航海的规则他当然得学；可甚至在那之前，这条船的某种灵性就已悄悄钻进了他的灵魂，他身上的一部分也回到了她那里。那是一种坚定的爱，一种对大海的、沉稳的理解。凭甲板的悸动和舵柄的顺滑触感，他本能地就知道能把船头压到多近的风向。他就像一个男人，把头枕在情人的胸膛上，从她的呼吸里读出她情欲的涨落。

此刻他本可以从巴巴多斯逃走，驾着结实的「伊丽莎白号」去劫掠，可他没必要。他的积蓄还不够多，他也还太年轻；况且，他对詹姆斯·弗劳尔还怀着一种古怪的、羞于启齿的爱。

亨利暂且满足了些时日。所有男人都有的、程度不一的欲念——有的沉迷于纸牌的闪烁，有的沉迷于酒，有的沉迷于女人的身体——这种欲念也在亨利·摩根身上，却被甲板的俯仰颠簸和帆布的啪啪作响所满足。从漆黑、可怖的天空里吹来的风，对他是一杯酒，一次挑战，一记热烈的抚爱。

他载着收成驶往牙买加（Jamaica），在群岛间来回穿梭。种植园的收益节节攀升，亨利那箱金币越来越沉。

可过了几个月，一种沉闷、折磨人的渴望攫住了他。那是那个小男孩的渴望，死灰复燃，愈发强烈。「伊丽莎白号」喂饱了他旧的欲念，却又留下一个新的。他以为是劫掠在召唤他：丝绸和黄金那些美丽的东西，以及人们的景仰，他的心比以往更热切地扑在这些上面。

亨利到奴隶棚屋里去找那些棕色和黑色的女人，想在他无法餍足的时候至少钝化那饥渴；她们母牛般温顺、被动地接纳他，巴望着讨他欢心。她们指望靠他的垂青多得些吃食，或得到一壶朗姆酒作赏赐。每一次，他离开时都满心厌恶，又带着一点怜悯，怜悯她们那种卑微而满怀希望的卖身。

===Original===
Once, in the slave dock at Port Royal, he found Paulette and bought her for a servant in the house. She was lithe, yet rounded; fierce and gentle in a moment. Poor little slave of the jumbled bloods, she was Spanish and Carib and Negro and French. The heritage of this ragbag ancestry was hair like a cataract of black water, eyes as blue as the sea, set in oriental slits, and a golden, golden skin. Hers was a sensuous, passionate beauty﻿—limbs that twinkled like golden flames. Her lips could writhe like slender, twisting serpents or bloom like red flowers. She was a little child, yet old in life. She was a Christian, but she worshiped wood spirits and sang low chants in honor of the Great Snake.

Henry thought of her as a delicate machine perfectly made for pleasure, a sexual contraption. She was like those tall, cool women of the night who ride with the wings of sleep﻿—soulless bodies﻿—bodies of passionate dreams. He built for her a tiny, vine-clad house roofed with banana leaves and there he played at love.

At first Paulette was only grateful to him for bringing her to a lazy, easy life, with comfort and days of little toil, but later she fell frenziedly in love with him. She watched his face like a quick terrier, waiting to jump with wild pleasure at one word or fall fawning in the dust at another.

When Henry was serious or distraught, she was afraid; then she would kneel before her little ebony figure of a jungle god and pray to the Virgin for his love. Sometimes she put out cups of milk for the winged Jun-Jo-Bee which keeps men true. With the frantic, tender arts of her mixed bloods, she strove to hold him surely in her sight. From her body and her hair there came a rich orient odor, for she rubbed herself with sandalwood and myrrh.

===Chinese===
有一次，在皇家港（Port Royal）的奴隶码头上，他相中了波莱特（Paulette），把她买下来做个屋里的侍女。她身段柔韧，却又丰腴；转眼之间忽而泼辣、忽而温柔。这个血统驳杂的可怜小奴隶，身上兼有西班牙人、加勒比人、黑人和法兰西人的血统。这堆破布袋似的祖传血统，给了她一头如黑色瀑布般的秀发，一双蓝得像海水、却长在东方人细长眼缝里的眼睛，和一身金灿灿的肌肤。她那是一种肉感的、热烈的美——四肢像金色的火焰般闪烁。她的嘴唇能像纤细、扭动的蛇一样蠕动，也能像红花一样绽放。她是个小小的孩童，却已历尽沧桑。她是个基督徒，可她崇拜林木的精灵，还低声吟唱着赞美大蛇的曲子。

亨利把她看作一台为欢愉而精制的、灵巧的机器，一具性的装置。她像那些高大、冷艳的夜的女子，乘着睡眠的翅膀而来——没有灵魂的肉体——满是热烈春梦的肉体。他给她盖了一间小小的、爬满藤蔓、覆着芭蕉叶顶的屋子，在那里同她玩着爱情的游戏。

起初波莱特只是感激他把她带进一种慵懒、安逸的日子，舒适而少劳作；可后来她疯了一样爱上了他。她像一只机灵的㹴犬一样盯着他的脸，等着为某一句话而欢喜得跳将起来，又为另一句话而匍匐在尘土里摇尾乞怜。

亨利若一本正经或心不在焉，她就害怕；于是她跪在她那尊乌木丛林小神像前，向圣母祈求他的爱。有时她摆出几杯牛奶，献给那能叫男人忠贞的有翅的 Jun-Jo-Bee。她以她那混血的、狂热而温柔的手段，拼命想把他牢牢攥在视线之内。她的身体和头发散发出一股浓郁的东方气息，因为她用檀香和没药涂抹自己。

===Original===
When he was gloomy﻿—

"Do you love Paulette?" she would ask. "Do you love Paulette? Are you sure you love Paulette?"

"Why, certainly, I love Paulette. How could a man see Paulette, little dear Paulette﻿—how touch the lips of sweet Paulette﻿—and not be loving her?" And his eyes would go wandering to the sea below, seeking and seeking along the curved shore.

"But do you surely, surely love Paulette? Come, kiss the little breasts of your own Paulette."

"Yes, surely I love Paulette. There! I have kissed them and the charm is made. Now do be still a little. Hear the pounding of the frogs. I wonder what startled the old bearded monkey in the tree there; some slave, perhaps, out stealing fruit." And his eyes would go wandering restlessly to sea.

As the year went on, the soil of her love thrust up strong vines of choking fear. She knew that when he finally deserted her she would be far more than just alone. She might be forced to kneel in the field rows and dig about the plants with her fingers as the other women did. And then, one day, she would be led to the hut of a great Negro with powerful muscles, and he would bruise her little golden body in his beast's clutch and make her pregnant of a black child﻿—a strong, black child that could toil and strain in the sun when it was grown. This happened to all the other slave women of the island. The half of her mind that was very old shuddered at this thought, and that same old mind knew well that Henry would leave her one day.

===Chinese===
他闷闷不乐时——

「你爱波莱特吗？」她会问，「你爱波莱特吗？你真的、真的爱波莱特吗？」

「哎，当然，我爱波莱特。一个男人怎么能见到波莱特、亲爱的波莱特——怎么能碰到甜美的波莱特的嘴唇——而不爱她呢？」可他的眼睛却溜向下方的大海，沿着那弯弯曲曲的海岸寻啊、寻啊。

「可你真的、真的爱波莱特吗？来，亲亲你的波莱特的小胸脯。」

「是的，我真的爱波莱特。瞧！我亲了，咒语就成了。现在安静一会儿吧。你听青蛙的鼓噪。我纳闷树上那只长胡子老猴子受了什么惊；也许是哪个奴隶出来偷果子。」可他的眼睛又不安地飘向了大海。

随着这一年过去，她爱情的土壤里抽出了强韧的、令人窒息的恐惧之藤。她知道，等他最终抛弃她，她将比孤单还要糟。她也许会被迫跪在田垄里，像别的女人一样用手指在作物旁刨土。然后，有朝一日，她会被领到一个肌肉强健的黑人大汉的棚屋里，他在野兽般的攫取中揉碎她那小小的金色身躯，让她怀上一个黑孩子——一个结实、强壮的黑孩子，长大后能在烈日下苦干受累。岛上所有别的女奴隶都遭过这样的事。她那半颗苍老的心一想到这个就发抖，而那颗苍老的心也深知，亨利终有一天会离开她。

===Original===
Then, to her child's mind, appeared the doorway for the passage of her fright. If only he would marry her﻿—impossible it seemed, yet stranger things had been﻿—if only he would marry her, then she need never fear. For those strange beings, wives, were, in some curious way, by some divine intent, shielded from ugly and uncomfortable things. Ah! she had seen them in Port Royal, surrounded by their men to keep foul contact off, breathing through scented cloths to deaden the vile smells, and sometimes with little pellets of cotton in their ears to stop the cursing of the streets from entering. And Paulette knew﻿—had she not been told?﻿—that in their homes they lay in great, soft beds, and languidly gave orders to their slaves.

This was the blessed state she dared to hope for. And her body was not enough, she knew. Often it failed in its soft potency. If she fed him full of love, he did not come again to her bower for a time; and when she refused him to make his passion mount, either he went sullenly away or laughed and flung her roughly on the low, palm couch. She must cast about for some compelling force, some very powerful means to make him marry her.

When Henry went away with cocoa for Port Royal, she was scarcely sane. She knew his love for the ship, his passion for the sea, and she was furiously jealous of them. In her mind she saw him fondle the wheel with the strong, dear touch of lover's fingers. Ah! she could scratch and tear that wheel which robbed her.

===Chinese===
于是，在她那孩童般的心里，出现了一扇门，好让她的恐惧从那里通过。要是他肯娶她——这看来不可能，可更离奇的事也曾有过——要是他肯娶她，那她就再也无须害怕了。因为那些奇怪的生灵，妻子们，不知怎地，凭着某种神圣的安排，被庇护在一切丑陋、难堪之事之外。啊！她在皇家港见过她们，被她们的男人团团围住，挡开污浊的接触，用洒了香水的手帕掩着口鼻以抵挡恶臭，有时耳朵里塞着小棉团，好让街上的咒骂钻不进去。波莱特知道——难道没人告诉过她吗？——她们在自家的大而柔软的床上躺着，慵懒地向奴隶们发号施令。

这就是她敢去奢望的福分了。而她也知道，光凭她的身子是不够的。那身子常常在它柔软的效力上失灵。要是她把他用爱喂得太饱，他就有一阵子不再到她的闺房来；而要是她拒绝他、好叫他的情欲上涨，他要么闷闷不乐地走开，要么一笑，把她粗鲁地摔到那低矮的棕榈榻上。她得另寻某种不可抗拒的力量，某种非常强有力的法子，让他娶她。

亨利载着可可去皇家港时，她简直要疯了。她知道他爱那条船，爱那片海，她为此嫉妒得发狂。她在脑海里看见他用情人那有力的、亲爱的手指抚摸舵柄。啊！她恨不得把那个夺走他的舵柄抓烂、撕碎。

===Original===
She must make him love Paulette more than the ships, more than the sea, or anything on earth, so that he would marry her. Then she could walk haughtily among the huts and spit at the slaves; then she need never think of grubbing in the earth or bearing strong black children; then she would have red cloth to wear, and a silver chain to go about her neck. It was even possible that once in a long while her dinner might be brought to her while she lay in bed, pretending to be ill. She wriggled her toes in delight at such a thought, and made up the insulting things she would say to one fat Negress with a spiteful tongue, when only she should be a wife. That old, fat wretch had called Paulette a slut before a gathering. Paulette had pulled out lots of hair before she could be held with her arms to her sides﻿—but still, that black one should see, one day. Paulette would have her whipped on the cross.

While Henry was away a trading ship came into port, and Paulette went to the beach to see the things she brought and to watch the wind-brown sailors come ashore. And one of them, a great, broad Irishman, laden with black rum, pursued and captured her against a pile of boxes. Strong and quick, she struggled to elude him, but he held her tightly, swaying though he was.

"I've caught a fairy to mend my shoes," he laughed, and peered into her face. "Sure enough, 'tis a fairy." And then he saw that she was small and very beautiful, and he spoke tenderly and low.

"You're a lovely fairy﻿—lovelier than the eyes of me have ever seen. Could a slim little body like you be thinking anything about a great, ugly hulk like me, I wonder? Come off and marry me, and you shall have anything 'tis in the power of a sailor to give you."

"No!" she cried. "No!" and slipped under his arm and away. The sailor sat in the sand staring dully before him.

"'Twas a dream," he whispered; "'tis only a dream from the spirits. There's no such thing to be happening to a poor sailor. No; for sailors there be pretty hags with sharp, hard eyes to say, 'Come! money first, sweetheart mine.'"

===Chinese===
她必须让他爱波莱特胜过爱船、胜过爱海、胜过爱世上一切，好让他娶她。那样她就能昂首阔步地走在棚屋之间，朝奴隶们啐唾沫；那样她就再也不必想下地刨土，或者生强壮的黑孩子；那样她就有红布穿，脖子上还能挂一条银链。甚至有可能，隔上好久，她躺着装病时，晚饭会被端到床前。想到这里，她乐得扭动脚趾，还盘算着一旦做了妻子，她要怎样去辱骂那个长舌的胖黑女人。那老胖婆娘当着一伙人的面骂过波莱特是荡妇。波莱特在被按住胳膊之前，揪下了她好大一把头发——不过，那个黑人，总有一天要好看的。波莱特会让人把她绑到十字架上鞭打。

亨利不在时，一艘商船进了港，波莱特到海滩上去看那船运来什么货，看那些被风吹得黝黑的水手上岸。其中一个，一个高大、魁梧的爱尔兰人，灌饱了黑朗姆酒，把她追到一堆箱子前堵住了。她虽强壮灵巧，拼命挣扎想脱身，可他尽管身子直晃，还是把她紧紧攥住。

「我抓到一个小仙女来给我补鞋啦，」他笑道，凑近端详她的脸，「可不，真是个仙女。」接着他看出她又小巧又极美，便压低嗓子，温存地说起话来。

「你是个可爱的仙女——比我这两只眼睛见过的都可爱。我想，像你这么个苗条的小人儿，会不会把我这么个又大又丑的蠢汉放在心上呢？跟我走吧，嫁给我，水手能给你的东西，你样样都有。」

「不！」她喊，「不！」从他臂下溜走，跑开了。那水手坐在沙地里，呆呆地望着前方。

「是个梦，」他低语，「不过是精灵送来的一个梦罢了。哪有这种好事落到一个穷水手头上。没有；落到水手头上的，只有些目光尖刻、硬邦邦的俏婆娘，说着『来呀！先付钱，我的心肝。』」

===Original===
But now Paulette had found the way to make Henry marry her. She would contrive to get drunkenness on him, would trap him with wine, and there would be a priest nearby to come at her hushed call. Oh, surely, stranger things had been!

She laid her snare for him on his first night back from sea﻿—a large stone flagon filled with Peruvian wine, and a priest, bribed with a stolen coin, waiting in the shadow of a tree. Henry was very tired. He had gone out short-handed and helped to work the ship himself. The little vine-clothed hut was a pleasant, restful place to him. A full white moon cast silver splashes in the sea below and strewed the ground with scarves of purple light. Sweetly there sang a little jungle breeze among the palms.

She brought the wine and filled a cup for him. "Do you love Paulette?"

"Ah, yes! as God sees me, I love Paulette; dear, sweet Paulette." Another cup, and still, persistently﻿—"Are you so sure you love Paulette?"

"Paulette is a little star hanging to my breast by a silver chain."

Another cup.

"Do you love none other save only your Paulette?"

"I came longingly to find Paulette; the thought of her sailed on the sea with me." And his arms locked tightly around her little golden waist.

Another and another and another; then his arms fell away from her and his hands clenched. The girl cried fearfully,

"Oh! do you love Paulette?" for Henry had grown morose and strange and cold.

===Chinese===
可这会儿波莱特找到了让亨利娶她的法子。她要设法把他灌醉，用酒把他套住，附近再备好一个神父，等她压着嗓子一唤就来。哦，肯定有的，更离奇的事也曾有过！

她在他从海上归来的头一晚就布下了陷阱——一大壶盛满秘鲁葡萄酒的石壶，还有一个被偷来的硬币买通的神父，在树影里候着。亨利累极了。他这趟出航人手不足，自己都亲自动手帮着驾船。那间爬满藤蔓的小屋对他是个惬意、安适的所在。一轮圆满的白月把银色的光斑洒进下方的大海，又在地面上铺开一条条紫色的光带。一阵小小的丛林微风在棕榈间轻唱。

她端来酒，给他斟满一杯。「你爱波莱特吗？」

「啊，是的！老天在上，我爱波莱特；亲爱的、甜美的波莱特。」又一杯，她还是锲而不舍——「你真的那么爱波莱特吗？」

「波莱特是一颗小星星，用一根银链挂在我胸前。」

又一杯。

「除了你的波莱特，你不爱别的人吗？」

「我是满心渴望地来找波莱特的；在大海上，思念一直陪着我航行。」他的双臂紧紧搂住她那小小的、金色的腰。

又一杯、又一杯、又一杯；然后他的双臂松开了她，两手攥紧。姑娘恐惧地喊道：

「哦！你爱波莱特吗？」因为亨利变得阴沉、古怪而冷漠了。

===Original===
"I shall tell you of an old time," he said hoarsely. "I was a little boy, a joyous little boy, yet old enough to love. There was a girl﻿—and she was named Elizabeth﻿—the daughter of a wealthy squire. Ah! she was lovely as this night about us, quiet and lovely as that slender palm tree under the moon. I loved her with that love a man may exercise but once. Even our hearts seemed to go hand in hand. How I remember the brave plans we told﻿—she and I, there, sitting on a hillside in the night. We were to live in a great house and have dear children growing up about us. You can never know such love, Paulette.

"Ah, well! It could not last. The gods slay happiness in jealousy. Nothing good can last. A gang of bastard sailors roved through the land and carried me off﻿—a little boy to be sold for a slave in the Indies. It was a bitter thing to lose Elizabeth﻿—a bitter thing the years cannot forget." And he was weeping softly by her side.

Paulette was bewildered by the change in him. She stroked his hair and his eyes, until his breath came more calmly. Then she began again, with almost helpless patience, like a teacher questioning a dull child.

"But﻿—do you love Paulette?"

He leaped up and glared at her.

"You? Love you? Why, you are just a little animal! a pretty little golden animal, for sure, but a form of flesh﻿—no more. May one worship a god merely because he is big, or cherish a land which has no virtue save its breadth, or love a woman whose whole realm is her flesh? Ah, Paulette! you have no soul at all! Elizabeth had a white winged soul. I love you﻿—yes﻿—with what you have to be loved﻿—the body. But Elizabeth﻿—I loved Elizabeth with my soul."

Paulette was puzzled.

"What is this soul?" she asked. "And how may I get one if I have not one already? And where is this soul of yours that I have never seen it or heard it at all? And if they cannot be seen, or heard, or touched, how do you know she had this soul?"

"Hush!" he cried furiously. "Hush! or I box your mouth and have you whipped on the cross. You speak of things beyond you. What can you know of love that lies without your fleshly juggling?"

===Chinese===
「我给你讲讲从前的事吧，」他哑着嗓子说，「那时我是个小男孩，一个快乐的小男孩，却已到了懂得爱的年纪。有个姑娘——她叫伊丽莎白——一个富裕乡绅的女儿。啊！她像我们眼前的这个夜晚一样可爱，像月光下那棵纤细的棕榈树一样沉静而可爱。我用那种一个男人一生只能付出一次的爱去爱她。我们的心仿佛都手牵着手。我至今记得我们说过的那些豪情万丈的计划——她和我，坐在夜里的山坡上。我们要住在一所大宅子里，身边有可爱的孩子长大成人。你永远体会不到那样的爱，波莱特。

「唉！可这没能长久。众神出于嫉妒杀死了幸福。凡是好的东西都不能长久。一伙杂种水手在乡间流窜，把我掳走——一个小男孩，被卖到印度群岛当奴隶。失去伊丽莎白是一件痛心的事——岁月也无法忘却的痛心之事。」他在她身旁轻轻地啜泣起来。

波莱特被他这变化弄糊涂了。她抚摸他的头发和眼睛，直到他的呼吸渐渐平稳下来。然后她又开了口，带着近乎无可奈何的耐心，像一个老师盘问一个迟钝的孩子。

「可是——你爱波莱特吗？」

他跳起来，瞪着她。

「你？爱你？嘿，你不过是只小畜生！一只漂亮的小金畜生，没错，可不过是一具肉身——仅此而已。难道仅仅因为一位神身形巨大，人就该去崇拜他？或者仅仅因为一片土地幅员辽阔而别无长处，就该去珍爱它？或者仅仅因为一个女人的全部疆域就是她的肉身，就该去爱她？啊，波莱特！你压根儿没有灵魂！伊丽莎白有一颗洁白的、长着翅膀的灵魂。我爱你——是的——用你有待被爱之处——那肉身——来爱。可伊丽莎白——我是用我的灵魂爱伊丽莎白的。」

波莱特困惑了。

「这灵魂是什么？」她问，「要是我没有，我又怎么能弄到一个？还有，你这灵魂在哪儿，我怎么从没见过它、听过它？要是它看不见、听不见、摸不着，你怎么知道她有这灵魂？」

「住嘴！」他怒吼，「住嘴！不然我扇你的嘴，让人把你绑到十字架上鞭打。你说的是你够不着的东西。那超乎你肉身把戏之外的爱你又能懂什么？」

### VI / VI

===Original===
Christmas came to the Hot Tropics, the fourth Christmas of Henry's servitude. And James Flower brought him a small box done up with colored string.

"It is a gift of the season," he said, and his eyes sparkled with delight while Henry untied the package. There was a little teakwood box, and in it, lying on the scarlet silk of its lining, the torn fragments of his slavery. Henry took the shreds of paper from the box and stared at them, and then he laughed unsteadily and put his head down on his hands.

"Now you are no longer a servant, but my son," the planter said. "Now you are my son, whom I have taught strange knowledges﻿—and I shall teach you more, far more. We will live here always and talk together in the evenings."

Henry raised his head.

"Oh! but I cannot, cannot stay. I must be off a-buccaneering."

"You﻿—you cannot stay? But, Henry, I have planned our life. You would not leave me here alone."

"Sir," said Henry, "I must be off a-buccaneering. Why, in all my years it has been the one aim. I must go, sir."

"But, Henry, dear Henry, you shall have half my plantation, and all of it when I am dead﻿—if only you will stay with me."

"That may not be," young Henry cried. "I must be off to make me a name. It is not given that I live a planter. Sir, there are plannings in my head that have grown perfect with pondering. And nothing may be allowed to interfere with them."

James Flower slumped forward in his chair.

"It will be very lonely here without you. I don't quite know what I shall do without you."

===Chinese===
圣诞节来到了炎热的热带，这是亨利服役的第四个圣诞节。詹姆斯·弗劳尔给他带来一个小盒子，用彩色的绳子扎着。

「这是节令的礼物，」他说，亨利解着包裹时，他眼里闪烁着喜悦的光。盒子里是一只小小的柚木盒，里面，在那猩红丝绸的衬里上，躺着他奴隶生涯的、被撕碎的纸片。亨利把那些碎纸从盒里拿出来，凝视着，然后不稳地笑了一声，把头埋进了双手。

「现在你不再是仆人了，而是我的儿子，」种植园主说，「现在你是我的儿子，我教过你种种奇奇怪怪的学问——我还要教你更多，多得多的。我们要永远住在这里，晚上一起聊天。」

亨利抬起头来。

「哦！可我不能，不能留下。我必须出海当海盗去。」

「你——你不能留下？可是，亨利，我把我们一辈子的日子都盘算好了。你总不能把我一个人撇在这儿。」

「先生，」亨利说，「我必须出海当海盗去。嘿，这些年我心里一直就这一个目标。我非走不可，先生。」

「可是，亨利，亲爱的亨利，你可以得到我一半的种植园，等我死了全归你——只要你肯留下来陪我。」

「那不行，」年轻的亨利喊道，「我必须出去闯出个名头来。老天没让我做个种植园主。先生，我脑子里有一些盘算，想来想去已经盘算得滴水不漏了。什么都不能来打搅它们。」

詹姆斯·弗劳尔颓然向前瘫在椅子里。

「没有你，这儿会很冷清的。我都不知道没你我该怎么办。」

===Original===
Henry's mind carried him back to that old time, with Robert smiling into the fire and saying these same words﻿—"It will be so lonely here without you, son." He wondered if his mother still sat coldly upright and silent. Surely she would have got over it. People always got over the things they feared so much. And then he thought of small Paulette who would be crying with terror in her hut when he told her.

"There is a little servant girl," he said; "little Paulette. I have protected her. And if I have ever pleased you, will you do these things for me? Always, always keep her in the house and never let her be sent to the fields, nor whipped, nor bred with any of the blacks. Will you do these things for me surely?"

"Of course I will," James Flower said. "Ah, but it has been good to have you here, Henry﻿—good to hear your voice in the evening. What will I do in the evening now? There is none to take your place, for you have very truly been my son. It will be lonely here without you, boy."

Said Henry, "The toiling I have done in your service has been more than repaid with the knowledge you have poured into my ears these same evenings. And I shall miss you, sir, more than I can say. But can't you understand? I must go a-buccaneering and take a Spanish town, for the thought is on me that if a man planned carefully, and considered his chances and the men he had, the thing might well be done. I have studied the ancient wars, and I must be making a name for myself and a fortune. Then, when I have the admiration of men, perhaps I shall come back to you, sir, and we may sit and talk again in the evenings. You will remember my wish about Paulette?"

"Who is Paulette?" the planter asked.

"Why, the servant girl I mentioned. Never let her go with the slaves, because I am fond of her."

"Ah, yes! I remember! And where do you go now, Henry?"

"To Jamaica. My uncle, Sir Edward, has long been Lieutenant-Governor there in Port Royal. But I have never seen him﻿—well, because I was a bond-servant, and he is a gentleman. I have a letter to him that my father gave me years past. Perhaps he will help me to buy a ship for my plundering."

"I would help you buy a ship. You have been very good to me," the planter said hopefully.

Now Henry was dipped in a kind of shame, for in the box under his bed there glistened a pile of golden coins﻿—over a thousand pounds.

"No," he said, "no; I have more payment in your teaching and in the father you have been to me than money could ever equal." Now he was going, Henry knew that he had grown to love this red-faced, wistful man.

===Chinese===
亨利的思绪飘回了从前那个时候，老罗伯特对着炉火微笑，说着同样的话——「没有你，这儿会很冷清的，孩子。」他不知道母亲是否仍冷冷地、直挺挺地坐着，一声不吭。她总该已经熬过来了吧。人们总能熬过他们那么害怕的事。接着他想到了小波莱特，等他告诉她，她会在棚屋里吓得直哭。

「有个小侍女，」他说，「小波莱特。我一直护着她。要是我曾让您高兴过，您肯不肯替我做这些事？永远、永远让她留在屋里，绝不要把她发配到田里，不要鞭打她，不要让她跟任何黑人生孩子。您一定替我做这些事吗？」

「我当然会。」詹姆斯·弗劳尔说，「啊，有你在这儿真好，亨利——晚上能听到你的声音真好。如今晚上我该怎么办？没人能替代你，因为你实实在在就是我的儿子。没有你，这儿会很冷清的，孩子。」

亨利说：「我为您效的这些劳，已从您这些晚上灌进我耳朵的知识里，得到了远超其值的回报。我也会想念您的，先生，想得没法说。可您难道不明白吗？我必须出海当海盗、攻下一座西班牙城，因为我心里有个念头：要是有个人精心谋划、权衡他的胜算和他手下的人，这事就大有可为。我研习过古代的战争，我必须为自己挣一个名头、一笔财富。等到我赢得了众人的景仰，也许我会回到您这儿来，先生，我们又可以坐下来，在晚上聊天。您会记住我对波莱特的托付吧？」

「波莱特是谁？」种植园主问。

「哎，就是我说的那个侍女呀。千万别让她跟奴隶混在一起，因为我喜欢她。」

「啊，对了！我想起来了！那你现在要去哪儿，亨利？」

「去牙买加。我叔叔爱德华爵士（Sir Edward）长久以来一直是皇家港的副总督。可我从未见过他——嗯，因为我是个契约仆役，而他是位绅士。我父亲多年前给过我一封写给他的信。也许他会帮我买条船，好去劫掠。」

「我愿意帮你买条船。你一直待我很好。」种植园主满怀希望地说。

这时亨利陷入了一种羞愧之中，因为床下的箱子里，一摞金币正闪闪发光——足有一千多镑。

「不，」他说，「不；您对我的教导、您待我如父，这些用钱是永远抵不过的。」如今要走了，亨利才明白，他早已爱上了这个红脸的、怅惘的男人。

===Original===
---

Strong, glistening blacks pulled at the oars of the canoe, and it went skimming toward an anchored ship, a ship commissioned by the States-General to carry black slaves from Guinea to the islands. James Flower, sitting in the canoe's stern, was very red and very silent. But as they neared the ship's side, he lifted up his head and spoke pleadingly to Henry.

"There are books on the shelves that you have never read."

"I shall come back, one day, and read them."

"There are things in my mind I have never told you, boy."

"When I have the admiration of men, I shall come to you and you shall tell them to me."

"You swear it?"

"Well﻿—yes, I swear."

"And how long will it take you to do these things, Henry?"

"I cannot tell; one year﻿—or ten﻿—or twenty. I must make a very admirable name." Henry was climbing over the ship's side.

"I shall be lonely in the evenings, son."

"And I, too, sir. Look! we cast off! Goodbye, sir. You will remember Paulette?"

"Paulette?﻿—Paulette?﻿—Ah, yes; I remember."

===Chinese===
---

结实、油光发亮的黑人划着独木舟的桨，小舟向一艘抛锚的船飞掠而去——那是一艘受荷兰国会之命、从几内亚运送黑奴到各岛的船。詹姆斯·弗劳尔坐在独木舟船尾，涨红了脸，一言不发。可当他们靠近船舷时，他抬起头，恳切地对亨利说起话来。

「书架上有许多你还没读过的书。」

「总有一天我会回来读的。」

「我心里有许多事还没告诉过你，孩子。」

「等我赢得了众人的景仰，我就回到你身边，你再讲给我听。」

「你发誓？」

「嗯——我发誓。」

「那你要花多久才能做成这些事，亨利？」

「我说不准；一年——或者十年——或者二十年。我必须挣一个令人钦佩的名头。」亨利正爬过船舷。

「晚上我会很孤单的，孩子。」

「我也会的，先生。瞧！开船了！再见了，先生。你会记得波莱特吧？」

「波莱特？——波莱特？——啊，对了；我记得。」

### VII / VII

===Original===
Henry Morgan came to the English town of Port Royal and left his baggage on the beach while he went looking for his uncle.

"Do you know where I may find the Lieutenant-Governor?" he asked in the streets.

"His palace is yonder, young man, and who knows he may be in it."

His palace﻿—it was like a British gentleman become an official far from home. It was like the man Robert Morgan had described. His letters dated from the Palace of the Lieutenant-Governor. Henry found the palace, a low grubby house with walls of whitewashed mud and a roof of red tiles badly molded. There was a gaudy halberdier standing at the door, holding his great, ineffectual weapon rigidly before him, the while he maintained a tortured decorum in the face of a swarm of enemy flies.

The halberd lowered across the pathway as Henry approached.

"I am looking for Sir Edward Morgan."

"What do you wish with His Excellency?"

"Why, you see, sir, he is my uncle, and I wish to speak with him."

The soldier scowled suspiciously and stiffened his hold on the halberd. Then Henry remembered his lessons of the plantation. Perhaps this man, for all his red coat, might be something of a slave.

"Get out of my way, you damned pup," he cried. "Get out of my way or I'll see you hanged."

The man cowered and almost dropped his weapon. "Yes, sir. I'll send your word, sir." He blew a little silver call, and when a servant in green lace came to the door, he said:

"A young gentleman to see His Excellency."

===Chinese===
亨利·摩根来到英国人的城镇皇家港，把行李撂在海滩上，便去找他叔叔。

「你知道我在哪儿能找到副总督吗？」他在街上问。

「他的府邸就在那边，年轻人，谁知道他在不在里头。」

他的府邸——这就像一位英国绅士在远离故土的地方当上了官。就像罗伯特·摩根所描述过的那个人。他的信都是署着「副总督府邸」的日期。亨利找到了那府邸，一栋低矮、肮脏的房子，墙是刷了白灰的泥墙，屋顶是粗制滥造的红瓦。门口站着一个花里胡哨的戟兵，把他那把又大又无用的兵器笔直地举在身前，与此同时，面对着一群敌军苍蝇，他还要强撑出一副受罪的庄重来。

亨利走近时，那柄戟横到了路上。

「我要找爱德华·摩根爵士。」

「您找阁下有什么事？」

「嘿，您瞧，先生，他是我叔叔，我想跟他说说话。」

那士兵疑心地皱起眉，把戟攥得更紧。这时亨利想起了他在种植园里学到的本事。也许这个人，别看他穿着红外衣，骨子里跟奴隶也差不了多少。

「滚开，你这该死的小杂种，」他喝道，「滚开，不然我让人绞死你。」

那人缩了缩，差点把兵器掉在地上。「是，先生。我这就给您通报，先生。」他吹了一声小小的银哨，等一个穿绿花边号衣的仆人来到门前，他说：

「一位年轻绅士求见阁下。」

===Original===
Henry was led into a little room made dark with thick, gray hangings edged with dull gold. There were three dim portraits on the walls, in black frames; two cavaliers in plumed hats, holding their swords horizontally so they looked like stiff, slender tails, and a pretty lady with powdered hair and a silken gown which left her shoulders and half her breasts uncovered.

From some place beyond the curtained doorway there came the thin twanging of a harp slow struck. The servant took Henry's letter and left him alone.

And he felt very much alone. It was a house of cold, precise hairsplitting. One was aware of a polite contempt even in the pictured faces on the wall. The British arms were embroidered on the curtains of the door, the lion on one side, holding half the shield, and the unicorn, with his half, on the other. When the curtains hung straight the design was complete. In this room, Henry began to fear his uncle.

But all these thoughts of his were shocked from his brain when Sir Edward appeared. It was his father as he remembered him, and yet never his father. Old Robert would never have had a mustache like an eyelash, and nothing in Robert's life could have made him pinch his lips together until they were as thin as the mustache. These two might have been born alike as beans, but each had created his own mouth.

Robert had spoken truth; this man was his strutting counterpart. But Sir Edward was like an actor, who, though cast in a ridiculous role, yet makes his part seem the correct thing and all others absurd. His purple coat with lace at the neck and wrists, the long rapier, lean as a pencil in a scabbard of gray silk, the gray silk stockings and soft gray shoes with bowed ribbons on them, seemed to Henry the highest type of proper wear. His own good clothing was shabby by comparison.

===Chinese===
亨利被领进一间小屋子，厚重的、镶着暗金色边的灰色帷幔把屋子弄得昏暗。墙上有三幅昏暗的肖像，装在黑框里；两个戴羽饰帽的骑士，横握着剑，那剑看上去像两条僵直、纤细的尾巴；还有一位俏丽的夫人，扑着粉发，穿着一件丝绸长裙，露出肩膀和半边胸脯。

从帷幔门后的某处传来细细的、缓慢拨弄竖琴的铮铮声。仆人接过亨利的信，把他一个人留下了。

他感到分外孤单。这是一座冷冰冰、咬文嚼字、斤斤计较的房子。就连墙上那些画像的脸，都能让人觉出一种彬彬有礼的轻蔑。门的帷幔上绣着英国纹章，狮子在一边，占着盾的一半，独角兽在另一边，占着另一半。帷幔垂直时，图案便完整了。在这间屋子里，亨利开始害怕起他叔叔来。

可当爱德华爵士出现时，这些念头全从他脑子里被震飞了。那是他记忆中的父亲，却又不是他的父亲。老罗伯特绝不会蓄一抹像睫毛似的髭须，罗伯特一生中的任何经历也不会让他把嘴唇抿得像那髭须一样细。这两人也许生得像两颗豆子，但各自都造出了自己的一张嘴。

罗伯特说的是真话；这个人就是他那趾高气扬的翻版。可爱德华爵士又像个演员，虽然分到了一个荒唐的角色，却能让自己的角色显得天经地义，而旁人都显得可笑。他那件紫色外衣，领口和袖口缀着花边，那柄长长的细剑，在一柄灰色丝绸剑鞘里细得像支铅笔，那双灰色丝袜，那双缀着蝴蝶结丝带的柔软灰鞋——在亨利看来，都是最得体的穿着的典范。相形之下，他自己那身好衣裳也显得寒酸了。

===Original===
His uncle had been looking at him steadily, waiting for Henry to speak first.

"I am Henry Morgan, sir﻿—Robert's son," he began simply.

"I see you are. There is a resemblance﻿—a faint likeness. And what may I do for you?"

"Why, I﻿—I don't know. I came to call on you and inform you of my existence."

"That was kind of you﻿—ah﻿—very kind."

It was difficult to broach speech into this field of almost sneering courtesy. Henry asked,

"Have you heard any single thing of my parents in the long five years I have been out?"

"Five years! What have you been doing, pray?"

"I was a bond-servant, sir. But of my parents?"

"Your mother is dead."

"My mother is dead," Henry repeated in a whisper. He wondered if she had died soon after he had gone. He did not feel very badly about it, and yet the words sounded such tremendous things, such final things. This was the end of something that might never be again. "My mother is dead," he murmured. "And my father?"

"I have heard that your father does peculiar things in his rose garden. Squire Rhys wrote me of it. He plucks the full flowers and casts them into the air like one amazed. The ground is covered with petals and the neighbors stand about and laugh at him. Robert was never normal; indeed, he was never quite sane, or he might have gone far with James I. I, for one, always thought he would come to some disgrace or other. He revered nothing worthy of reverence. Why must he do this thing in the open, with the people jeering? It brings ridicule on his﻿—ah﻿—relatives."

"And do you think he is really insane, Uncle?"

"I do not know," Sir Edward said, and added with a touch of impatience, "I merely quoted Squire Rhys' letter. My position does not allow me time for vain conjecture﻿—nor much time for idle conversation," he said pointedly.

===Chinese===
他叔叔一直定定地看着他，等着亨利先开口。

「我是亨利·摩根，先生——罗伯特的儿子。」他直截了当地开了头。

「我看得出来。有几分相像——一点隐约的相似。我能为你做点什么？」

「嘿，我——我不知道。我是来拜见您，告诉您我这个人还存在。」

「你真好——啊——真好。」

要在这片近乎讥诮的客套里插进话去，实在不容易。亨利问：

「我出来这长长的五年里，您可曾听到过关于我父母的任何一点消息？」

「五年！你都干了些什么，请问？」

「我是个契约仆役，先生。可我父母呢？」

「你母亲去世了。」

「我母亲去世了，」亨利轻声重复。他不知道她是不是在他走后不久就死了。他对此并不怎么难过，可这话听来是如此重大、如此决绝的事。这是一件再也不会重现之事的终结。「我母亲去世了，」他喃喃道，「那我父亲呢？」

「我听说你父亲在他的玫瑰园里做些古怪的事。赖斯乡绅写信告诉我的。他把盛开的玫瑰花摘下来，像受了什么惊吓似的抛向空中。地上落满了花瓣，邻居们围在一旁嘲笑他。罗伯特向来不正常；说实在的，他从来就神智不大清楚，要不然他在詹姆斯一世手下本可以大有前途。就拿我来说，我一直觉得他迟早要闹出什么丢脸的事。凡是值得敬畏的东西他一概不敬。他为什么非得当着众人嘲弄的面干这种事呢？这给他——啊——亲戚们招来耻笑。」

「叔叔，您觉得他真的疯了吗？」

「我不知道，」爱德华爵士说，带着一点不耐烦又补了一句，「我不过是引用赖斯乡绅信里的话。我的职位不容许我有工夫作无谓的揣测——也不容许我有多少工夫闲聊，」他话里有话地说。

===Original===
The methodic twanging of the harp had ceased, and now the curtain of the door was thrust aside and a slender girl entered the room. It was difficult to see her in this dark place. It was plain she was not beautiful, but rather proudly pretty. She was softly dressed and her face was pale. Even her hair was pale fragile gold. Altogether she seemed a wan, tired echo of Sir Edward.

The girl was startled at seeing Henry there, and he found that he was a little afraid of her in the same manner that he was growing to fear Sir Edward. She looked at Henry as though he were some distasteful food which only the strict rules of courtesy prevented her from pushing away from her place.

"Your cousin Henry," Sir Edward said shortly; and, "My motherless daughter, Elizabeth." Then, nervously, as though no good could possibly come of this contact, "Hadn't you better practice your music a little longer, my dear?"

She dropped a suggestion of a curtsey to Henry, and in a voice like her father's, greeted him.

"How d'ye do. Yes, sir, I think I had better practice. That last piece is difficult but beautiful." And she disappeared behind the curtain whence came again the slow, accurate striking of the harp.

Henry gripped his resolve, though he was afraid of this man.

"There is a thing I wish to speak of, sir. I want to go a-buccaneering, Uncle﻿—on the sea, in a great ship with guns. And when I have taken prizes, and a cloud of men gather to my reputation, then I would be capturing a Spanish town for plunder and ransom. I am a good sailor, my uncle. I can navigate in any sea, I think; and I have it in me to plan carefully my campaign. I have read a great lot on the ancient wars. The buccaneers have never been the force I mean to make them. Why, I could form armies and navies of them, my dear uncle. In time I would lead the whole Free Brotherhood of the Coast, and it would be an armed power to reckon with.

"These things I have considered in the long years of my slavery. There is a crying in my heart to do these things. I think the end of all my dreaming is a great name and a great fortune. I know my powers. I am twenty years old; I have had several years at sea; and I have a thousand pounds. The man who helps me now﻿—who goes with me as partner﻿—I will make rich. I am so very sure I can do these things﻿—so very sure.

"I ask you, my uncle, to add to my thousand pounds enough so that I may buy a fitted ship and gather the free, brave spirits about me to do my will. If you will place another thousand pounds in my hands, I swear to make you richer than you are."

===Chinese---
===Chinese===
那一阵有板有眼的竖琴铮铮声停了，接着门帷幔被掀开，一个纤瘦的姑娘走进屋来。在这昏暗的地方很难看清她。显然她并不算美，而是一种带着矜持的俏丽。她穿着柔软的衣裳，脸色苍白。连她的头发也是苍白、脆弱的金色。总的看来，她像是爱德华爵士的一个憔悴、疲倦的回声。

那姑娘见到亨利在这儿，吃了一惊，而他发觉自己有点怕她，就像他渐渐害怕爱德华爵士一样。她看着亨利，仿佛他是一道倒胃口的菜，要不是碍于严格的礼数，她早把他从自己面前推开了。

「你表亲亨利，」爱德华爵士简短地说；又说，「我那没娘的女儿，伊丽莎白（Elizabeth）。」然后，神经质地，仿佛这种接触不可能带来任何好处：「亲爱的，你再去多练一会儿琴好不好？」

她向亨利微微一屈膝，用跟她父亲一样的嗓音招呼了他。

「您好。是的，先生，我想我还是去练琴的好。最后那一首很难，可是很美。」说完她消失在帷幔后头，那里又传来缓慢、准确的拨琴声。

亨利攥紧了自己的决心，尽管他怕这个男人。

「有件事我想说说，先生。我要去当海盗，叔叔——在海上，驾一条带炮的大船。等我掳获了猎物，一批人聚到我名下，我就要去攻占一座西班牙城，劫掠、勒索。我是个好水手，叔叔。我想在哪片海上我都能导航；我也有本事精心谋划我的战局。我读过很多关于古时战争的书。海盗从未成为我打算让他们成为的那种力量。嘿，亲爱的叔叔，我能把他们编成陆军和海军。假以时日，我要统领整个海岸的自由兄弟会，让它成为一支不可小觑的武装力量。

「这些事，我在漫长的为奴岁月里都盘算过了。我心里有一股呐喊，要做这些事。我想我一切梦想的终点，是一个响亮的名头和一笔巨大的财富。我了解自己的本事。我二十岁；我在海上待过好几年；我有一千镑。现在肯帮我的人——肯跟我合伙的人——我会让他发财。我如此确信我能做成这些事——如此确信。

「叔叔，我求您，在我的那一千镑之外，再添上足够的钱，好让我买一条装备齐全的船，把那些自由、勇敢的人聚到我身边，听我差遣。您要是肯再拿一千镑交到我手里，我发誓让您比现在更富。」

===Original===
The harp was no longer sounding. At the beginning of the boy's outburst, Sir Edward had held up his hand as though to stop him, but the words plunged on. And when the harp had been silenced, Sir Edward looked uneasily toward the door. Now he seemed to bring his interest back to Henry.

"I have no money to risk in unsure ventures," he said sharply. "And I have no more time for talk. The Governor is coming to consult with me in a moment. But I would say that you are a wild, careless boy who is like to come to hanging of your ventures. Your father is like you, only his is a wildness of the mind.

"And I must inform you that there is peace between Spain and England; not very good feeling, it is true, but still, peace. If you go marauding it will be my duty to see you punished, sorry for it though I may be. The Roundheads are no longer in power and those wild things that Cromwell overlooked are carefully watched now. Remember what I say, for I would not like to hang my nephew. Now I must really bid you good day."

Tears of resentment stood in Henry's eyes.

"Thank you for coming to see me," his uncle said. "Goodbye." And he went through the curtained doorway.

In the street, Henry walked moodily along. He saw his cousin a short distance ahead of him, a tall Negro attendant upon her. He continued slowly that she might leave him behind, but the girl lagged on her way.

"Perhaps she wishes to speak with me," Henry thought, and quickened his steps to come up with her. He saw, incredulously, what the darkened room had hidden. She was only a little girl, not more than fourteen at the most. Elizabeth looked up as he came beside her.

"Do you find interesting things to be doing here in the Indies?" Henry asked.

"As many as one might expect," she replied. "We have been here a good while, you know." And touching her slave's arm with her little parasol, she turned into a crossing street, and left young Henry looking after her.

He was bitter against these proud relatives who seemed to edge away from him as though he were foul. He could not call them silly, for they had impressed him too deeply. They had succeeded in making him feel alone and helpless and very young.

===Chinese===
竖琴声不再响了。在男孩这番爆发的开头，爱德华爵士曾抬手像是要制止他，可那些话还是一股脑地涌了出来。竖琴停下之后，爱德华爵士不安地朝门口望了望。此刻他似乎把心思重新拉回到亨利身上。

「我没有钱拿来冒险，投到没把握的买卖里，」他厉声说，「我也没有工夫再谈了。总督马上要来跟我商议公事。不过我得说，你是个莽撞、胡来的小子，你那些行当迟早要把自己送上绞架。你父亲跟你一样，只不过他胡来的是脑子。

「而且我必须告诉你，西班牙和英格兰之间目前是和平的；虽说关系不怎么样，可毕竟是和平。你要是出去劫掠，我有责任看着你受罚，尽管我会为此难过。圆颅党人已经不在台上，克伦威尔睁一只眼闭一只眼的那些胡作非为，如今都被人盯得紧紧的。记住我的话，我可不想绞死我的外甥。现在我当真得跟你道别了。」

愤懑的泪水涌上了亨利的眼睛。

「谢谢你来看我，」他叔叔说，「再见。」说完他穿过帷幔门走了。

到了街上，亨利闷闷不乐地走着。他看见表妹就在前面不远处，一个高个子黑人随从跟着她。他故意走得很慢，好让她把自己落下，可那姑娘也磨磨蹭蹭地不走。

「说不定她想跟我说话，」亨利心想，便加快脚步追上她。他难以置信地看清了那间昏暗屋子所遮掩的真相。她不过是个小姑娘，顶多不超过十四岁。伊丽莎白在他走到身边时抬起头来。

「你觉得在印度群岛这儿有什么有趣的事可做吗？」亨利问。

「多多少少总有几样吧，」她答道，「你也知道，我们到这儿好些时候了。」她用她那柄小阳伞碰了碰她奴隶的胳膊，拐进一条横街，丢下年轻的亨利望着她的背影。

他心里恨恨的，恨这些高傲的亲戚，他们似乎都像躲什么脏东西似的躲着他。他又不能说他们蠢，因为他们给他的印象太深了。他们成功地让他觉得自己孤单、无助，而且嫩得很。

===Original===
---

The narrow ways of Port Royal were deep with muddy filth, ground to thick liquid by the carts and the numberless bare feet. Port Royal bore the same resemblance to a city as the Palace of the Lieutenant-Governor did to Whitehall. The streets were only narrow alleys lined with dirty wooden houses. And each house had a balcony above the street where people sat and stared at Henry as he passed; stared not with interest, but wearily, as men in sickness watch flies crawling on the ceiling.

One street seemed to have no inhabitants save only women﻿—black women, and white, and gray women, with fever written on their hollow cheeks. They leaned from their balconies like unclean sirens and softly called as he went by. Then, when he paid no attention to them, they shrieked like angry parrots and screamed curses and spat after him.

Near the waterfront he came to a kind of tavern with a great crowd gathered in front of it. Standing in the center of the way was a cask of wine with its head staved in, and a big, drunken man in crazy laces and a plumed hat strutted beside it. He passed out cups and basins and even hats full of wine to the reaching men. Now and then he called for a toast and a cheer, and his crowd screamed its acclaim.

Young Henry sought to pass them in his misery.

"Come drink my health, young man."

"I do not wish to drink," said Henry.

"You do not wish to drink?" The big man was overwhelmed with this new situation. Then he recovered his wrath.

"By God! you will so drink when Captain Dawes that took the supply ship *Sangre de Cristo* this day week asks you." The lowering man came close, then suddenly drew a great pistol from his belt and pointed it waveringly at Henry's breast.

===Chinese===
---

皇家港狭窄的街道积着厚厚的烂泥污垢，被大车和无数赤脚碾成了稠糊。皇家港跟一座城市的相似程度，就和副总督府邸跟白厅的相似程度差不多。街道不过是些窄巷，两旁排列着肮脏的木屋。每栋房子临街都有个阳台，人们坐在那儿，盯着路过的亨利；那盯视里没有兴趣，只有疲倦，就像病中的人看着天花板上爬行的苍蝇。

有一条街似乎没有别的居民，只有女人——黑人、白人，还有灰皮肤的、两颊凹陷写满疟疾的女人。她们像不洁的海妖一样从阳台上探出身子，在他经过时轻声叫唤。等到他不理睬，她们便像发怒的鹦鹉一样尖叫，破口大骂，朝他身后啐唾沫。

快到水边时，他来到一家酒馆模样的地方，门前聚了一大群人。路中央立着一桶敲开了盖的酒，一个身材魁梧的醉汉，穿着胡乱缀着花边的衣裳、戴着羽饰帽，在桶旁神气活现。他把盛满酒的杯子、盆子、甚至帽子递给伸过来的手。他不时招呼人干杯欢呼，他那群人就尖声叫好。

年轻的亨利满心凄苦，想从他们中间穿过去。

「来，为我的健康干一杯，年轻人。」

「我不想喝。」亨利说。

「你不想喝？」那大汉被这新情况弄得不知所措。随即他又恢复了怒气。

「老天为证！道斯船长（Captain Dawes），就是一星期前的今天截获运补给船『圣血号』（Sangre de Cristo）的那位，请你喝酒，你就得喝。」那阴沉着脸的人逼上前来，接着猛地从腰间抽出一把大手枪，晃晃悠悠地指着亨利的胸口。

===Original===
The boy eyed the pistol.

"I will drink your health," he said. And while he drank, an idea came to him. "Let me speak to you alone, Captain Dawes, sir," and he tugged the pirate into the tavern door. "About your next trip﻿—" he began.

"My next trip and hell!" the captain roared. "I've just taken a good prize, haven't I? I've got money, haven't I? Then what is this you are squalling about a next trip? Wait till the prize is spent and the wounds healed. Wait till I've drained Port Royal dry of wine, and then come talking about the next trip." He rushed back into the street crowd. "Boys!" he yelled. "Boys, you have not drunk my health for hours. Come, shout together now, and then we will sing!"

Henry walked onward in despair. In the harbor a number of ships were lying at anchor. He approached a sailor sitting in the sand.

"That one's fast," he said, to open the acquaintance.

"Aye, good enough."

"Are there any buccaneers of note in this town?" Henry asked.

"None but that Dawes, and he's only a roaring mouse. He takes a little boat loaded with supplies for Campeche, and you'd think it was Panama he brought home for the noise he makes about it."

"But are there none others?"

"Well, there's one they call Grippo but he takes no prizes unless they go unarmed. Afraid of his shadow, Grippo. Yes, he's in port with no prize, and drinking black rum on tick, I guess."

"Which is his ship?" Henry asked.

"Why, there she is. They call her *Ganymede*. They tell that Grippo stole her in Saint Malo when her crew was drunk. He and nine others tumbled the poor stiff wretches overside and made off with the ship for the Indies. Yes, she's a good craft, but Grippo is no master. A wonder it is that he's not wrecked her before now. Take Mansveldt; there's a master for you﻿—a real master. But Mansveldt is in Tortuga."

"A good, swift sailor," Henry observed; "though she could carry more canvas without hurt. How about her guns?"

"They say she's over armed if anything."

===Chinese===
那男孩瞅着那手枪。

「我为您的健康干杯。」他说。他喝酒的当口，一个念头冒了出来。「让我单独跟您说几句话，道斯船长，先生，」他把那海盗拽到酒馆门口。「关于您下一趟出海——」他开了个头。

「下一趟出海见鬼去吧！」船长吼道，「我刚弄到手一桩好买卖，不是吗？我有钱了，不是吗？那你瞎嚷嚷下一趟出海是干什么？等这笔钱花光了、伤养好了再说。等我把皇家港的酒喝得一滴不剩，你再来谈下一趟出海。」他冲回街上的人群里。「弟兄们！」他嚷，「弟兄们，你们好几个钟头没为我的健康干杯了。来，一块儿喊一嗓子，然后咱们唱歌！」

亨利绝望地继续往前走。港湾里停着几艘船。他走到一个坐在沙地里的水手跟前。

「那条船挺快。」他说，借此搭上话。

「嗯，还过得去。」

「这镇上有什么了不得的海盗吗？」亨利问。

「除了那个道斯，没别人，可他不过是只嗷嗷叫的老鼠。他截了一条装着补给开往坎佩切（Campeche）的小船，就那点动静，你还以为他抢回来的是巴拿马（Panama）呢。」

「可再没别人了吗？」

「嗯，有个他们叫格里波（Grippo）的，可他只劫那些不设防的船。格里波怕自己的影子。对，他就在港里，没抢到东西，我猜是赊着账喝黑朗姆呢。」

「他的船是哪条？」亨利问。

「嘿，就是那条。他们管它叫『加尼米德号』（Ganymede）。听人说格里波是在圣马洛偷来的，趁那条船的船员喝醉了。他和另外九个人把那些醉得直挺挺的可怜虫掀到海里，开着船逃到印度群岛来了。是，它是条好船，可格里波不是当船长的料。奇就奇在他到现在还没把它撞沉。瞧瞧曼斯维尔德（Mansveldt）；那才叫船长——真正的船长。可曼斯维尔德在托尔图加。」

「是条又好又快的船，」亨利评论道，「虽说它还能再多吃点帆也无妨。它的炮怎么样？」

「要我说，它的炮反倒多了点。」

===Original===
And on that night, Henry found the buccaneer drinking in a hovel on the beach. The man was nearly black; two fat wrinkles cut each cheek as though a silken cord were pulled tight against the flesh until it disappeared. His eyes darted here and there like sentries before a camp of little fears.

"Are you one they call Grippo?" Henry asked.

"I took no prize," the man cried, starting back. "I take no prizes. You have nothing to fasten on me for." Once in Saint Malo he had been accosted thus, and afterwards they had whipped him on the cross until a hundred sagging mouths opened on his body and every one laughed blood. Grippo had feared all semblance of authority since then.

"Who are you?" he asked.

"I think I am going to make your fortune, Grippo," Henry said with assurance. He knew how to handle this man, for he was a counterpart of the many slaves of the plantation﻿—fearful, and perhaps greedy. "What would you do with five hundred English pounds, Grippo?"

The black man licked his lips and glanced at the empty cup before him. "What must I do for this money?" he whispered.

"You will sell me the captaincy of the *Ganymede*."

Now Grippo was wary.

"The *Ganymede* is worth much more," he said firmly. "But I do not want to buy the ship﻿—only the captaincy. Look, Grippo! I'll make this compact with you. I will give you five hundred pounds for a half interest in the *Ganymede*, and all of her command. Then we will put to sea. I think I know how to win plunder if there be no interference in my company. Grippo, I will give you a writing to this effect. If I fail in one single undertaking in the *Ganymede*, then you shall have the whole ship back, and you shall keep the five hundred pounds."

Grippo still looked into his empty cup, but suddenly he was filled with excitement.

"Give me money," he cried out. "Quick! give me the money." Then﻿—"Oloto! Oloto! bring white wine﻿—white wine﻿—for the love of Christ."

===Chinese===
那天夜里，亨利在海滩上一间窝棚里找到了那个正喝着酒的海盗。那人黑得几乎不像话；两道胖嘟嘟的褶子切过他每边脸颊，仿佛有一根丝绳紧绷着贴在肉上，绷到看不见为止。他的眼睛东瞟西瞟，像一群驻扎在恐惧小营帐前的哨兵。

「你就是人家叫的格里波吗？」亨利问。

「我没抢过东西，」那人往后一缩，嚷道，「我不抢东西。你没什么能抓着我的把柄。」当年在圣马洛（Saint Malo），他就被人这样搭过话，随后他们把他绑在十字架上鞭打，打得他身上豁开了一百张耷拉着的嘴，每一张都笑出血来。从那以后，格里波一见任何像权威的东西就怕。

「你是谁？」他问。

「我想，我是要来让你发财的，格里波。」亨利胸有成竹地说。他知道怎么拿捏这个人，因为他跟种植园上那许多奴隶是一个路数——又怕，又或许贪。「格里波，要是你有五百英镑，你要拿来干什么？」

那黑人舔了舔嘴唇，瞥了一眼面前那只空杯子。「我得为这笔钱做什么？」他压低嗓子问。

「你把『加尼米德号』的船长之位卖给我。」

这会儿格里波警觉起来。

「『加尼米德号』值钱得多，」他坚决地说。「可我并不想买这条船——只想买船长之位。听着，格里波！我跟你这么定：我给你五百镑，换『加尼米德号』一半的权益，外加全部的指挥权。然后我们就出海。我想，只要船上没人和我捣乱，我就知道怎么去赢取战利品。格里波，我会给你立一张字据，写明这个意思。要是我指挥『加尼米德号』，哪一次行动失了手，那整条船就还给你，那五百镑也归你。」

格里波还是盯着他的空杯子，可突然间他激动起来。

「给我钱，」他喊，「快！把钱给我。」然后——「奥洛托！奥洛托！拿白葡萄酒来——白葡萄酒——看在基督的份上。」

## III / III

===Original===
There were many glittering reputations along the coast of Darien and among the green Caribbean islands when Henry Morgan came to be a buccaneer. In the wine shops of Tortuga were tales of a thousand fortunes made and spent, of fine ships taken and sunk, of gold and plate dumped on the docks like wood.

===Chinese===
当亨利·摩根（Henry Morgan）前来投身为海盗时，达连（Darien）沿岸与翠绿的加勒比诸岛之间，已流传着无数声名赫赫的传奇。在托尔图加（Tortuga）的酒肆里，人们讲述着一千笔暴富又挥霍一空的财富，讲述着一艘艘被夺走又被凿沉的好船，讲述着如柴薪般堆在码头上的金银与器皿。

===Original===
The Free Brotherhood had grown to be a terrible thing since Pierre le Grand and a little band of hunters slipped out of the woods of Hispaniola and captured the Vice-Admiral of the plate fleet from a canoe. France and Britain and Holland had seen in these islands a good hermitage for their criminals, and for years they had unloaded worthless human freight on the Indies. There was a time in those old nations when anyone who could not give a good, virtuous account of himself was crammed into a ship and sent off to be a bond-servant to any man who would pay a small sum for him. And when their time was up, these people stole guns and warred on Spain. It was not strange, for Spain was Catholic and rich, while Huguenots and Lutherans and Church of England men were poor and out at heel. They fought a holy war. Spain had locked up the treasures of the world. If poor, ruined beggars could be reaching a coin through the keyhole, who was the worse for it? Who minded except Spain? Surely England and France and Holland took little heed of it. Sometimes they provided the pirates with commissions against Aragon and Castile, so that you might come on a man who, ten years before, had been sent out in a prison ship, carrying the style of “Captain by the grace of the King.”

===Chinese===
自从大个子皮埃尔（Pierre le Grand）带着一小队猎人潜出伊斯帕尼奥拉（Hispaniola）的密林、从一只独木舟上俘获了银船队的副司令以来，自由兄弟会已壮大成一股骇人的势力。法兰西、不列颠与荷兰都把这些岛屿视作流放本国罪犯的现成隐修之地，多年来一直把一文不值的人货卸到印度群岛。在那些古老的国家里，曾有过这样一段时日：凡是说不出自己清白德行来历的人，都被塞进船舱，以一笔小钱卖给任何人充当契约仆役。待到契约期满，这些人便偷来火枪，与西班牙开战。这并不奇怪，因为西班牙人既信天主教又富甲一方，而胡格诺派、路德派与英国国教徒却贫穷落魄、鞋破跟穿。他们打的是一场圣战。西班牙把世间的财宝尽数锁了起来，若是穷苦潦倒的乞丐能从锁孔里摸出一枚钱币，又有谁因此受损？除了西班牙，谁会在意？英格兰、法兰西与荷兰对此当然不闻不问。他们有时还发给海盗私掠特许状（commission），命其劫掠阿拉贡与卡斯蒂利亚。于是你或许会撞见这样一个人——十年前他被押上囚船流放至此，如今却顶着「蒙国王恩典的船长」的头衔。

===Original===
France had the good of her wayward children at heart, for she sent out twelve hundred women to Tortuga to be the wives of buccaneers. The whole twelve hundred turned to a business more profitable than wifehood immediately they landed, but France could not help that.

===Chinese===
法兰西倒是挂念着她那些任性子民的福祉，特意送了一千二百名女子到托尔图加，给海盗们做妻子。这一千二百人一登岸便纷纷转入了比为人妻更营利的营生，不过法兰西对此也无能为力。

===Original===
They had got their name, these buccaneers, of a time when they were nothing more than cattle-hunters. There was a way of smoking meat by burning small bits of fat and flesh in the fire. This made the meat more savory than usual. It was called the “boucan” process, and from it the pirates were named.

===Chinese===
这些海盗（buccaneer）的名号，得自他们当年不过是些猎牛之人的时代。那时有种熏肉的法子：把一小块一小块的油脂与碎肉投进火里焚烧来烟熏。这样熏出的肉比寻常的更可口。这法子叫作「布干」（boucan）熏制法，海盗们的名号便由此而来。

===Original===
But after a time these hunters came out of the woods in little, careful groups; then bands formed, and then whole fleets of eight or ten vessels. And finally thousands assembled in Tortuga, and from that spot of safety buzzed about the flanks of Spain.

===Chinese===
但过了一阵，这些猎人开始三五成群、小心翼翼地走出密林；渐渐地结成帮伙，再后来竟凑成了八九艘船的整支舰队。到最后，数以千计的人聚拢到托尔图加，从这个安全的巢穴嗡嗡地四处侵扰西班牙的侧翼。

===Original===
And Spain could not combat them. Did she hang ten, a hundred joined their ranks; so she fortified her towns and sent her treasures on the sea under the protection of warships filled with soldiers. The numberless vessels of the Spanish colonies were nearly all driven from the sea by the fierce buccaneers. Only once a year did the plate fleet sail out for home.

===Chinese===
西班牙却无力与之抗衡。她绞死十个，便有一百人投到海盗麾下；于是她加固城防，让满载士兵的战船护航，把财宝由海上运送。西属殖民地数不清的船只，几乎被凶悍的海盗悉数逐出了大海。银船队一年只出海一次，驶向本土。

===Original===
There were fine names among the Brotherhood, and exploits to make Henry Morgan squirm with jealousy if he had not been so confident of overshadowing them all one day.

===Chinese===
兄弟会里名号响亮的人物不计其数，其事迹足以令亨利·摩根妒忌得辗转难安——倘若他不是那样笃信自己有朝一日定会盖过他们所有人的话。

===Original===
Bartolomeo Portugues there was, who took a great prize. But before he could get away with it, he was captured near Campeche. The gibbet was erected on the shore for his hanging. He watched them put it up from his prison aboard ship. And in the night before his execution, he stabbed his guard and swam away, supported by a keg. Before eight days had passed, he came again with pirates in a long canoe and stole the same ship away from the harbor of Campeche. He lost her, of course, in a storm off Cuba, but the story was, nevertheless, told with glee in the taverns.

===Chinese===
其中有一位巴尔托洛梅奥·葡萄牙人（Bartolomeo Portugues），劫得了一份大彩头。可还没等他把战利品运走，就在坎佩切（Campeche）附近被擒。岸上已为他竖起了绞刑架，只待行刑。他从船上的囚室里眼看着他们把架子搭起来。然而行刑前的那一夜，他刺死了看守，抱住一只小桶泅水逃走。不出八天，他又带着海盗乘一条长独木舟杀了回来，把同一艘船从坎佩切的港口里偷走了。当然，那船后来在古巴（Cuba）外海的一场风暴中沉没了，但这个故事，依旧在酒馆里被人讲得眉飞色舞。

===Original===
Roche Braziliano was a Hollander with a chubby face. When he was young he was driven from Brazil by the Portuguese, and from their colony he had taken his name. Curiously, he held no rancor against Portugal. His hatred turned on Spain. He was a kindly, gentle, beloved captain, so only there were no Spaniards about. His men worshiped him, and drank no other toast but to his name. Once, when his ship was wrecked in Castilla de Oro, he killed most of a troop of Spanish horse and used their beasts to ride off on. When the men of Spain were near him, Roche was a foaming beast. It was told that once he roasted prisoners on green spits over a slow fire.

===Chinese===
还有罗歇·巴西利亚诺（Roche Braziliano），是个胖脸的荷兰人。他年轻时被葡萄牙人赶出巴西，他的名号便取自这块殖民地。说来奇怪，他对葡萄牙并无半点怨恨，他的仇恨全都转向了西班牙。他是个和善、温柔、深受爱戴的船长——只要身边没有西班牙人。他的部下敬他如神，举杯只祝他一人的健康。有一次，他的船在黄金卡斯蒂利亚（Castilla de Oro）失事，他杀光了西班牙骑兵小队的大半，骑着他们的坐骑扬长而去。可一旦西班牙人近在眼前，罗歇便成了一头口吐白沫的野兽。据说他有一次曾把俘虏绑在青树枝削成的烤签上，架在文火上慢慢炙烤。

===Original===
As the rich shipping was driven from the sea, the buccaneers must be taking villages, and then even towns with forts. Lewis Scot pillaged Campeche and left it a black, smoking pile.

===Chinese===
既然海上的富商船队都被逐尽，海盗们便不得不去攻取村落，进而连有堡垒设防的城镇也一并拿下。刘易斯·斯科特（Lewis Scot）劫掠了坎佩切，把它丢作一堆冒烟的焦黑废墟。

===Original===
L’Ollonais had come from the Sables d’Ollone, and very rapidly he became the most feared man in the western ocean. He began with a real hatred for Spain and ended with a strong love of cruelty. He had pulled out tongues, and carved his prisoners to pieces with his sword. The Spanish men would far rather have met the devil in any form than L’Ollonais. The whisper of his name emptied the villages in his path of every living unit. It was said that the mice fled to the jungle when he came. Maracaibo he took, and New Gibraltar, and St. James de Leon. Everywhere he slaughtered men for the fierce love of it.

===Chinese===
洛洛诺瓦（L’Ollonais）来自莱萨布勒-多洛讷（Sables d’Ollone），他极快地就成了西大洋上最令人闻风丧胆的人物。他起初是真心痛恨西班牙，到后来却爱上了残虐本身。他曾拔出人的舌头，曾用剑把俘虏一刀刀剐成碎块。西班牙人情愿遇见任何模样的魔鬼，也不愿遇见洛洛诺瓦。他的名号一传开，沿途村落里一切活物都逃之一空——据说连老鼠听见他来了都躲进丛林。他攻下了马拉开波（Maracaibo），攻下了新直布罗陀（New Gibraltar），攻下了莱昂的圣雅各（St. James de Leon）。每到一处，他都为着那嗜杀的狂热而屠戮人命。

===Original===
Once, when the blood lust was in him, he had commanded that eighty-seven prisoners be bound and laid in a row on the ground. Then he walked down the line, carrying a whetstone in one hand and a long sword in the other. That day he cut off eighty-seven heads with his own hand.

===Chinese===
有一次，杀性大发的他下令把八十七名俘虏捆缚起来，一排排摁倒在地上。然后他一手提着磨刀石、一手提着长剑，顺着队伍一路走下去。那一天，他亲手砍下了八十七颗人头。

===Original===
But L’Ollonais was not content with murdering the Spanish men. He went into the gentle country of Yucatan, where the people lived in ruined stone cities, and where the virgins went crowned with flowers. They were a quiet people in Yucatan, and their race was dying in an inexplicable decay. When L’Ollonais went away, the cities were heaps of stones and ashes, and there were no crowns any more.

===Chinese===
但洛洛诺瓦并不以杀尽西班牙男人为满足。他闯进了尤卡坦（Yucatan）那片温柔的土地——那里的百姓住在倾颓的石城里，少女们头戴花冠出行。尤卡坦是个安宁静谧的部族，他们的种族正以一种无从解释的衰败走向消亡。等洛洛诺瓦离去时，那些城池只剩下一堆堆乱石与灰烬，再也没有花冠了。

===Original===
The Indians of Darien were different; fierce, and fearless, and unrelenting. The Spaniards called them “bravos” and swore that they were untamable. They had been friends of the pirates because they so hated Spain, but L’Ollonais robbed them and murdered the tribesmen. These Indians waited many years for their vengeance, and at last they caught L’Ollonais when his ship had wrecked on the shores of their country. They built a fire and danced for hours, and then they burned the Frenchman’s body bit by bit before his eyes, a finger and a pinch of flesh at a time.

===Chinese===
达连的印第安人却不同：凶猛、无畏、毫不留情。西班牙人唤他们作「布拉沃斯」（bravos，意为「猛汉」），发誓说他们永不可驯服。他们原本因为同样痛恨西班牙而与海盗为友，可洛洛诺瓦却抢劫他们、屠杀他们的族人。这些印第安人苦等多年图谋复仇，终于在洛洛诺瓦的船于他们的海岸失事后逮住了他。他们生起一堆火，跳了几个钟头的舞，然后当着这个法国人的面，把他的身体一寸一寸地烧掉——一次烧一根手指、一小撮肉。

===Original===
A lean French gentleman came into a tavern at Tortuga one night, and when they asked his name, seized a large rum keg and hurled it from him.

===Chinese===
一天夜里，一个精瘦的法国绅士走进托尔图加的一家酒馆。众人问他的姓名，他一把抓起一只大朗姆酒桶，把它狠狠掷了出去。

===Original===
“Bras de Fer,” he said, and no one ever questioned him farther. It was never known whether his name was hidden for a shame or a sorrow or a hatred, but all the Coast came to know of him as a great, brave captain.

===Chinese===
「铁臂（Bras de Fer），」他说，自此再没人追问下去。他那名字究竟是为遮一段耻辱、一桩哀痛，还是一份仇恨，始终无人知晓，但整条海岸渐渐都知道了他是一位伟大而英勇的船长。

===Original===
These were men who made phrases to be repeated.

===Chinese===
这些人，都是能说出被人反复传诵之隽语的人物。

===Original===
“No prey, no pay,” the Exterminator had bawled, and now everyone was saying it. When Captain Lawrence, in a small boat, was assailed by two Spanish frigates, he said to his men, “You have too much experience not to be sensible of your danger, and too much courage to fear it.” This was a fine saying, and on the strength of it his followers captured the two Spanish ships and took them home to Goaves.

===Chinese===
「无猎物，无报酬，」灭绝者号船长（the Exterminator）曾这样吼过，如今人人都挂在嘴边。劳伦斯船长（Captain Lawrence）乘一条小船遭到两艘西班牙护卫舰围攻时，对部下说：「你们的经验太丰富，不会感觉不到其中的危险；可你们的勇气也太足，绝不会为此畏惧。」这是一句妙语，凭这句话撑腰，他的部下竟俘获了那两艘西班牙船，把它们带回戈阿夫（Goaves）。

===Original===
Not all were cruel or even violent men. Some had a curious streak of piety. There was Captain Watling who made it a point to hold divine service every Sabbath, with the whole crew standing uncovered. Daniel once shot a sailor for an irreverence. These buccaneers prayed loudly before battle, and, if they were successful, half of them trooped off to a captured cathedral to sing the Te Deum, while the other half plundered the prize.

===Chinese===
并非人人都是残忍乃至暴戾之徒。其中有些人怀着一缕奇异的虔诚。沃特林船长（Captain Watling）每逢安息日必举行礼拜，全船水手一律脱帽肃立。丹尼尔（Daniel）曾因一名水手出言不敬而一枪将其击毙。这些海盗开战前总要高声祷告，一旦得胜，便有一半人蜂拥到夺来的教堂里去唱《感恩赞》（Te Deum），另一半人则趁机洗劫战利品。

===Original===
Captains of ships maintained the strictest discipline among their men, swiftly punishing insubordination or any other wrongdoing which might interfere with their success. There were no such riots at sea as were later tolerated by Kidd and Blackbeard and Lafitte.

===Chinese===
船长们在部下中维持着最严明的纪律，对违令或任何可能妨碍其功业的过错都雷厉风行地加以惩处。海上绝没有后来基德（Kidd）、黑胡子（Blackbeard）和拉菲特（Lafitte）所纵容的那种哗变作乱。

===Original===
But out of the whole history of the Brotherhood, one man towered. There was a Dutchman named Edward Mansveldt. In bravery and in soldiery he was preeminent, for he had taken Granada and St. Augustine in Florida, and St. Catherine’s Isle. With a great fleet of ships he had gone cruising along the coasts of Darien and Castilla de Oro, taking what he might lay his hands on. But there was a power of dream in him. Out of his mob of ragamuffin heroes he wanted to make a strong, durable nation, a new, aggressive nation in America. As more and more of the buccaneers flocked to his command, his dream solidified. He consulted the governments of England and France. They were shocked, and forbade him to consider such a thing. A race of pirates not amenable to the gibbets of the crowns? Why, they would be plundering everybody. He must not be thinking about it at all.

===Chinese===
然而，在兄弟会的整部历史里，有一人巍然高出众人。那是一个名叫爱德华·曼斯维尔德（Edward Mansveldt）的荷兰人。论勇毅、论将才，他都首屈一指——他曾攻下格拉纳达（Granada）、佛罗里达（Florida）的圣奥古斯丁（St. Augustine）以及圣凯瑟琳岛（St. Catherine’s Isle）。他率一支庞大舰队巡弋达连与黄金卡斯蒂利亚沿岸，凡伸手可及之物尽数夺取。但他骨子里有一股梦想的力量。他想把自己那群衣衫褴褛的英雄草莽，锻造成一个强韧持久的国度，一个美洲新生的、锐意进取的国度。随着越来越多的海盗涌到他的麾下，他的梦想日益成形。他与英法两国政府商议。两国都大为惊骇，严禁他再作此想。一个不受各国王绞刑架管辖的海盗种族？哎呀，那他们岂不是要劫掠所有人。他万万不可再动此念头。

===Original===
But still he went on planning and planning his new government. It would be started at St. Catherine’s Isle. He settled a body of his men there, and then went casting about for more to join his new nation. His ship was wrecked near the city of Havana, and Spaniards strangled Edward Mansveldt on the garotte.

===Chinese===
可他仍旧一意孤行，筹划着他那套新政府。它将以圣凯瑟琳岛为起点。他在岛上安置了一批亲信，随即四处奔走，搜罗更多的人来加入他的新国度。后来他的船在哈瓦那（Havana）城附近失事，西班牙人用绞刑铁环把爱德华·曼斯维尔德活活勒死了。

===Original===
These were the men Henry Morgan had set about to lead. And, confidently, he saw no barrier, so only a man planned carefully and considered his chances. These stories and these men were well enough, but they fell short in the large actions. They were improvident and vain. They might help him one day.

===Chinese===
这些，便是亨利·摩根立意要去统率的人物。他信心十足地看不出有任何障碍——只要一个人谋划周密、审时度势。这些故事、这些人物固然都不错，但他们在大的举措上终究略逊一筹。他们既无远虑，又好虚荣。他们有朝一日或许能为他所用。

===Original===
Mansveldt was living and Bras de Fer was an old man when Henry Morgan went sailing with black Grippo in the *Ganymede*.

===Chinese===
亨利·摩根随同黝黑的格里波（Grippo）驾着「加尼米德号」（Ganymede）出海时，曼斯维尔德尚在人世，铁臂已是个老人了。

### I / I

===Original===
There was excitement and curiosity in Port Royal when Morgan was fitting out the *Ganymede* for sea. Strange stores and unusual weapons were going into her hold. Drawn on by the quiet confidence of this young man, many sailors volunteered for his crew. The captain found five gunners of reputation in the port and signed them to go with him. When the *Ganymede* dropped her sails and slipped from the harbor, a crowd of idlers stood on the beach and watched her go.

===Chinese===
摩根在皇家港（Port Royal）为「加尼米德号」装配出海之际，港口里一片兴奋与好奇。一批批奇异的给养与不同寻常的兵器正被运进底舱。被这个年轻人沉静的自信所吸引，许多水手自愿报名加入他的船员。船长又在港中物色到五位颇有名望的炮手，签下他们随行。当「加尼米德号」落帆驶离港口时，一群闲人站在海滩上目送她远去。

===Original===
They cruised to the coast of Darien looking for prey, but the sea appeared to be swept clean of Spanish craft. One morning, near the port of Cartagena, they sighted the tall red hull of a trading ship. Captain Morgan hid his men. No soul was allowed to show himself. Even the steersman worked in a tiny house, while a dummy wheel swung idly on the deck. Then down on the Spanish boat they bore, and the Spanish crew was overwhelmed. Here came a boat and no man working her. It smacked of witchcraft, or of one of those nameless tragedies of the sea the sailors talked about. Perhaps a plague had killed all the crew, and they could take and sell this ship. But when they were close, three masked guns spouted flame; they fired only at one spot, and when they had done, the rudder of the Spanish ship hung in splinters and she floundered about in no control. Then Captain Morgan, hanging aft, out of range of her broadside, poured shot into her hull until the flag fluttered down. It was the first prize of his planning.

===Chinese===
他们巡弋到达连沿岸搜寻猎物，可海上西班牙船只似已被一扫而空。一天清晨，在卡塔赫纳（Cartagena）港附近，他们瞭望到一艘商船高耸的红色船身。摩根船长把船员尽数藏起，不许一个人露面。连舵手也躲在一间小舱房里操舵，甲板上只留一只假舵轮空转着。他们直扑那艘西班牙船，西班牙船员猝不及防。一只船驶来，却无一人操纵。这景象透着巫术的气味，又像水手们口中那些无名海难的惨剧。也许是一场瘟疫杀光了全船的人，他们正好把这条船夺来发卖。可当他们逼近时，三门作了遮掩的大炮喷出火舌；炮火只集中轰击一处，轰罢，那艘西班牙船的舵已炸成碎片，只能在海上失控地乱打转。随后摩根船长吊在船尾、处在敌船侧舷炮火射程之外，朝其船身倾泻弹丸，直到那面旗帜飘然落下。这是他谋划所得的头一份战利品。

===Original===
A few days later he brought up with another ship and ran alongside to board. The Spanish crew was massed against the bulwarks to repel the attack. And immediately the air was filled with clay powder pots which landed in the thick group and exploded. The Spanish men ran screaming to the shelter of the hold to escape this flashing death.

===Chinese===
几天后，他又追上一艘船，靠上去强行接舷。西班牙船员集结在船舷护栏后拼死抵御。霎时间，空中布满了陶土火药罐，它们落入密集的人群中轰然炸开。西班牙人尖叫着奔进货舱躲避这场闪光纷飞的死亡。

===Original===
When Henry Morgan came at last to Tortuga, four prizes followed in his wake, and he had not lost a man. It was as easy as he had known it would be. Here were four monuments to his planning. One had only to do the unexpected thing quickly. This was the secret of successful war.

===Chinese===
亨利·摩根最终抵达托尔图加时，四艘战利船尾随而来，他未折一兵一卒。一切正如他所料那般容易。这四艘船便是他谋略的四座丰碑。只要出其不意、迅雷不及掩耳——这便是制胜战争的不二法门。

===Original===
Mansveldt was in Tortuga when Henry Morgan came, and his little eyes glittered as he looked at this plunder. Soon he sent for this new leader.

===Chinese===
亨利·摩根抵达时，曼斯维尔德正巧也在托尔图加。他一双小眼睛望着这批战利品，闪闪发亮。不久他便派人召见这位新秀首领。

===Original===
“You are Captain Morgan who took the four prizes in the harbor?”

“Yes, sir, I am.”

“And how did you do this thing? The Spanish ships are strongly armed and wary.”

“I did it, sir, with my planning. Many nights have I considered how to do these things. I work with surprise, sir, when other men use only force.”

===Chinese===
「你就是那个在港里连夺四艘战利船的摩根船长？」

「是的，先生，正是在下。」

「你是怎么做到这件事的？西班牙船只武装精良，警惕性也高。」

「靠的是谋划，先生。不知有多少个夜晚我在思量该怎样做成这些事。别人只用蛮力，先生，而我用的是出其不意。」

===Original===
Mansveldt regarded him with admiration.

“I am fitting out an expedition to take St. Catherine’s Isle,” he said. “Then I am going to form a republic of buccaneers who will fight with a patriotism. Would you like to be Vice-Admiral of this expedition? I have some reputation for picking men.”

===Chinese===
曼斯维尔德满含钦佩地打量着他。

「我正在装备一支远征军，去夺取圣凯瑟琳岛，」他说，「随后我要建立一个海盗共和国，让他们怀着爱国之心去作战。你愿不愿意做这支远征军的副司令？我在识人方面还算小有名气。」

===Original===
The name of Mansveldt was mighty on the seas, and Henry flushed with pleasure.

“I would like it, sir,” he said quickly.

===Chinese===
曼斯维尔德的威名在海上一时无两，亨利高兴得涨红了脸。

「我愿意，先生，」他脱口而出。

===Original===
The fleet sailed out, and Captain Morgan was Vice-Admiral. There was a fine assault; the ships threw in their ragged hordes and slaughter walked on the walls. The island could not withstand the fierceness of the attack, and finally the fortress fell. Then the Dutch admiral drew up his government and left Henry Morgan in command while he went out to scour the world for recruits. He and his ship were lost and never heard from again. It was said the Spaniards strangled him in Cuba.

===Chinese===
舰队出海了，摩根船长任副司令。那是一场漂亮的强攻；各船把衣衫褴褛的群氓一拨拨投上城头，杀戮在城墙上行走。海岛承受不住这般猛烈的攻势，堡垒终于陷落。随后这位荷兰司令拟就了他的政府章程，把指挥权交给亨利·摩根，自己则出海去四处招兵买马。他与他的船从此杳无音信，据说是西班牙人在古巴把他勒死了。

===Original===
Captain Morgan was now the paramount leader of the Spanish Main. Ships flew from the ways to join his fleets, to sail under his command and fight with him and share in his success. He went up against Puerto Bello and sacked the town. The houses were burned and all the helpless citizens were plundered. When Captain Morgan’s ships sailed off the jungle was already creeping into the ruins.

===Chinese===
如今摩根船长已是西属大陆（the Spanish Main）至高无上的首领。船只从各个船坞争相驶来加入他的舰队，要在他的麾下航行、与他并肩作战、共享其功业。他挥师进攻贝略港（Puerto Bello），将该城洗劫一空。房屋被付之一炬，所有无助的市民都被劫掠殆尽。当摩根船长的舰队扬帆离去时，丛林已悄然爬进了那片废墟。

===Original===
For ten years he sailed about the ocean, among the islands and along the green coasts of Tropic America, and his was the greatest name of all who had gone out for plunder. The pirates of the world came flocking to his reputation. People cheered him in Tortuga and Goaves. Numberless men volunteered for every expedition. Now all the Brotherhood waited for Captain Morgan to open a keg of liquor in the streets or to run wild through the town. He never did. Coldly he walked about, clad in a purple coat and gray silk stockings and gray shoes with bows. At his side hung a long rapier no thicker than a pencil, in a scabbard of gray silk.

===Chinese===
整整十年，他巡行于大洋之上，穿行于岛屿之间，沿着热带美洲翠绿的海岸航行；在所有出海劫掠的人中，他的名号最为煊赫。全天下的海盗都慕他的威名蜂拥而至。人们在托尔图加与戈阿夫为他欢呼。每一次远征都有数不清的人自愿投效。如今整个兄弟会都盼着摩根船长哪一天在街头当众开启一桶酒痛饮，或是发狂般地横扫全城。可他从不。他冷然地四处走动，身披一件紫色外衣，足蹬灰色丝袜与缀着蝴蝶结的灰鞋。腰间悬着一柄比铅笔粗不了多少的长细剑，剑鞘是灰色的丝绸。

===Original===
At first the sailors strove to establish a comradeship with him, but he pricked them away with frigid insults. The lessons of the slaves lived on in him. He did not try to buy his popularity, and all the Free Brotherhood showered it upon him—cast their lives and their fortunes on the knees of his success.

===Chinese===
起初水手们试图与他结成兄弟情谊，他却用冷冰冰的讥嘲把他们一一刺退。当奴隶时学到的教训在他身上长存。他从不刻意收买人心，可整个自由兄弟会却争相把人心捧给他——把性命与家产都掷在他功业的膝头。

### II / II

===Original===
Ten years of fighting and plundering and burning, and he was thirty. His graying hair seemed to coil more closely to his head. Henry Morgan was successful, the most luck-followed freebooter the world had known, and the men of his profession gave him that admiration he had craved. His enemies—and any man of Spain who had money was his enemy—shuddered at the mention of his name. They had placed him in their fears beside Drake and L’Ollonais.

===Chinese===
十年的厮杀、劫掠与焚烧，他已三十岁了。他那渐生的灰发似乎更紧地贴在头皮上。亨利·摩根功成名就，是世间有史以来最受好运眷顾的劫掠者，同行们也把那份他渴慕已久的钦佩献给了他。他的仇敌——凡是有钱的西班牙人皆与他为敌——一听见他的名字便不寒而栗。在他们的恐惧里，他们已把他与德雷克（Drake）和洛洛诺瓦并列。

===Original===
He had gone out with Grippo in the *Ganymede*, assured that when his guns roared into a Spanish hull, when he stood embattled on a Spanish deck with cries and clash of iron weapons about him, there would come that flaming happiness his heart desired. These things he had experienced, and there was not even content. The nameless craving in him grew and flexed its claws against his heart. He had thought the adulation of the Brotherhood might salve the wound of his desire; that when the pirates saw the results of his planning and marveled at them, he would be pleased and flattered. And this thing happened. The men fairly fawned on him, and he found that he despised them for it and considered them fools to be taken with such simple things.

===Chinese===
当初他与格里波驾着「加尼米德号」出海时，曾笃信当他的炮火轰进西班牙船身、当他立于西班牙甲板鏖战之中、四周尽是呐喊与铁器交锋之声时，他内心所渴求的那炽烈的幸福便会降临。这一切他都亲身经历了，可其中连一丝满足都没有。他体内那无名的饥渴愈发壮大，把利爪抵着他的心口伸缩挠抓。他曾以为兄弟会的奉承或许能抚平他欲望的伤口；曾以为当海盗们看见他谋划的成果而惊叹不已时，他会欣然自得、受人逢迎。而这件事果然发生了。众人几乎是在对他摇尾乞怜，他发现自己因此反倒鄙视起他们来，觉得他们全是些被区区琐事就轻易打动的蠢货。

===Original===
Henry had grown lonely in his glory. Old Merlin had spoken truth so long ago, for Captain Morgan had come to his success, and he was alone in his success, with no friend anywhere. The craving of his heart must lie crouched within him. All his fears and sorrows and conceits, his failures and little weaknesses, must be concealed. These, his followers, had gathered to the cry of his success; they would leave him at the first small sign of weakness.

===Chinese===
亨利在自己的荣光里愈发孤独了。老默林（Merlin）很久以前说过真话，因为摩根船长已攀至功业的顶峰，而他在自己的功业里孑然一身，四海之内再无一友。他心底的渴慕只能蜷伏在自身之中。他所有的恐惧与悲伤与自负，他的失败与种种小小弱点，都必须藏匿起来。这些追随者是闻着他功业之声聚拢来的；只消他露出一丝软弱的苗头，他们便会弃他而去。

===Original===
While he was engaged in winning plunder, a little rumor had come stealthily across the isthmus, had floated among the islands and stolen aboard the ships. Men caught the whispered name and listened carefully.

“There is a woman in Panama and she is lovely as the sun. They call her the Red Saint in Panama. All men kneel to her.” Thus said the whispering. The voice grew and grew until men in the taverns drank to La Santa Roja. Young seamen whispered of her in the dog watch. “There is a woman in the Cup of Gold and all men fall before her as heathen kneel before the sun.” They spoke softly of her in the streets of Goaves. No one had seen her; no one could tell the tint of her cheeks or the color of her hair. Yet, in a few years, every man in the wide, wild Main had drunk to the Red Saint, had dreamed of her; many had prayed to La Santa Roja. She became to every man the quest of his heart, bearing the image of some fair young girl left on a European beach to be gloriously colored by the years. And Panama was to every man the nest of his desire. It was a curious thing. In time, no speech among gathered men could end without mention of La Santa Roja. She was become a queer delirium in the minds of the rough pirates, a new virgin for their worship. Many said she was Mary come to live on earth again, and they added her name in their prayers.

===Chinese===
正当他忙于夺取战利品之际，一则小小的传闻已悄然越过地峡，飘过诸岛，又偷偷溜上了船。人们捕捉到那个低语的名字，便屏息细听。

「巴拿马（Panama）有一个女子，她美如太阳。巴拿马人唤她作红圣女。所有的男人都向她跪拜。」那低语如是说。这声音愈传愈广，直到酒馆里的人们都举杯祝红圣女健康。年轻的水手在二更班（dog watch）里悄声谈论她。「金杯（the Cup of Gold）里有一位女子，所有男人都拜倒在她脚下，如同异教徒朝太阳跪拜。」他们在戈阿夫的街头轻声谈论她。没有人见过她；没有人说得出她脸颊的颜色或她头发的色泽。然而不出几年，这片广袤蛮荒的大陆上每个男人都为红圣女举过杯，都梦见她；许多人还向红圣女（La Santa Roja）祈祷。对每个男人而言，她成了心头追寻的目标，承载着某个留在欧洲海滩上的美貌少女的影像，任凭岁月为她添上辉煌的色彩。而巴拿马，对每个男人而言便是欲望之巢。这是一桩奇事。时日一久，但凡众人聚谈，没有一次能不提红圣女便收场。她已成了粗豪海盗们心头一种奇异的谵妄，一个供他们膜拜的新圣女。许多人说她是下凡重临人间的圣母马利亚，还把她的名字加进了自己的祷词。

===Original===
Now, when Captain Morgan had taken Puerto Bello, the Governor of Panama was filled with admiration and wonder that such a ragged band of ill-ordered men, and without uniforms, could capture such a city. He sent a messenger asking for a small sample of the weapons which had made this thing possible. Captain Morgan took the runner to a small room that had escaped the general fire.

===Chinese===
且说摩根船长攻下贝略港之后，巴拿马的总督满心惊异与叹服：这样一群衣衫褴褛、毫无秩序、连制服都没有的人，竟能攻下这样一座城池。他派了一名信使，索要一小件能成此奇迹的兵器样品。摩根船长把这名信差领进一间幸免于大火的小屋。

===Original===
“Have you seen the woman whom they call the Red Saint in Panama?” he asked.

“I have not seen her, no; but I have heard of her. The young men put only the Blessed Virgin before her in their worship. It is said that she is lovely as the sun.”

===Chinese===
「你见过巴拿马那个被唤作红圣女的女子吗？」他问。

「我没见过她，先生；不过我听说过她。年轻人敬奉她，只把圣母放在她前面。据说她美如太阳。」

===Original===
“What is her name besides La Santa Roja?”

“I do not know. I have only heard that she is lovely as the sun. They tell in Panama that she came from Cordova and has been to Paris. It is said her family is noble. They tell how she rides great horses, sitting astride, in a meadow guarded with a thick hedge. It is said that in her hand a rapier is a living thing, and that she can fence more skillfully than any man. These things she does in secret that no one may see the crime against her modesty.”

===Chinese===
「除了红圣女，她叫什么名字？」

「我不知道。我只听说她美如太阳。巴拿马人讲她来自科尔多瓦（Cordova），还去过巴黎（Paris）。据说她出身贵族。他们说她骑高头大马，跨骑着在四周围着密密篱笆的草地上奔驰。据说一柄细剑在她手里便成了活物，她的剑术比任何男人都精妙。这些事她都背着人偷偷做，免得有人看见她这般触犯妇德之罪。」

===Original===
“Ah, well!” said Captain Morgan, “if she be beautiful enough what need has she of modesty. This modesty is only a kind of beauty patch which is put on when there are visitors—an enthralling gesture. I should like to see her ride. And do you know nothing more of her?”

“Only what they say in the taverns, sir—that she has stolen worship from the Blessed Saints.”

===Chinese===
「哈，也罢！」摩根船长说，「她若足够美丽，又何须什么妇德。这种妇德不过是做客时才贴上的一种美人斑——一个撩人的姿态。我倒想看看她骑马的样子。此外你当真对她一无所知了？」

「只剩酒馆里人们说的那些了，先生——说她把众圣徒该受的礼拜都夺走了。」

===Original===
Captain Morgan dreamed long in his chair while the runner waited silently. At last Henry shook his head, as though to disengage it of cloying thoughts. He drew a pistol from his belt and gave it to the messenger.

“Take this to Don Juan Perez de Guzman, and say that this is a sample of the weapons we have used in laying Puerto Bello in the dust. But my other weapons are the strong hearts of my followers. I will not send him one of these, but I will bring him a great number. And tell him to keep the pistol for a year, when I myself will come to Panama to receive it from his own hands. Do you understand?”

“I do, sir.”

===Chinese===
摩根船长在椅中神游良久，那信差在一旁静默候着。末了亨利摇了摇头，仿佛要甩开那些缠人的念头。他从腰间抽出一把手枪，递给信差。

「把这个带给唐·胡安·佩雷斯·德·古兹曼（Don Juan Perez de Guzman），就说这是我们用来把贝略港夷为平地的兵器样品。可我其余的兵器，是我部下坚毅的心。这一样我不送他，但我会带一大批给他。再告诉他，把这把手枪保管一年，到时候我亲自到巴拿马，从他手中把它取回。你听明白了吗？」

「明白了，先生。」

===Original===
And in a few days the runner came again, bringing the pistol back, and a large square emerald set in a ring.

“My master begs that you accept this stone as a token of his regard. He begs that you do not give yourself the trouble to come to Panama, for then his duty would overwhelm his admiration and force him to hang you to a tree.”

“It is a good message,” said the captain; “a good, brave message. I should like to meet with Don Juan if only at swords’ points. It has been long since anyone defied me. And did you learn more of La Santa Roja?”

“Only what they tell in the streets, sir. I inquired closely for your benefit. I was told that in the streets she wears a thick veil that none may see her face. Some think she does this so that the poor men who meet her will not kill themselves for love. That is all I could learn. Have you further messages, sir?”

“Only repeat that I will go to the Cup of Gold within the year.”

===Chinese===
几天之后，那信差又来了，把手枪带了回来，外加一枚嵌在戒指上的大方块祖母绿。

「我家主人恳请您收下这枚宝石，作为他敬意的信物。他恳请您不必劳神亲临巴拿马，因为到那时，他的职责便会压倒他的钦佩，迫不得已把您吊死在树上。」

「这是一则好口信，」船长说，「一则又好又硬气的口信。我倒愿意和唐·胡安会一会面，哪怕只是剑锋相向。已经很久没有人敢与我抗衡了。你可又打听到红圣女更多的消息？」

「只剩街谈巷议里说的那些了，先生。我专为您的缘故细细打听了一番。有人告诉我，她在街上戴着一层厚面纱，不让任何人看见她的容貌。有人揣测她这样做，是怕遇见她的穷苦男子会为她殉情自尽。我能打听到的就这些了。您还有什么口信要传吗，先生？」

「只重复一句：一年之内，我要亲赴金杯。」
### III / III

===Original===
Through all his life his will had been like an iron weathervane, steadfastly pointing, always, but never long in one direction. The Indies and the sea and pillage and glory all seemed to have failed him. He had touched all things and watched them pale and shrivel at his touch. And he was lonely. His men regarded him with respect and sullen awe. They were afraid of him, and this state did not feed his vanity as once it had.

===Chinese===
