他这一生的意志，一直像一只铁制风信鸡，始终坚定地指着某个方向——却从未在同一个方向停留太久。印度群岛、大海、劫掠与荣耀，似乎都叫他失望了。他样样都曾触及，又眼睁睁看着它们在自己触手下黯然失色、枯萎凋零。他是孤独的。部下们敬重他，带着阴沉的敬畏。他们畏惧他，可这种畏惧已不像从前那样能滋养他的虚荣了。

===Original===
He wondered if he might not make a friend among his followers, but the time he had dwelt alone in the castle of himself had been so long that this thought filled him with a curious, boyish embarrassment. Who among his followers might be his friend? He considered them, remembering their sullen scowls, their gleaming, avaricious eyes at the division of spoil. He felt nothing but contempt for them.

===Chinese===
他暗想，或许能在追随者中交到一个朋友，可他独居在自身那座城堡里的时日太久了，这个念头竟令他生出一种古怪的、孩子气的不自在。他的追随者里有谁配做他的朋友呢？他把他们一一掂量，想起他们阴沉的怒容，想起分赃时他们那贪婪闪烁的眼睛。他对他们只感到轻蔑。

===Original===
But there was one whom he had noticed, a young Frenchman who was called Coeur de Gris. Captain Morgan had seen him in action, leaping about the deck like a supple animal while his rapier flicked out in lithe tongues of silver fire. He scorned a cutlass for the long thin blade.

===Chinese===
但有一个人他注意到了，是个年轻的法国人，人称柯尔·德·格里（Coeur de Gris）。摩根船长见过他在战斗中的身手，他像一头矫健的野兽般在甲板上腾跃，手中的细剑吐出一道道柔韧的银色火舌。他鄙弃短弯刀，专爱那又长又细的利刃。

===Original===
And this young man answered his orders with a smile at Captain Morgan. There was respect in his eyes, surely, but no fear, no jealousy, and no suspicion.

===Chinese===
而这个年轻人接到他的命令时，总朝摩根船长微微一笑。他眼中无疑有敬意，却没有畏惧，没有嫉妒，也没有猜疑。

===Original===
“I wonder if this Coeur de Gris would be my friend,” mused Henry Morgan. “It is said that he has left a trail of broken hearts from Cuba to Saint Kit’s, and somehow, for this, I fear him a little.”

===Chinese===
「不知这柯尔·德·格里愿不愿意做我的朋友，」亨利·摩根暗自思忖，「据说从古巴到圣基茨岛（Saint Kit’s），他留下了一串心碎的女子。不知怎的，正因这一点，我对他还有几分忌惮。」

===Original===
Captain Morgan sent for the young man, and when he was come, found difficulty in speaking to him.

“Ah—how are you, Coeur de Gris?”

The young man was overwhelmed by any show of warmth from this captain.

“Why, sir, I am very well. Have you orders for me?”

“Orders? No; I—I thought I would like to talk with you—that is all.”

“To talk with me, sir? But to talk of what?”

“Well—How are the many little loves you are reputed to have?” the captain asked in an uneasy effort at joviality.

“Repute is kinder to me than nature, sir.”

===Chinese===
摩根船长派人把年轻人请来，等他到了跟前，却又不知该如何开口。

「呃——你近来可好，柯尔·德·格里？」

这年轻人被船长任何一丝温情的表现都弄得受宠若惊。

「啊，先生，我很好。您有什么吩咐吗？」

「吩咐？没有；我——我想和你聊聊——就这样。」

「和我聊聊，先生？聊些什么呢？」

「嗯——你那据说多不胜数的小情人，可都安好？」船长强作轻松，颇有些别扭地问。

「传言对我可比老天爷对我仁慈多了，先生。」

===Original===
Henry Morgan plunged to his purpose.

“Listen to me, Coeur de Gris! Can you not imagine that I may need a friend? Can you not think of me as a lonely man? Consider how all my followers are afraid of me. They come for orders, but never to pass a quiet time of day. I know I made this so. It was necessary once, for I had to build up respect before I could command obedience. But now there are times when I should like to be telling my thoughts and talking of something besides war and spoil. For ten years I have ravaged the seas like a silent wolf, and I have no friend anywhere.

“I have chosen you to be my friend; first, because I like you, and second, because you have not a thing on earth you might be thinking I want to steal. Thus you may like me without fear. It is a strange thing how my men suspect me. I have given a strict accounting for every voyage, yet, if I spoke to them as friends, they would beat their brains to discover my plot. And will you be my friend, Coeur de Gris?”

===Chinese===
亨利·摩根索性直奔主题。

「听我说，柯尔·德·格里！你就不能想象我也需要一个朋友吗？你就不能把我当作一个孤独的人来想吗？你瞧瞧，我的追随者个个都怕我。他们来找我只为领命，却从不来和我闲话片刻光阴。我知道这是我一手造成的。从前必须如此，因为我得先立起威信，才能号令出服从。可如今有些时候，我真想把心里的话对人说说，谈点战争与劫掠以外的东西。整整十年，我像一头沉默的狼蹂躏着大海，而四海之内我再无一友。

「我选中你做我的朋友：其一，因为我喜欢你；其二，因为你在世上并无一物会让我起意去偷。这样你就可以毫无忌惮地喜欢我。我的部下疑心我疑得真叫奇怪。我每一次出航都把账目算得清清楚楚，可我若拿他们当朋友说句话，他们便会绞尽脑汁去揣测我又在打什么主意。你愿意做我的朋友吗，柯尔·德·格里？」

===Original===
“Why, certainly, I will, my Captain, and had I known of such a thing in your mind, I would have been for long. How may I serve you, Sir?”

===Chinese===
「啊，当然愿意，我的船长，早知您心里有这念头，我早就做了。我能为您效什么劳吗，先生？」

===Original===
“Oh, just by talking with me now and then, and by trusting me a little. I have no motive save my loneliness. But you speak and act like a gentleman, Coeur de Gris. May I ask of your family? or do you draw this name about you like a cape, as so many do here on the Main?”

===Chinese===
「噢，不过是时常和我说说话，再稍稍信我一点。我别无他图，只因寂寞。可你言谈举止都像个世家子弟，柯尔·德·格里。我能问问你的家世吗？还是说你这名字不过像件斗篷一样披在身上遮掩自己，正如大陆上许多人所做的那样？」

===Original===
“It is very simple to tell you of my family. It is said that my father was the great Bras de Fer, and who he was no one ever knew. The people gave me my name, remembering his. My mother is one of the free women of Goaves. She was sixteen when I was born. Hers was a very ancient family, but Huguenot in worship. Their holdings were destroyed in the murders of St. Bartholomew. Thus it came about that they were penniless when my mother was born. And she was picked up by the watch in Paris streets one day and sent to Goaves with a shipload of women vagrants. Bras de Fer found her soon afterwards.”

===Chinese===
「我的家世说起来很简单。据说家父就是那位大名鼎鼎的铁臂，至于他究竟是谁，从无人知晓。众人念着他的名号，便给了我这个名字。家母是戈阿夫的一名自由之身的女子。她生我时年方十六。她出身一个极为古老的世家，却信奉胡格诺派。他们家的产业在圣巴托洛缪（St. Bartholomew）大屠杀中被毁于一旦。于是到了家母出生时，他们已一贫如洗。有一天她在巴黎街头被巡夜的捕快抓住，和满满一船流浪女子一道被发配到了戈阿夫。不久铁臂便遇到了她。」

===Original===
“But you say she is a free woman,” said Henry Morgan, scandalized at this young man’s apparent lack of shame. “Surely she has given up this—this practice, now you are successful on the sea. You are taking home enough for both of you, and more.”

===Chinese===
「可你说她是自由之身的女子，」亨利·摩根说，对这年轻人竟似毫无羞耻之色大为骇然，「如今你已在海上功成名就，她想必早不干这——这营生了吧。你带回家的钱养活你们两个绰绰有余，还多的是。」

===Original===
“I know I am, but she continues. I do not mention it, for why should I interfere with what she considers a serious work. She is proud of her position, proud that her callers are the best people in the port. And it pleases her that, although she is nearly forty, she can more than compete with the young, unseasoned squabs who come in every year. Why should I change the gentle course of her ways, even if I could? No, she is a dear, lovely woman, and she has been a good mother to me. Her only fault is that she is filled with over-many little scruples. She nags at me when I am at home, and cries so when I leave. She is dreadfully afraid that I may find some woman who may do me harm.”

===Chinese===
「我知道我带得够多，可她照旧干着。我从不提这事，因为我有什么资格去干涉她所认定的正经事业呢。她以自己的地位为荣，以登门的人都是港中头面人物为荣。她也颇为得意：虽说已近四十，那些年年新来的嫩雏儿根本不是她的对手。我凭什么要改变她那温柔从容的做派呢，就算我能改变？不，她是个可爱迷人的女子，对我一直是个好母亲。她唯一的毛病，就是小顾虑实在太多。我在家时她对我唠叨个没完，我临走她又哭得一塌糊涂。她生怕我会遇上某个对我有害的女子。」

===Original===
“That is strange, is it not?—considering her life,” said Henry Morgan.

“Why is it strange? Must they have a different brain in that ancient profession? No, sir; I assure you that her life is immaculate—prayers thrice a day, and there is no finer house in all Goaves than hers. Why, sir, when last I went there, I took with me a scarf which fell to my lot in the division, a glorious thing of gossamer and gold. She would not have it. It belonged about the neck of some woman who put her faith in the Romish church, she said, and it would not be decent for a good Huguenot to wear it. Ah! she worries so about me when I am off to sea. She is terribly afraid I may be hurt, but far more afraid of the tainting of my soul. Such is all my knowledge of my family, sir.”

===Chinese===
「这不是很奇怪吗？——想想她的营生。」亨利·摩根说。

「有什么奇怪的？干那一行古老营生的人难道就长着另一种脑子？不，先生；我向您保证，她的生活清白无瑕——一日三次祷告，整个戈阿夫再没有比她那所宅子更体面的了。嘿，先生，上回我去她那儿，带了一条分赃时分到我名下的围巾，那是一件轻纱织金、华美无比的好东西。她却死活不肯收。她说这玩意儿只配戴在某个信罗马公教（Romish church）的女子的脖子上，一个虔诚的胡格诺教徒戴上它可不体面。唉！我一出海她就挂念得要命。她生怕我受了伤，可更怕我的灵魂受了玷污。这就是我对家世所知的全部了，先生。」

===Original===
Captain Morgan had stepped to a cupboard and brought out some queer little jugs with wine of Peru. There were two necks on each jug, and when the wine was poured out from one, a sweet, whistling sound came from the other.

“I took these from a Spanish ship,” he said. “Will you drink with me, Coeur de Gris?”

“I should be very much honored, sir.”

===Chinese===
摩根船长走到一只橱柜前，取出几只古怪的小酒壶，里面装着秘鲁的葡萄酒。每只壶都有两个壶嘴，从一个里倒出酒来，另一个便会发出一声悦耳的哨鸣。

「这是我从一艘西班牙船上得来的，」他说，「你愿不愿意和我共饮一杯，柯尔·德·格里？」

「不胜荣幸，先生。」

===Original===
They sat a long time sipping the wine, then Captain Morgan spoke dreamily.

“I suppose, Coeur de Gris, that you will one day be stricken with the Red Saint, and then we shall have the bees of Panama buzzing out upon us. I have no doubt she is as jealously guarded as was Helen. You have heard of the Red Saint, have you not?”

===Chinese===
两人对酌良久，摩根船长这才如梦似幻地开口。

「我想，柯尔·德·格里，有朝一日你也会被那红圣女击中神魂的，到那时巴拿马那群蜜蜂就会嗡嗡地扑到我们头上来。我毫不怀疑，她被护卫得一定如当年的海伦（Helen）一般森严。你听说过红圣女吧？」

===Original===
The young man’s eyes were glowing with the wine.

“Heard of her!” he said softly. “Sir, I have dreamed of her and called to her in my sleep. Who has not? Who in all this quarter of the world has not heard of her, and yet who knows any single thing about her? It is a strange thing, the magic of this woman’s name. La Santa Roja! La Santa Roja! It conjures up desire in the heart of every man—not active, possible desire, but the ‘if I were handsome, if I were a prince’ kind of desire. The young men make wild plans; some to go disguised to Panama, others to blow it up with quantities of powder. They daydream of carrying the Red Saint off with them. Sir, I have heard a seaman all rotten with disease whispering to himself in the night, ‘If this thing were not on me, I would go adventuring for La Santa Roja.’

“My mother frets and frets there in Goaves, lest I go mad and run to her. She is terrified by this strange woman. ‘Go not near to her, my son,’ she says. ‘This woman is wicked; she is a devil; besides, she is without doubt a Catholic.’ And no one has ever seen her that we know of. We do not know certainly that there exists such a woman as the Red Saint in the Cup of Gold. Ah! she has spread the sea with dreams—with longing dreams. I have been thinking, sir, that perhaps, sometime, the Cup of Gold may go the way of Troy town on account of her.”

===Chinese===
这年轻人的双眼已被酒意映得发亮。

「听说过她！」他轻声说，「先生，我梦见过她，在睡梦里唤过她的名字。谁没有呢？天下这一方天地里，有谁没听说过她，可又有谁对她当真知道一星半点？这真是一桩奇事——这个女子名号里所藏的魔力。红圣女！红圣女！这名字能在每一个男人心头勾起欲望——不是那种切实可行的欲望，而是那种「倘若我生得英俊、倘若我是个亲王」式的欲望。年轻人们做着种种狂妄的计划；有的打算乔装潜入巴拿马，有的打算用大量火药把它炸上天。他们白日里幻想着把红圣女掳走。先生，我曾听见一个浑身溃烂的水手在夜里喃喃自语：『要不是这病缠着我，我也要为红圣女去闯荡一番的。』

「我母亲在戈阿夫那里左也担忧、右也担忧，生怕我哪天发了疯奔向她。这个陌生的女子把她吓坏了。『别靠近她，我的儿，』她说，『这女人是邪祟；她是魔鬼；况且她八成还是个天主教徒。』可据我们所知，从没有人见过她。我们甚至不能肯定金杯里真有这么一个叫红圣女的女子存在。唉！她用梦境——用相思的梦境——铺满了整片大海。先生，我一直在想，或许有朝一日，金杯会因她而重蹈特洛伊城（Troy）的覆辙。」

===Original===
Henry Morgan had filled the glasses again and again. He was slumped forward in his chair, and a little crooked smile was on his mouth.

“Yes,” he said rather thickly, “she is a danger to the peace of nations and to the peace of men’s minds. The matter is wholly ridiculous, of course. She is probably a shrewish bitch who takes her bright features from the legend. But how might such a legend be started? Your health, Coeur de Gris. You will be a good friend to me and true?”

“I will, my Captain.”

===Chinese===
亨利·摩根已把杯子斟了一遍又一遍。他瘫坐在椅中前倾着身子，嘴角挂着一丝微微歪斜的笑。

「不错，」他口齿有些含混地说，「她于诸国的太平、于人心头的安宁，都是一种威胁。这事当然荒唐透顶。她多半是个泼辣的悍妇，她那光鲜的姿色全是传说虚构出来的。可这样的传说又是怎么起的头呢？祝你康健，柯尔·德·格里。你会真心实意地做我的好朋友吧？」

「我会的，我的船长。」

===Original===
And again they sat silently, drinking the rich wine.

“But there is much suffering bound up in women,” Henry Morgan began, as though he had just finished speaking. “They seem to carry pain about with them in a leaking package. You have loved often, they say, Coeur de Gris. Have you not felt the pain they carry?”

“No, sir, I do not think I have. Surely I have been assailed by regrets and little sorrows—everyone has; but mostly I have found only pleasure among women.”

“Ah, you are lucky,” the captain said. “You are filled with luck not to have known the pain. My own life was poisoned by love. This life I lead was forced on me by lost love.”

===Chinese===
两人又默默坐着，饮着那醇厚的葡萄酒。

「可女人身上系着太多的苦痛，」亨利·摩根开口道，仿佛他刚刚才说完一句话似的，「她们像是揣着一个漏了底的包裹，一路把痛苦带到处都是。人家说你常常恋爱，柯尔·德·格里。你就没觉出她们所携的苦痛吗？」

「没有，先生，我想我未曾觉出。我当然也被悔恨与小哀愁袭击过——人人皆然；可我在女人身上，大多只寻到过欢愉。」

「啊，你真走运，」船长说，「你满身都是福气，竟不曾尝过那种苦痛。我自己的命便是被爱毒害的。我现在过的这种日子，是失恋逼着我走上来的。」

===Original===
“Why, how was that, sir? Surely, I had not thought that you—”

“I know; I know how I must have changed so that even you laugh a little at the thought of my being in love. I could not now command the affection of the daughter of an Earl.”

“The daughter of an Earl, sir?”

“Yes, an Earl’s daughter. We loved too perfectly—too passionately. Once she came to me in a rose garden and lay in my arms until the dark was gone. I thought to run away with her to some new, lovely country, and sink her title in the sea behind us. Perhaps even now I might be living safe in Virginia, with little joys crowding my footstool.”

“It is a great pity, sir.” Coeur de Gris was truly sorry for this man.

“Ah, well; her father was informed. On one dark night my arms were pinned to my sides, and she—oh, dear Elizabeth!—was torn away from me. They placed me, still bound, in a ship, and sold me in Barbados. Can you not see, Coeur de Gris, the bitterness that lies restlessly in my heart? During these years, her face has followed me in all my wanderings. Somehow I feel that I might have made some later move—but her father was a powerful lord.”

“And did you never go back for her, after your imprisonment was done?”

Henry Morgan looked down at the floor.

“No, my friend—I never did.”

===Chinese===
「怎么，那又是怎么回事，先生？我万没想到您竟——」

「我知道；我知道自己一定变了许多，连你一想到我竟会恋爱都会忍不住笑一笑。如今的我，怕是连一个伯爵千金的青睐都挣不到了。」

「一位伯爵千金，先生？」

「是的，一位伯爵的女儿。我们爱得太完美——太炽烈了。有一次她到玫瑰园里来找我，伏在我怀中直到夜色褪尽。我满心想着带她私奔到某个崭新可爱的国度，把她的头衔沉在我们身后的大海里。也许到了今日，我正安然地住在弗吉尼亚（Virginia），脚边围着一堆小小的欢喜。」

「太可惜了，先生。」柯尔·德·格里是真心为这个人难过。

「唉，也罢；她父亲风闻了此事。一个漆黑的夜里，我的双臂被反绑在身侧，而她——哦，亲爱的伊丽莎白（Elizabeth）！——被从我怀里硬生生拽走了。他们把我五花大绑塞上一条船，卖到了巴巴多斯（Barbados）。柯尔·德·格里，你难道看不出，我心头那不得安宁的苦涩吗？这些年来，无论我漂泊何方，她的容颜都一路追随。我总觉得我后来本该再有所举动——可她父亲是个权势赫赫的大人。」

「那么您坐牢期满之后，可曾回去找过她？」

亨利·摩根垂眼望着地板。

「没有，我的朋友——我从未回去过。」

### IV / IV

===Original===
The legend of the Red Saint grew in his brain like a powerful vine, and a voice came out of the west to coax and mock, to jeer and cozen Henry Morgan. He forgot the sea and his idling ships. The buccaneers were penniless from their long inactivity. They lay about the decks and cursed their captain for a dreaming fool. He struggled madly against the folding meshes of his dream and argued with the voice.

“May God damn La Santa Roja for sowing the world with an insanity. She has made cutthroats bay the moon like lovesick dogs. She is making me crazy with this vain desire. I must do something—anything—to lay the insistent haunting of this woman I have never seen. I must destroy the ghost. Ah, it is a foolish thing to dream of capturing the Cup of Gold. It would seem that my desire is death.”

===Chinese===
红圣女的传说在他脑中像一株疯长的藤蔓般滋长起来，一个声音从西方飘来，哄骗着他，嘲弄着他，讥诮着他，诱哄着亨利·摩根。他忘却了大海，也忘却了那无所事事地停泊着的船。海盗们因长久的无所事事已囊空如洗。他们瘫在甲板上，咒骂他们的船长是个做白日梦的傻瓜。他疯狂地与那梦境合拢的罗网搏斗，与那个声音争辩。

「愿上帝诅咒红圣女，把整个世界播撒得一片疯癫。她叫杀人不眨眼的暴徒也像害了相思的狗一样对着月亮狂吠。她这徒然的欲望快把我逼疯了。我必须做点什么——做点什么——来镇住这个我从未谋面的女人那挥之不去的缠扰。我必须除掉这个幽灵。唉，痴心妄想夺取金杯，真是愚不可及。看来我的欲望便是死亡。」

===Original===
And he remembered the hunger which had drawn him from Cambria, for it was duplicated and strengthened now. His thoughts were driving sleep away. When drowsiness crept in on the heels of exhaustion, La Santa Roja came in, too.

“I will take Maracaibo,” he cried in desperation. “I will drown this lusting in a bowl of horror. I will pillage Maracaibo, tear it to pieces, and leave it bleeding in the sand.”

(There is a woman in the Cup of Gold, and they worship her for unnamable beauties.)

“Make the gathering at the Isle de la Vaca! Call in true hearts from the corners of the sea! We go to riches!”

===Chinese===
他想起了当年把他从坎布里亚（Cambria）拽出来的那股饥渴，因为它如今又重现了，而且愈发猛烈。他的念头把睡意驱赶得无影无踪。当困倦随着疲惫袭来，红圣女也随之而入。

「我要拿下马拉开波，」他绝望地喊道，「我要把这情欲溺死在一碗恐怖里。我要劫掠马拉开波，把它撕成碎片，让它流着血瘫在沙地上。」

（金杯里有一位女子，人们因她那不可名状的美貌而膜拜她。）

「到瓦卡岛（Isle de la Vaca）集结去！把四海上真心实意的好汉都召集来！我们去发财！」

===Original===
His ships flew out to the bay of Maracaibo and the town was frantic in defense.

“Run into this bottle harbor! Yes, under the guns!”

===Chinese===
他的舰队飞驶向马拉开波湾，那座城池慌乱地拼死防守。

「冲进这瓶状港湾！对，顶着炮火冲！」

===Original===
Cannon balls cried through the air and struck up clouds of dirt from the walls, but the defense held ground.

“It will not fall? Then take it in assault!”

===Chinese===
炮弹尖啸着划破长空，从城墙上炸起一团团烟尘，可守军寸土不让。

「攻不下来？那就强攻！」

===Original===
Powder pots flew over the walls, tearing and maiming the defenders in their burst.

“Who are these wolves?” they cried. “Ah, brothers! we must fight until we die! We must ask no clemency, brothers. If we fall, our dear city—”

===Chinese===
火药罐飞过墙头，在爆炸中把守军撕得血肉模糊。

「这群豺狼是什么人？」他们喊道，「啊，弟兄们！我们必须战至最后一人！我们绝不乞求怜悯，弟兄们。若我们倒下，我们亲爱的城池——」

===Original===
Ladders rose against the fort, and a wave of roaring men swarmed over the walls.

“Ah, San Lorenzo! hide us! bear us away! These are no men, but devils. Hear me! Hear me! Quarter! Ah, Jesus! where art thou now?”

“Throw down the walls! Let no two stones stand together!”

(There is a woman in the Cup of Gold, and she is lovely as the sun.)

“Grant no quarter! Kill the Spanish rats! Kill all of them!”

===Chinese===
云梯竖上了堡垒，一浪怒吼着的人潮蜂拥翻过城墙。

「啊，圣洛伦佐（San Lorenzo）！藏藏我们吧！把我们带走吧！这不是人，是魔鬼。听我说！听我说！饶命啊！啊，耶稣！您如今在哪里？」

「把城墙推倒！别让两块石头还摞在一起！」

（金杯里有一位女子，她美如太阳。）

「不留活口！杀光这些西班牙老鼠！把他们统统杀光！」

===Original===
And Maracaibo lay pleading at his feet. Doors were torn from the houses, and the rooms gutted of every movable thing. They herded the women to a church and locked them in. Then the prisoners were brought to Henry Morgan.

“Here is an old man, sir. We are sure he has riches, but he has hidden them away and we can never find any.”

“Then put his feet in the fire!—why, he is a brazen fool! Break his arms!—He will not tell? Put the whipcord about his temples!—Oh, kill him! kill him and stop his screaming—Perhaps he had no money—”

(There is a woman in Panama—)

“Have you scratched out every grain of gold? Place the city at ransom! We must have riches after pain.”

===Chinese===
于是马拉开波匍匐在他脚下哀求。屋门被从门框上扯下来，房间里的每一样能搬走的东西都被搜刮一空。他们把妇女们驱赶进一座教堂里锁起来。然后俘虏们被押到亨利·摩根跟前。

「这位是个老头儿，先生。我们断定他有钱财，可他把东西藏了起来，我们怎么也找不着。」

「那就把他的脚架到火上烤！——嗨，这老东西真是死硬！把他的胳膊折断！——他还不招？拿绳索勒住他的太阳穴！——哎，杀了他！杀了他别让他再嚎了——说不定他本就没钱——」

（巴拿马有一位女子——）

「每一粒金子都刮出来没有？让全城缴赎金！受完了苦，总该发财了。」

===Original===
A fleet of Spanish ships came sailing to the rescue.

“A Spanish squadron coming? We will fight them! No, no; we shall run from them if we can get away. Our hulls lag in the water with their weight of gold. Kill the prisoners!”

(—she is lovely as the sun.)

===Chinese===
一支西班牙舰队驶来救援。

「西班牙舰队来了？我们迎战！不，不；能脱身的话我们就跑。咱们的船身被金子的重量压得在水里走不动了。把俘虏都杀掉！」

（——她美如太阳。）

===Original===
And Captain Morgan sailed from broken Maracaibo. Two hundred and fifty thousand pieces of eight were in his ships, and rolls of silken stuffs and plates of silver and sacks of spices. There were golden images from the Cathedral, and vestments crusted with embroidery of pearls. And the city was a fire-swept wreck.

“We are richer than we could have hoped. There will be joy in Tortuga when we come. Every man a hero! We shall have a mad riot of a time.”

(La Santa Roja is in Panama.)

===Chinese===
摩根船长驶离了破碎的马拉开波。他船上有二十五万枚八里亚尔银币（pieces of eight），还有一卷卷丝绸料子、一块块银板和一袋袋香料。有大教堂里抢来的金质神像，有缀满珍珠绣花的事神法衣。而那座城池已是一片被火舌舔过的废墟。

「我们比预期的还要富有。等我们回去，托尔图加要欢腾了。人人都成了英雄！咱们要好好疯闹一场。」

（红圣女就在巴拿马。）

===Original===
“Ah, God! then if I must, I must. But I fear I go to my death. It is a dreadful thing to be attempting. If this is my desire, I must, though I die.” He called young Coeur de Gris to him.

“You have distinguished yourself in this fight, my friend.”

“I have done what was necessary, sir.”

“But you fought finely. I saw you when we engaged. Now I have made you my lieutenant in the field, my second in command. You are brave, you are sagacious, and you are my friend. I can trust you, and who among my men will bear this trust if it be worth his while to fail?”

“It is a great honor, sir. I will pay you, surely, with my fidelity. My mother will be very pleased.”

===Chinese===
「啊，上帝！既然非去不可，那就去吧。可我怕我是去送死。这是一桩可怕的事啊。倘若这就是我的欲望，那我就非去不可，纵然赴死。」他把年轻的柯尔·德·格里唤到跟前。

「这一仗里你立了大功，我的朋友。」

「我不过是做了分内之事，先生。」

「可你打得漂亮极了。接舷交战时我瞧见你了。如今我任命你为我的陆军中尉，我的副指挥。你勇敢、你机敏，而且你是我的朋友。我能信任你——可我手下这些人里，若论值得为辜负这份信任而冒险的，又有谁当得起呢？」

「这是莫大的荣耀，先生。我必以我的忠心来回报您。我母亲一定会非常高兴。」

===Original===
“Yes,” said Captain Morgan; “you are a young fool, and that is a virtue in this business as long as one has a leader. Now the men are straining to get back that they may spend their money. If it were possible they would be pushing the ships to hurry them. What will you do with your money, Coeur de Gris?”

“Why, I shall send half to my mother. The remaining sum I shall divide in two. Part I shall put away, and on the other I expect to be drunk for a few days, or perhaps a week. It is good to be drunk after fighting.”

“Drunkenness has never been a pleasure to me,” the captain said. “It makes me very sad. But I have a new venture turning in my brain. Coeur de Gris, what is the richest city of the western world? What place has been immune from the slightest gesture of the Brotherhood? Where might we all make millions?”

“But, sir, you do not think—Surely you cannot consider it possible to take—”

“I will take Panama—even the Cup of Gold.”

===Chinese===
「不错，」摩根船长说，「你是个年轻的傻瓜，而在这行当里，只要头上还有一个首领领着，这傻瓜气便是美德。如今弟兄们恨不得立刻回去把钱花掉。要是可能的话，他们恨不得亲手推着船快走。你打算拿你的钱怎么办，柯尔·德·格里？」

「嘿，我会寄一半给我母亲。剩下的一半我还要再分作两份。一份存起来，另一份嘛，我打算拿去醉上几天，也许一个礼拜。打完仗之后痛饮一场，真是惬意。」

「醉酒于我从来不是乐事，」船长说，「它只会叫我格外悲伤。可我脑子里正转着一个新主意。柯尔·德·格里，西半球最富庶的城池是哪一座？有哪个地方连兄弟会最轻微的一指头都未曾沾染过？我们大家都能在哪里发大财？」

「可是，先生，您不是在想——您总不会当真以为有可能攻下——」

「我要拿下巴拿马——拿下那座金杯。」

===Original===
“How may you do this thing? The city is strongly guarded with walls and troops, and the way across the isthmus is nigh impassable but for the burro trail. How will you do this thing?”

“I must take Panama. I must capture the Cup of Gold.” The captain’s jaw set fiercely.

Now Coeur de Gris was smiling quietly.

“Why do you grin at me?” demanded Captain Morgan. “I was thinking of a chance remark I made a little time ago, that Panama was like to go the way of Troy town.”

“Ah! this nameless woman is in your mind. Dismiss her! It may be there exists no such woman.”

“But then, sir, we are rich enough of this last spoil.”

“It would be no evil thing to grow richer. I am tired of plundering. I would rest securely.”

===Chinese===
「您怎么做到这件事？那座城有城墙和重兵把守，而穿越地峡的路除了驴子走的小径之外几乎无路可通。您要怎样做成这件事？」

「我必须拿下巴拿马。我必须夺取金杯。」船长的下颚凶狠地绷紧了。

此时柯尔·德·格里正静静地微笑着。

「你冲我笑什么？」摩根船长质问。「我在想我不久前随口说的一句话——巴拿马怕是要重蹈特洛伊城的覆辙。」

「啊！原来你心里惦记着这个无名的女子。把她忘了吧！也许世上根本没有这么个女子。」

「可是先生，光凭上回这一票，我们已经够富了。」

「更富一些也不算坏事。我厌倦了劫掠。我想安稳地歇一歇。」

===Original===
Coeur de Gris hesitated a time, while his eyes were covered with a soft veil.

“I am thinking, sir, that when we come to Panama every man will be at his friend’s throat over the Red Saint.”

“Oh, you may trust me to keep order among my men—strict order—though I hang half of them to do it. A while ago I sent word to Panama that I would go there, but I did it as a joke. And I wonder, now, whether they have been fortifying themselves. Perhaps they, too, thought it a joke. Go, now, Coeur de Gris, and speak to no man of this. I make you my ambassador. Let the men throw their gold away. Encourage gambling—here—now—on the ship. Give them an example at the taverns—an expensive example. Then they will be driven to go out with me. I must have an army this time, my friend, and even then we may all die. Perhaps that is the chief joy of life—to risk it. Do my work well, Coeur de Gris, and it may be one day you will be richer than you can think.”

===Chinese===
柯尔·德·格里迟疑了一阵，眼中似蒙上一层柔纱。

「我在想，先生，等我们到了巴拿马，为了红圣女，每个人都会跟自己的伙伴撕破脸皮的。」

「哦，你尽可放心，我自会管束住我的弟兄——严加管束——哪怕为此得绞死他们一半。不久前我传话到巴拿马说我要去，可那不过是一句玩笑。如今我倒想知道，他们是不是一直在加筑防御。也许他们也当那只是句玩笑。去吧，柯尔·德·格里，此事不可对任何人提起。我任命你做我的使节。让弟兄们把金子都挥霍掉。怂恿他们赌钱——就在这儿——现在——在这船上。到酒馆里给他们做个样子——做个出手阔绰的样子。这样他们就不得不跟我出航了。这一回我必须有一支大军，我的朋友，即便如此，我们也可能全体殒命。或许那正是人生最大的乐事——拿性命去冒险。把我的差事办好，柯尔·德·格里，将来有一天你或许会富得超出你的想象。」

===Original===
Young Coeur de Gris stood musing by the mast.

“Our captain, our cold captain, has been bitten by this great, nebulous rumoring. How strange this pattern is! It is as though the Red Saint had been stolen from my arms. My dream is violated. I wonder, when they know, if every man will carry this feeling of a bitter loss—will hate the captain for stealing his desire.”

===Chinese===
年轻的柯尔·德·格里站在桅杆旁出神。

「我们的船长，我们那位冷若冰霜的船长，竟也被这庞大而朦胧的传闻咬住了。这格局何其古怪！仿佛红圣女是从我怀里被人偷走了一般。我的梦被人侵犯了。我纳闷，等众人知道之后，是否每个男人都会怀着这种痛失所爱的感觉——是否都会因为船长偷走了他心头的欲望而恨他。」

### V / V

===Original===
Sir Edward Morgan led forces against St. Eustatius, and, while the battle raged, a slim, brown Indian slipped up and drove a long knife into his stomach. The Lieutenant-Governor set his lips in a straight, hard line, and crumpled to the ground.

“My white breeches will be ruined,” he thought. “Why did the devil have to do it, just when we were getting on so nicely. I should have got special thanks from his Majesty, and now I shall not be here to receive them. Heaven! he chose a painful place!” And then the full tragedy struck him.

“An ordinary knife,” he muttered; “and in the stomach. I should have preferred a sword in the hand of an equal—but a knife—in the stomach! I must look disreputable with all this blood and dirt on me. And I cannot straighten up! Christ! the wretch struck a sensitive spot.”

His men sadly bore him to Port Royal.

===Chinese===
爱德华·摩根爵士（Sir Edward Morgan）率军进攻圣尤斯特歇斯（St. Eustatius），酣战正炽之际，一个精瘦的棕色印第安人悄悄摸上来，将一柄长刀捅进了他的肚子。这位副总督把双唇抿成一条又直又硬的线，颓然瘫倒在地。

「我这条白裤子要毁了，」他想，「这恶魔为什么偏偏挑这种时候下手，我们正打得顺风顺水呢。我本该得到陛下亲口嘉奖的，如今我却要不在人世去领那份恩典了。天哪！他竟挑了这么个要命的地方！」紧接着，这出悲剧的全部分量才落在他心头。

「一把寻常的刀子，」他喃喃道，「还捅在肚子上。我本宁愿挨一位同等级别手中的剑——可偏偏是刀子——还是捅在肚子上！我身上沾了这么多血和泥，一定狼狈极了。我还直不起身来！基督啊！这恶棍竟戳中了我这么敏感的地方。」

部下们满怀悲恸地把他抬回了皇家港。

===Original===
“It was unavoidable,” he told the Governor; “slipped up on me with a knife and stabbed me in the stomach. Such a little devil he couldn’t reach any higher, I suppose. Report the affair to the Crown, will you, Sir? And please do not mention the knife—or the stomach. And now will you leave me with my daughter? I shall be dying soon.”

===Chinese===
「这事无可避免，」他对总督说，「他偷偷摸到我身边，拿刀子捅了我肚子一下。那么个小鬼，估计也只够得着这么高。请把这事儿上报朝廷，好吗，先生？不过请千万别提那把刀子——也别提肚子。现在，能让我和女儿单独待会儿吗？我快不行了。」

===Original===
Elizabeth stood over him in a darkened room.

“Are you hurt badly, father?”

“Yes, quite badly. I shall die presently.”

“Nonsense, papa; you are only joking to excite me.”

“Elizabeth, does it sound like nonsense—and have you ever heard me joke? I have several things to speak of, and the time is very short. What will you do? There is little money left. We have been living on my salary ever since the King made his last general suggestion for a loan.”

===Chinese===
伊丽莎白（Elizabeth）在一间昏暗的房间里俯身看着他。

「您伤得重吗，父亲？」

「是的，很重。我不久就要死了。」

「胡说，爸爸；您不过是想吓唬我才开玩笑的。」

「伊丽莎白，这话听来像胡说吗——你几曾听我说过笑话？我有几句话要交代，时日已不多了。你以后怎么办？钱已所剩无几。自从国王上回暗示全朝认捐一笔借款，我们一直靠我的薪俸度日。」

===Original===
“But what are you talking about, papa! You cannot die and leave me here alone and lost in the colonies. You cannot, cannot do it!”

“Whether I can or not, I shall die presently. Now let us discuss this matter while we can. Perhaps your cousin who has come to such fame through robbery will care for you, Elizabeth. I am pained at the thought, but—but—it is necessary to live—very necessary. And after all, he is your cousin.”

“I will not believe it. I simply will not believe it. You cannot die!”

===Chinese===
「可您在说什么呀，爸爸！您不能丢下我，让我一个人孤苦伶仃地流落在这殖民地里。您不能，绝不能这样做！」

「不管我能不能，我反正是快死了。趁我们还能谈，咱们就把这事商量妥当吧。也许你那位靠打劫闯出名头的表兄会愿意照应你，伊丽莎白。我一想到这个就心痛——可是——可是——人总得活下去——非常必要。况且，他终归是你的表兄。」

「我不信。我简直不信。您不能死！」

===Original===
“You must stay with the Governor until you can meet your cousin. Tell him the exact standing of the matter; no fawning—but do not be too proud. Remember he is your own blood cousin, even though he is a robber.” His heavy breathing filled the room. Elizabeth had begun to cry softly, like a child who cannot quite tell whether or not it is hurt. Finally words were forced from Sir Edward’s lips.

“I have heard that you can tell a gentleman by the way he dies—but I should like to groan. Robert would have groaned if he had wished. Of course, Robert was queer—but then—he was my own brother—he would have shrieked if he had felt like it. Elizabeth, will you—please—leave the room. I am sorry—but I must groan. Never speak of it—Elizabeth—you promise—never—never to speak of it?”

===Chinese===
「你暂时就跟着总督住，直到能见到你表兄为止。把事情的确切情形如实告诉他；不必谄媚——可也别太高傲。记住他终归是你血肉相连的表兄，纵然他是个强盗。」他沉重的呼吸声充斥着整个房间。伊丽莎白已开始轻声啜泣，像一个吃不准自己到底疼不疼的孩子。最后，爱德华爵士终于把话硬逼出了嘴唇。

「我听说，看一个人死的样子，就能看出他是不是绅士——可我很想哼唤一声。罗伯特要哼便哼了。当然，罗伯特脾气古怪——可话说回来——他是我一母同胞的兄弟——他要觉得难受，叫也叫了。伊丽莎白，你能不能——劳驾——先出去一会儿。对不住——可我得哼唤一声。这事绝不能对人提——伊丽莎白——你答应我——绝不——绝不对人提，好吗？」

===Original===
And when she came again, Sir Edward Morgan was dead.

===Chinese===
等她再进来时，爱德华·摩根爵士已经死了。

### VI / VI

===Original===
Spring had come to Cambria, welling up out of the Indies and out of the hot, dry heart of Africa, and this the fifteenth Spring since Henry went away. Old Robert liked to think, and then came curiously to believe, that his son sent the Spring to Cambria out of the tropic places. There was a green fur climbing up the hills, and the trees were testing new, fragile leaves in the winds.

===Chinese===
春天来到了坎布里亚（Cambria），它从印度群岛涌起，从非洲炎热干燥的心脏涌起，而这是亨利离去后的第十五个春天。老罗伯特（Old Robert）先是乐于去想，继而又古怪地信以为真：是他的儿子从那些热带地方把春天送回了坎布里亚。一层绿色的绒毛正攀上山岗，树木正在风里试探着新抽出的一片片娇嫩叶子。

===Original===
Old Robert’s face had grown more set. Around his mouth lived less a smile and more a grimace, as though some ancient, anguished smile had frozen there. Ah! the years had been lonely, barren things, with nothing left in their arms for him. He knew the meaning, now, of Gwenliana’s words—that age brought nothing with it save a cold, restless waiting; a dull expectancy of a state that might not be imagined with any assurance. Perhaps he waited for the time when Henry would come to him again. But that could scarcely be so. He was not at all sure that he wanted to see Henry any more. It would be disturbing. When one is old, one hates disturbing things.

===Chinese===
老罗伯特的面容愈发凝滞了。他嘴角边笑容少了，愁苦多了，仿佛一抹古老而痛苦的微笑在那里冻结住了。唉！这些年来尽是些孤独贫瘠的岁月，它们的臂弯里再没给他留下什么。如今他懂得了格温莉安娜（Gwenliana）那番话的意思——老来一无所得，唯有冰冷的、焦躁的等待；唯有对一种无法确然想象的境况作着迟钝的期盼。也许他等的，是亨利再度回到他身边的那一天。可这也未必。他根本说不准自己还想不想再见到亨利。那会扰乱心绪的。人老了，就厌恶扰乱心绪的事。

===Original===
For a long time he had wondered, “What is Henry doing now? what seeing now?” And then the boy had faded slightly, had come to be like people in old books—not quite real, yet real enough to be remembered. But Robert thought often of this abstract person, his son, of whom he heard wavering rumors now and again.

===Chinese===
很长一段时间里他总在琢磨：「亨利这会儿在做什么？这会儿在瞧见些什么？」可后来那少年的形象便渐渐淡了，变得像旧书里的人物——不那么真切了，却仍真切得能被人记起。但罗伯特时常想起这个抽象的人——他的儿子，关于他时不时会传来些含糊的传言。

===Original===
With waking on the fine morning of the Spring, Robert had said, “I will climb up to see Merlin today. Strange how that old man lives under the growing pressure of his years. There must be more than a hundred of them now. His body is a thin wisp—nothing more than a suggestion that here was once a body. But William says, if you can be picking thought out of William’s speaking, that his voice is golden and strong as always, and that he still talks tremendous nonsense that would not be tolerated at London. It is amazing how this road-mender has his whole life curled like a kitten around four days in London. But I must be going to Merlin. It is not likely that I shall go again.”

===Chinese===
在这个春日美好的清晨醒来，罗伯特便说：「今天我要爬上去看看默林（Merlin）。奇怪，这老头怎么在岁月愈积愈重的压力下还活得下去。他如今少说也有一百多岁了。他的身子瘦成了一缕——只像是表示这儿曾经有过一具身躯罢了。可威廉（William）说——要是你能从威廉的话里头挑拣出点意思来的话——他的嗓音还像往常一样洪亮有力，还在说着伦敦容不下的滔滔胡话。这位修路工竟把自己一生的日子像只小猫似的蜷在伦敦的那四天上，真是不可思议。可我非去看默林不可了。我大概不会再去了。」

===Original===
The steep, rocky path was a thing of torture to him; more a cruel thing because of his memory of lithe, powerful legs, and lungs as tireless as bellows. Once he had led all comers in the mountain race, but now he climbed a bit, then rested on a stone, and climbed again—up and up into the cleft and over the rock shoulder. It was noon when he came at last to Crag-top.

===Chinese===
那条陡峭多石的山路于他简直是受刑；更残忍的是，他还记得自己当年那双矫健有力的腿，和那如风箱般不知疲倦的肺。他曾经领着所有登山的来者一路领先，可如今他爬上一程，就在石头上歇一歇，再爬一程——往上，再往上，爬进山缝，翻过岩肩。等他终于到达崩顶（Crag-top）时，已是正午了。

===Original===
Merlin met him at the door before he had time to knock, and Merlin had no more changed than the harps and spearheads hanging to his walls. He seemed to have discarded time like a garment. Merlin came to Robert with no surprise. It was as though he had known of this slow pilgrimage a thousand years before the day had happened.

===Chinese===
他还没来得及敲门，默林已在门口迎着他了。默林一点没变，就跟挂在他墙上的竖琴和矛头一样。他仿佛把时间像件衣裳似的脱下丢弃了。默林走到罗伯特跟前，毫无惊讶之色，仿佛在这一天到来的一千年前，他早已知道会有这趟缓慢的朝圣。

===Original===
“It is very long, Robert, since you climbed the path to me, and long since I went down it.” And “down, down” sang the harps. He spoke the language of the strings, and they responded like a distant choir in high mass of the mountains.

“But it’s an old man who climbs to you now, Merlin. The trail is a beast enemy to wrestle with. You seem no older. I wonder when you will come to die. Do not your years sometimes argue that question with you?”

===Chinese===
「罗伯特，自从你爬上这条路来看我，已经过去很久了；自我顺着它走下去，也过去很久了。」竖琴则唱着「下去，下去」。他说着琴弦的语言，琴弦便如群山高处的弥撒中一支远方的歌咏队般应和着。

「可如今爬上来的，是个老头子了，默林。这山道简直是一头与人搏斗的野兽冤家。你倒是一点不见老。我纳闷你什么时候才会死。你的年岁有时可曾拿这个问题来跟你争辩？」

===Original===
“Why, to speak truthfully, Robert, I have taken it in my mind several times—but always there were too many things to think about. I could not take the time to die. If I did, I might not be able to think ever again.

“For up here, Robert, that furtive hope the valley men call faith becomes a questionable thing. Oh, without doubt, if there were a great many about me, and they all intoning endlessly the chant, ‘There is a wise, kind God; surely we shall go on living after death,’ then I might be preparing for the coming life. But here, alone, halfway up the sky, I am afraid that death would interrupt my musing. The mountains are a kind of poultice for a man’s abstract pain. Among them he laughs—oh, far more often than he cries.”

===Chinese===
「怎么，说实话，罗伯特，我已经动过几次这念头了——可总是要想的事太多。我抽不出空来死。我要是死了，也许就再也想不了事情了。

「因为在这儿，在这么高的地方，山谷里的人所称之信仰的那种鬼鬼祟祟的希望，便成了件可疑的东西。哦，毋庸置疑，倘若我身边有许许多多人，而他们都无休止地齐声吟诵着『有一位仁慈智慧的上帝；我们死后必定还会活下去』这支圣歌，那么我也许会为来世做些准备。可在这儿，独自一人，身在半天之上，我只怕死亡会打断我的玄想。群山对一个人抽象的苦痛，好比一帖敷药。在群山之间，人欢笑——哦，远比哭泣的次数多。」

===Original===
“You know,” said Robert, “my mother, the old Gwenliana, made a last, curious prophecy before she died. ‘This night the world ends,’ she said, ‘and there will be no more earth to walk upon.’”

“Robert, I think she spoke truth. I think her dying words were truth, whatever may have been her other auguries. This gnawing thought comes visiting, sometimes, and because of it I am afraid to die—horribly afraid. If by my living I give life to you, and fresh existence to the fields and trees and all the long green world, it would be an unutterable deed to wipe them all out like a chalk drawing. I must not—yet awhile.

“But enough of these foreboding things. There is no laughter in them. You, Robert, have been too long in the valley of men. Your lips laugh, but there is no amusement in your heart. I think you place your lips so, like twigs over a trap, to conceal your pain from God. Once you tried to laugh with all your soul, but you did not make the satirist’s concession—that of buying with a little amusement at yourself the privilege of laughing a great deal at others.”

===Chinese===
「你知道吗，」罗伯特说，「我母亲，老格温莉安娜，临终前下了一个古怪的预言。她说：『今夜世界便要终结，此后再没有可走的土地了。』」

「罗伯特，我想她说的是真话。我想她临终的话是真理，不管她其余的预言如何。这个啃噬人的念头有时会来造访，正因如此我才怕死——怕得要命。倘若我活着便能给你以生命，给田野和树木、给整个绵长的翠绿世界以新鲜的存在，那么像抹去一幅粉笔画一般把它们统统抹掉，便是一桩不可言说的恶行。我万万不能——至少现在不能。

「不说这些不祥的事了。其中毫无欢笑可言。你呀，罗伯特，在人类的山谷里待得太久了。你的嘴唇在笑，可你心里没有半点欢趣。我想你是把嘴唇这样摆着，像陷阱上面盖着的细枝，好把你的苦痛藏起来不让上帝看见。你曾经试着用整个灵魂去笑，可你没有做讽刺家那种让步——拿对自己的一点自嘲，换得大量嘲笑别人的特权。」

===Original===
“I know that I am defeated, Merlin, and there seems to be no help for it. Victory, or luck, or whatever you wish to call it, appears to lie hidden in a chosen few as babies’ teeth hide under the gums. Of late years this God has a hard, calculating game with me. There have been moments when I thought he cheated.”

===Chinese===
「我知道我败了，默林，而且似乎无可救药。胜利也好，运气也好，随你怎么叫它，仿佛只暗藏在少数几个天选之人身上，就像婴儿的牙齿藏在牙床底下。这些年来，这位上帝跟我下的是一局冷酷而精于算计的棋。有些时候，我甚至觉得他作弊。」

===Original===
Merlin spoke slowly:

“Once I played against a dear young god with goat’s feet, and that game was the reason for my coming here. But then, I made the great concession and signed with sad laughter. Robert, did I not hear a long time past that you were roving in your mind? Surely William stopped by and told me you had grown insane. Did you not do reprehensible things in your rose garden?”

===Chinese===
默林缓缓说道：

「我曾经和一个长着山羊蹄子的可爱小神对弈，那一局棋正是我来此地的缘由。不过后来，我作了那重大的让步，用哀伤的笑签了降书。罗伯特，我不是好久以前就听说你神志有些涣散了吗？威廉显然顺道来过，告诉我你疯了。你是不是在你的玫瑰园里做了些要不得的事？」

===Original===
Robert smiled bitterly. “That was one of this God’s tricks,” he said. “I will tell you how it was. One day, when I was pulling the dead leaves from my roses, it came upon me to make a symbol. This is no unusual thing. How often do men stand on hill tops with their arms outstretched, how often kneel in prayer and cross themselves. I pulled a bloom and threw it into the air, and the petals showered down about me. It seemed that this act gathered up and told the whole story of my life in a gesture. Then the loveliness of white petals on black earth absorbed me, and I forgot my symbol. I threw another and another, until the ground was snowed with rose leaves. Suddenly I looked up and saw a dozen men standing about laughing at me. They had come by from church. ‘Hee!’ they said, ‘Robert has lost his mind. Hee! his sense is slipping out of him. Ho! he is a child again, throwing rose petals.’ It seemed a crazed God who could allow this thing.”

===Chinese===
罗伯特苦涩地笑了笑。「那正是这位上帝的把戏之一，」他说，「我告诉你事情是怎样的。有一天，我正从玫瑰上摘除枯叶，忽然动了做个象征的念头。这原不是什么稀罕事。人们有多少次站在山顶上张开双臂，有多少次跪下来祷告、在自己胸前画十字。我摘下一朵花抛向空中，花瓣便如雨点般洒落在我四周。这一举仿佛用一个姿态就把我一生的全部故事聚拢起来、讲述尽了。随后，白花瓣落在黑土地上的那番美景把我整个吸住了，我便忘了我的象征。我又抛了一朵，又抛一朵，直到地上落满了玫瑰花瓣，像覆了一层雪。猛然间我一抬头，看见十来个人围着我大笑。他们是从教堂做完礼拜路过。『嘻！』他们说，『罗伯特疯了。嘻！他的神智一点点溜走了。嗬！他又成了个孩子，抛玫瑰花瓣玩呢。』能容许这种事发生的上帝，想必是个疯子。」

===Original===
Merlin was shaking with a silent glee.

“Oh, Robert! Robert! why must you blame the world when it protects itself against you? I think God and the world are one to you. If there were ten people in the valley below who liked the look of rose leaves on the ground, you would only be a very queer person, interesting and something of a curiosity. They would bring strangers to your house on Sunday afternoons and exhibit you. But, since there are none, of course you are a radical who must be locked up or hanged. Judgment of insanity is truly the hanging of a man’s mind. If it be whispered of him that his brain wanders, then nothing he can say will matter to anyone ever again, except as a thing to laugh at.

“Can you not see, Robert? People have so often been hurt and trapped and tortured by ideas and contraptions which they did not understand, that they have come to believe all things passing their understanding are vicious and evil—things to be stamped out and destroyed by the first comer. They only protect themselves, thus, against the ghastly hurts that can come to them from little things grown up.”

===Chinese===
默林乐得无声地抖了起来。

「噢，罗伯特！罗伯特！世人抵御你的时候，你何必非要怪罪他们呢？我想在你眼里，上帝和世人原是一回事。倘若底下山谷里有十个人喜欢地上落着玫瑰花瓣的景致，那你不过是个非常古怪的人，有趣，多少算个稀罕物。他们会在礼拜天下午带陌生人到你家来，把你当稀奇展示。可既然一个都没有，你自然就成了个必须关起来或者绞死的过激分子。判定一个人疯癫，实在是把他的心智绞死了。只要有人在他耳语说他脑子走了神，那么从此他再说什么都不会对任何人有分量了，除了当作笑料。

「你难道看不出吗，罗伯特？人们曾被他们不理解的观念和器物伤害、困陷、折磨过太多次，以至于他们渐渐相信，凡是不为他们所理解的事物都是恶毒而邪恶的——是任何人都该一脚踩死、毁掉的东西。他们这样做，无非是在保护自己，抵御那些从小事长成的骇人伤害。」

===Original===
“I know,” said Robert; “I know all that, and I do not cry out against it. My great complaint is that the only possession I carry about with me is a bag of losses. I am the owner solely of the memory of things I used to have. Perhaps it is well—for I seem to love them more now that I have them not. But I cannot understand how this fortune may be born hidden in a chosen few. My own son assaults and keeps each one of his desires, if the winds tell truth.”

===Chinese===
「我知道，」罗伯特说，「这一切我都知道，我也不为此叫屈。我最大的怨言是：我随身携带的唯一家产，就是一袋损失。我所拥有的，仅仅是我曾经拥有之物的记忆罢了。这也许也不错——因为我如今失去它们了，反倒似乎更爱它们。可我怎么也想不通，这好运怎么就会天生藏在少数几个人身上。我自己那个儿子，倘若风传不假的话，却是在逐一攻取、逐一保有着他每一个欲望。」

===Original===
“You had a son, Robert; I remember now. I think I prophesied that he would rule some world or other if he did not grow up.”

“And so he does. News of him comes out of the south on a light, inaccurate wind. Rumor has wings like bats. It is said that he rules a wild race of pirates; that he has captured towns and pillaged cities. The English are elated, and call him a hero and a patriotic man—and so do I, sometimes. But I fear if I were a Spaniard, he would be only a successful robber. I have heard—though I do not believe it; I do not want to believe it—that he has tortured prisoners.”

===Chinese===
「你有个儿子来着，罗伯特；我想起来了。我记得我预言过，他要是不长大的话，迟早会统治某个世界的。」

「他果然做到了。关于他的消息，乘着一缕轻飘飘、不大可靠的风从南方吹来。传言长着蝙蝠一样的翅膀。据说他统治着一群野蛮的海盗；据说他攻下了城镇、洗劫了城池。英国人洋洋得意，称他为英雄、为爱国志士——有时我也这样。可我恐怕，我要是个西班牙人，他不过就是个得手的强盗罢了。我还听说——尽管我不信；我也不愿信——他酷刑拷打俘虏。」

===Original===
“So,” Merlin mused, “he has come to be the great man he thought he wanted to be. If this is true, then he is not a man. He is still a little boy and wants the moon. I suppose he is rather unhappy about it. Those who say children are happy, forget their childhood. I wonder how long he can stave off manhood.

“Robert, have you seen those great black ants which are born with wings? They fly a day or two, then drop their wings and fall upon the ground to crawl for all their lives. I wonder when your son will drop his wings. Is it not strange, Robert, how, among men, this crawling is revered—how children tear at their wings, so they may indulge in this magnificent crawling?”

“What makes boys grow to men?” Robert asked. “What circumstance rots out their wing roots?”

===Chinese===
「原来如此，」默林沉吟道，「他成了他自以为想成为的那种大人物了。倘若这是真的，那他就还不是个男子汉。他还是个小男孩，还想要天上的月亮。我料想他为此相当不快活。那些说孩子是快乐的人，都忘了自己的童年。我纳闷他还能把成年推迟多久。

「罗伯特，你可曾见过那种生来就长着翅膀的黑色大蚂蚁？它们飞上一两天，便脱了翅膀，跌落在地，从此一辈子爬行度日。我纳闷你儿子什么时候才会脱掉他的翅膀。罗伯特，这岂不古怪吗——在人类当中，这种爬行竟备受推崇——孩子们竟会去扯掉自己的翅膀，好尽情享受这种堂而皇之的爬行？」

「是什么使男孩长成男子汉的？」罗伯特问，「又是什么情形腐蚀掉他们翅膀的根基？」

===Original===
“Why, a great many never have wings, and some tear them off for themselves; some are sudden things and others very tedious. I do not know them all, but mine was ridicule—a kind of self-ridicule. I loved a small girl in the valley, and I suppose she was beautiful. I hope I was handsome. I made a song for her and called her the Bride of Orpheus. I rather fancied myself Orpheus, then. But she considered marriage with a deity as some manner of a crime against nature. She lectured me. Every man, so she said, owed it to something or other—his family or his community or himself, I forget just what—to make a success of himself. She was vague as to the nature of success, but she made it very plain that song was not a structure of success. And deities she abhorred, especially pagan deities. There was a man with lands and houses who was reassuringly human. Even in my old age I think spitefully that he was deplorably human. So they were married, and ridicule gnawed off my wings.

“I paraded murder and suicide and fields of glory through my mind to fight this little paining ridicule. In my shame, I thought to lock up my songs from the world, so that never again might people hear them. The world did not even know when I was gone. No little groups of people came to plead with me to return—and I had promised the ridicule they would. My bitten wings dropped; I was a man and did not want the moon. And when I tried to sing again, my voice had grown husky like a drover’s voice, and my songs were thick with forethoughts and plannings.”

===Chinese===
「怎么说呢，许许多多的人压根儿就没长过翅膀，还有些人自己把它们扯掉；有的是一刹那的事，有的则拖得很漫长。我不能一一尽知，但我那一次是嘲笑——一种自我嘲笑。我爱过山谷里的一个小姑娘，我想她是很美的。但愿我当时也算英俊。我给她作了一支歌，唤她作俄耳甫斯的新娘（Bride of Orpheus）。那时我颇为自得地把自己当作俄耳甫斯（Orpheus）。可她觉得嫁给一位神祇，是某种违背天性的罪行。她训了我一顿。她说，每个男人，都欠着这个那个——他的家族、他的乡邻，或是他自己，我记不清究竟是哪个——一份情分，要把自己造就成一个成功者。至于成功的内涵，她说得含含糊糊，可她把话说得很明白：唱歌可不是成功的根基。而神祇是她所深恶痛绝的，尤其是异教的神祇。倒是有一个有田有宅的汉子，叫人放心地通人情。即便到了我这把年纪，我仍要刻薄地想：他通人情通得叫人可叹。于是他俩结了婚，而嘲笑把我的翅膀啃掉了。

「我在脑海里搬演出凶杀、自尽与一片片荣耀的沙场，想以此来对抗这点小小的、刺痛人的嘲笑。羞惭之下，我打算把我的歌锁起来不让世人听见，好让人们从此再也听不见它们。世人甚至不知道我已经离去。没有一小群一小群的人来恳求我回去——而我曾向嘲笑保证过他们会的。我被啃咬过的翅膀脱落了；我成了个男子汉，不再想要天上的月亮。等我再想唱歌时，我的嗓音已变得沙哑，像赶牲口人的喉咙，而我的歌里塞满了先见与筹谋。」

===Original===
“I wonder how I grew,” Robert said. “I do not remember. Perhaps my youth went out of me sticking to coins—or perhaps it lives in those lands I used to dream of. But is swimming in his dreams, and sometimes I am very jealous of him.

“Do you know, Merlin, there is a thing which has appeared strange to me. My mother, Gwenliana, thought she had the second sight, and we humored her because she took such joy of it. And on the night that Henry went away, she cast a picture of his life. Merlin, nearly all her words have come to be the truth. Can these thoughts have come on her like a series of bright paintings? It is a strange, unlikely thing.”

===Chinese===
「我纳闷我是怎么长大的，」罗伯特说，「我记不得了。也许我的青春是粘在一枚枚钱币上从我身上流走的——又或许它仍活在我从前梦想的那些土地上。可他正在他的梦里遨游，有时我嫉妒他嫉妒得要命。

「你知道吗，默林，有一件事我一直觉得蹊跷。我母亲格温莉安娜，自以为有第二视觉（second sight），我们也都顺着她，因为她从中得到那么大的乐趣。就在亨利离家的那天夜里，她描画了他一生的图景。默林，她的话如今几乎全都成了真。这些念头难道会像一连串明亮的画儿那样降临到她头上？这真是件古怪而难以置信的事。」

===Original===
“Perhaps she read his desire, Robert, and sensed the strength of his desire. I taught old Gwenliana many things which had to do with magic; she was very apt at reading signs—and faces.”

===Chinese===
「也许她读出了他的欲望，罗伯特，也觉察出了他欲望的力量。我教过老格温莉安娜许多与巫术有关的东西；她极善于解读征兆——也善于解读面相。」

===Original===
Old Robert rose to stretch himself. “Ah, well—I must be going now. It takes a weary time for such an aged man to be getting down the path. It will be night when I am home again. Here is William coming with his pick which was an appendage born to him. I will be going down a bit in his company and learning the way of things at London. You must love words, Merlin, to be making so many of them; and I must love pain, to be engendering it against myself.

“And, Merlin, I think you are a trickster and a fraud; every time I have gone away from you it has been with the conviction that you have said mighty things, yet, on thinking, I could never recall any of them. I think you work a subtle conjuring with the soft voice of you, and your harps.”

===Chinese===
老罗伯特起身伸展了一下。「唉，也罢——我该走了。这么一个老头子下这条山路，得费好些功夫。等我到家，怕天都黑了。威廉拿着他那把镐来了，那镐简直像是他天生带着的一截肢体。我要随他走一段，顺便打听打听伦敦的事。默林，你一定很爱言辞，才会造出这么多来；我也一定很爱苦痛，才会这样给自己招来苦痛。

「还有，默林，我觉得你是个变戏法的骗子；每次我从你这儿离开，都深信你说了些了不起的话，可一旦细想，我却一句也回忆不起来。我觉得你是用你那轻柔的嗓音、和你的竖琴，变着一种微妙的戏法。」

===Original===
And as he went down the path, the hanging harps crooned after him the Sorcerer’s Farewell.

===Chinese===
当他顺着山路走下去时，悬挂着的竖琴在他身后低吟起巫师的告别（Sorcerer’s Farewell）。

## IV / IV

===Original===
Panama was a great, lovely city in 1670 when Henry Morgan determined on its destruction; a rich, strong city, and justly called the Cup of Gold. No place in all the raw New World could compare with it in beauty and in wealth.

Over a century before, Balboa had come to the shore of a new ocean. He dressed himself in scoured armor and waded into the Pacific until the gentle water washed his thighs. Then, in an oration, he firmly addressed the sea and claimed all lands it broke on for the crown of Spain. He demanded that the water be tractable and loyal, for it was to be the honored private lake of Castile and Aragon.

Behind Balboa, on the shore, huddled a small grass village of the Indians, and its name was Panama. In the native tongue this signified a place of good fishing. When the soldiers of Spain put torch to the litter of huts, and in its place built a new town, they kept the old name, Panama, which is a song. And soon the meaning justified itself, for out of this little town the nets of Spain were flung to the four directions.

===Chinese===
1670年，当亨利·摩根（Henry Morgan）决意要毁灭巴拿马（Panama）时，它还是一座宏伟而美丽的城市；一座富庶、坚固的城，被恰如其分地称作“金杯”（the Cup of Gold）。在整个蛮荒的新大陆，没有任何一个地方能在美貌与富庶上与它媲美。

一个多世纪以前，巴尔沃亚（Balboa）来到了一片新海洋的岸边。他披上擦得锃亮的甲胄，涉进太平洋，直到柔和的海水漫过他的大腿。随后他发表了一通演说，郑重地向大海宣告，把它所冲击的一切陆地都收归西班牙王冠之下。他勒令这海水要驯服、要忠诚，因为它将成为卡斯蒂利亚（Castile）与阿拉贡（Aragon）尊贵的私家湖泊。

在巴尔沃亚身后，岸上挤着印第安人的一个小小草屋村落，它的名字叫巴拿马。在土话里，这名字的意思是“一个捕鱼的好地方”。当西班牙的士兵放火点燃了那一堆乱糟糟的棚屋，在原处建起一座新城时，他们保留了那个旧名——巴拿马，一个如歌的名字。不久，这名字的含义便自己应验了，因为西班牙的渔网，正是从这座小镇撒向四方。

===Original===
Pedrarias carried the nets to the north and enmeshed the cities of the ancient Mayan race. He was enabled, of his fishing, to send strangely wrought serpents and frightful images and tiny graven insects, all of gold, to Panama. When there were no more ornaments to gather, when the temples were vacant boxes of stone, then Pedrarias threw the net of Spain over the people and drove them into the mines under his whips.

Pizarro sailed to the southward with horses and armored men, and the powerful Inca nation fell before him. He killed the rulers and ripped the life from the governmental structure. Then diamonds, plates from the temple walls, symbols of the sun in gold, and ceremonial golden shields were shipped to Panama. And Pizarro forced the broken Inca people into the mines with whips.

A hundred captains led little bands of soldiers to the east and southeast where the fierce Indians of Darien lived in trees and caves. Here the Spanish men found nose rings and anklets and god-sticks and eagle quills filled with gold. Everything was dumped into sacks and carried on mule-back to Panama. When all the graves had been robbed of golden ornaments, even the wild Indians dug in the earth by authority of the whips.

===Chinese===
佩德拉里亚斯（Pedrarias）把渔网带到了北方，网住了古老玛雅（Mayan）族的诸城。凭着这番捕捞，他能把那些奇形怪状的蛇、骇人的神像和细小的雕虫，通通赤金的，运回巴拿马。等到再没有饰物可搜刮，等到那些神庙都成了空荡荡的石匣子，佩德拉里亚斯便把西班牙的网罩到人身上，用鞭子把他们赶进矿里去。

皮萨罗（Pizarro）带着马匹和披甲的人驶向南方，强大的印加（Inca）帝国便覆灭在他脚下。他杀掉那里的统治者，把生命的血肉从政权的骨架上一寸寸撕下来。于是钻石、神庙墙上的金板、太阳的金徽、典礼用的金盾，都被一船船运往巴拿马。而皮萨罗也用鞭子把那些被击碎的印加人赶进了矿井。

成百个船长率领小股士兵向东、向东南而去，那里的达连（Darien）凶悍的印第安人住在树上和洞穴里。在这里，西班牙人找到了鼻环、脚链、神杖和灌满黄金的鹰羽。所有的东西都倒进麻袋，用骡子驮回巴拿马。等到所有的坟墓都被搜尽了金饰，就连那些野性的印第安人，也在鞭子的号令下钻进了泥土里去挖掘。

===Original===
The ships of Spain discovered little islands to the westward in whose shallow bays pearls might be found if only one dived deep enough; and in a little time the dull folk of the islands were jumping into a sea where sharks lived. And bags of pearls found their way to Panama.

All the long workings, the craftsmanship in precious things, came at last to Panama, where the melting pots received them like glowing gourmands and transformed them to thick golden logs. The warehouses were piled high with the sticks of gold, waiting for the treasure fleet to sail for Spain. At times there were bars of silver tiered up in the streets for lack of warehouse room, but they were safe from thievery in their weight.

Meanwhile the city grew to be a glorious thing. The wealth of enslaved nations was put to building thousands of fine houses with red roofs and little inset patios where rare secret flowers grew. All the colored arts and comforts of old Europe flew westward to beautify the Panamanian houses at the call of golden lumps.

===Chinese===
西班牙的船只在西边发现了一座座小岛，那儿的浅湾里，只要潜得够深，便能采到珍珠；不久，岛上那些迟钝的居民便纵身跳进了鲨鱼出没的大海。一袋袋珍珠，就这样源源不断地流向巴拿马。

所有那些漫长的劳作、那些珍物上的精工巧匠，最终都汇聚到巴拿马，那里的熔炉像一个个火红的饕餮之徒把它们吞下，炼成一根根粗大的金条。仓库里高高地堆满了金条，等着运宝舰队启航驶回西班牙。有时由于仓库不够用，银条便一摞摞码在街上，可它们因为分量太沉，反倒不怕人偷。

与此同时，这座城长成了一桩辉煌的事物。被奴役的诸民族的财富，被用来建造数以千计的华美宅邸，覆着红瓦屋顶，嵌着一个个小小的内庭，里面种着稀罕而隐秘的花。旧欧洲所有色彩缤纷的技艺和享受，都应着一块块金子的召唤飞越重洋，来装点巴拿马的宅第。

===Original===
The first Spaniards to invade the country had been cruel, grasping robbers; but also they were soldiers whom no bloody prospect might frighten. Small bands of them captured the New World with little force save spiritual courage. But when the peoples of Nicaragua and Peru and Darien had become gangs of whimpering slaves, when there was no danger any more, a different breed of men came to live in Panama. These were the merchants, keenly decisive when there was a farm to be wrested by law from its owner, or when the price of food was raised for outland colonists, but fearful and cowardly when steel was rattling about on steel.

The merchant class soon dominated all the isthmus. Some of the soldiers had died; others grew restless in security and marched away to new, dangerous lands, leaving the battle of foodstuffs and extravagances in the hands of the traders who doled out flour and wine, and gathered, in return, jewels and bars of gold for their coffers. The merchants combined so that all might charge the same high price for food, and with the profits they built their cedar houses roofed with rosy tiles; they dressed their women in foreign silks and were followed about in the streets by bands of retaining slaves.

A company of Genoese slave dealers came to the city and built a large warehouse for their merchandise. In it were tiers of cages where the black men sat until they were brought into the light to be felt over and bargained for.

===Chinese===
最早入侵这片国土的西班牙人是残忍、贪婪的强盗；但他们也是兵，没有哪一种血腥的前景能吓倒他们。他们一小股一小股地，几乎只凭着精神上的胆气，便拿下了新大陆。可等到尼加拉瓜（Nicaragua）、秘鲁（Peru）和达连的百姓都成了一群群啜泣的奴隶，等到再无危险可言，另一种血统的人便来巴拿马定居了。这些人是商人：在要靠法律从一个主人的手里抢走一座农庄时、在为了外乡的殖民者而抬高粮价时，他们果断凌厉；可一旦钢与钢铿锵相击，他们便胆战心惊、怯懦如鼠。

商人阶层很快便主宰了整个地峡。有些士兵已经死了；另一些在安逸中坐立不安，开拔到新的、危险的国土去了，把粮食与奢侈品的争夺留给了那些商人——他们论份配给面粉和酒，换回的是收进自己钱箱的珠宝和金条。商人们联合起来，好让所有人都能索取一样高昂的粮价，他们用这些利润建造起雪松木的宅子，覆着玫瑰色的瓦顶；他们给自家的女人穿上外邦的丝绸，走在街上身后总跟着一群家奴。

一帮热那亚（Genoese）奴隶贩子来到城里，为他们的货色盖了一座大仓库。里面是一层层笼子，黑人们坐在里面，直到被拖到光天化日之下，任人摸捏、讨价还价。

===Original===
It was a lovely city, Panama. Two thousand great cedar houses lined its principal streets, and farther from the center were five thousand smaller dwellings for clerks and messengers and paid soldiers of the King. Clustering in the outskirts were innumerable thatched huts where the slaves were quartered. In the center of the town were six churches, two convents, and a tall cathedral, all with full gold services and vestments heavy with jewels. Already two saints had lived and died in Panama﻿—not major saints, perhaps, but of enough importance to make their bones valuable.

One whole section of the city was crowded with the houses and stables and barracks of the King. Here, one-tenth of all the produce of the land was stored, waiting for the next plate fleet, when it would be carried on donkey back across the isthmus to be loaded on the ship. Panama was supporting the kingdom of Spain﻿—paying for the King’s new palaces and wars. Because of the ready money in his treasury, the King gave Panama a seat of dignity. It bore a proud name: The Very Noble and Very Loyal City of Panama. Its rank was made equal with that of Cordova and Seville, for did not its officials wear golden chains of office about their necks? And the King granted to the city a resplendent coat of arms﻿—a shield in a field of gold on the left yoke, and on the right, two caravels and a handful of gray arrows. Above all was the north star of discovery, while the Lions and Castles of the twin Spanish kingdoms circled around the shield. Truly Panama was one of the greatest cities in the world.

===Chinese===
巴拿马是一座美丽的城。两千座高大的雪松木宅邸排列在它的主街两侧，离市中心更远处是五千座较小的民居，供文书、信使和国王雇佣的士兵居住。城郊则簇拥着无数茅草棚屋，那是奴隶们的栖身之所。城中心有六座教堂、两座修道院和一座高耸的大教堂，全都备着成套的金质法器和缀满珠宝的法衣。已经有两位圣徒在巴拿马度过了他们的一生并死于斯——也许算不上顶级的大圣徒，但地位也够显要，足以让他们的遗骨价值连城。

城中有一整片区域，挤满了国王的宅邸、马厩和营房。这里存放着这片土地全部出产的十分之一，等着下一支银船队（plate fleet）到来，届时便用驴子驮着越过地峡，装运上船。巴拿马在供养整个西班牙王国——为国王的新宫殿和战争买单。因为国库里有了这样充裕的现银，国王便赐予巴拿马一席尊荣。它有了一个骄傲的名号：巴拿马至贵至忠之城（The Very Noble and Very Loyal City of Panama）。它的品级被抬到与科尔多瓦（Cordova）和塞维利亚（Seville）比肩，因为它的官员们颈上不也佩戴着金质的职衔链吗？国王还赐给这座城市一面辉煌的纹章——左边是金地上的一副牛轭和一面盾，右边是两艘轻型帆船和一握灰色的箭。最上方是那颗发现之星的北极星，而西班牙双王之国的雄狮与城堡则环绕着盾牌。巴拿马确确实实是世上最伟大的城市之一。

===Original===
The center of the Cup of Gold was a broad, paved plaza with a raised stand in the middle where music was played in the evening. While it played, the people strolled about, proving their positions by those to whom they spoke; the merchant aristocracy was very tender in its pride. A man might argue over the price of flour like a Jew in the daytime, but at night, in the Plaza, he bowed stiffly to acquaintances not so rich as he, and imperceptibly fawned on those richer.

They had grown soft in their security. The city was considered impregnable. On one side the sea protected it, and there were no foreign ships on the southern ocean anyway; to the landward side were walls and a broad morass which might be flooded in time of danger, making the city a veritable island. In addition, an attacking army must cut its way through the isthmian jungle to approach in force, must wind through narrow passes which might easily be defended by a small body of men. No one considered it possible that any sane leader could dream of the conquest of Panama. And so, when Campeche and Puerto Bello and Maracaibo fell to the buccaneers, the merchants of the Cup of Gold shrugged their shoulders and went about their usual business. It was unfortunate, of course; indeed it was sad that their own countrymen should be so used and robbed﻿—but what could they expect? Their cities were on the wrong ocean. Panama need never think of these disturbances except with pity. God was good, and business﻿—well, terrible; no money any more, and the farmers hanging on to their goods like thieves.

===Chinese===
金杯的中心是一片宽阔的铺石广场，当中有一座高起的乐台，入夜便奏起乐来。乐曲奏响时，人们在广场上踱来踱去，凭着与自己说话的人来印证自己的地位；商人贵族们的骄傲是极其娇嫩的。一个人白天可能像个犹太人那样为一袋面粉的价钱锱铢必较，可一到了夜里，在广场上，他便板着脸向那些不如他阔的熟人颔首致意，又不易察觉地向那些比他阔的人谄媚。

他们在安逸里养软了筋骨。这座城被认为是坚不可摧的。它的一面有大海庇护，再说南边的海洋上本也没有外邦的船只；靠陆地的一面有城墙和一片辽阔的沼泽，危急时可以放水淹没，使整座城变成一座名副其实的岛屿。再者，一支进攻的军队要大举逼近，必得一路砍着穿过地峡的丛林，必得蜿蜒穿过那些狭窄的山口，而那些山口只需一小股人马便能轻易守住。没有人觉得，哪个神志正常的领袖竟敢梦想征服巴拿马。于是，当坎佩切（Campeche）、贝略港（Puerto Bello）和马拉开波（Maracaibo）相继落入海盗之手时，金杯的商人们只是耸耸肩，照旧做他们的营生。当然，这是不幸的；自己的同胞竟被人这样糟蹋、劫掠，确实令人悲伤——可他们又能指望什么呢？他们的城市所在的那片大洋不对。巴拿马除了同情，大可不必把这些动乱放在心上。上帝是仁慈的，至于生意嘛——唉，糟透了；钱再也没有了，那些农户把东西攥得像贼一样紧。

===Original===
Don Juan Perez de Guzman was Governor of the Cup of Gold﻿—a quiet nobleman whose life was devoted to being a complete gentleman and nothing else. He drilled his little army, changed his uniforms, and looked with care to the marriages of his relatives. He had been a soldier all his life﻿—not a good campaigner, perhaps, but an extremely gallant officer. The communications he wrote to his subordinates were magnificent. His wording of a demand for surrender by an Indian village was above reproach. The people loved their Governor. He dressed so well; he was so proud, yet condescending. They cheered him daily as he clattered down the street with a troop of horse behind him. If there were any apprehension of attack, surely the gallant figure of Don Juan would reassure the people. His were the noblest blood and the richest warehouses in the city.

Thus they lived happily in Panama, going to the green country places when the hot days were in, and returning to the balls and receptions of the town during the rainy season. And this was the Cup of Gold when Henry Morgan determined on its destruction.

===Chinese===
唐·胡安·佩雷斯·德·古兹曼（Don Juan Perez de Guzman）是金杯的总督——一位安静的贵族，他的一生都致力于做一个彻头彻尾的绅士，别无其他。他操练他那支小小的军队，更换他的制服，并悉心安排亲眷们的婚事。他当了一辈子兵——也许算不上出色的征战之将，却是个极有骑士风度的军官。他写给下属的文书辞藻华美。他起草的那份勒令某个印第安村落投降的通牒，更是无可指摘。百姓爱戴他们的总督。他衣着考究；他是那样骄傲，却又那样礼贤下士、平易近人。每天他嗒嗒地策马沿街而过、身后跟着一队骑兵时，人们都向他欢呼。倘若对袭击有任何忧惧，唐·胡安那英武的身影也定能叫百姓安心。全城最高贵的血统和最殷实的仓库，都归他所有。

他们就这样在巴拿马幸福地过活，酷暑天里便去青翠的乡间别墅避暑，雨季一到便回到城里的舞会和宴席中去。当亨利·摩根决意要毁灭它时，这便是金杯的模样。

---

===Original===
One day the news crept into Panama that the terrible Morgan was coming in conquest. At first there was amused unbelief, but when more messengers came in, the city roused itself to frantic activity. The people rushed to the churches, confessed, kissed relics, and rushed home again. Hundreds of priests marched in procession bearing the Host through the streets. The dark brotherhood fiercely whipped themselves and dragged the heavy cross about for everyone to see. The broken walls went unmended; the rusty cannons were not replaced. Don Juan heard mass after mass, spoke to the frenzied people, and suggested a procession of all the priests in the city.

Horrible stories began to grow up﻿—how the buccaneers were not men at all, but animal things with heads like crocodiles and lions’ claws. Grave men discussed such possibilities in the streets.

===Chinese===
有一天，消息悄悄渗进了巴拿马：那个可怕的摩根要来征服此地了。起初人们带着觉得好笑的不信，可当越来越多的信使涌进城来，整座城便陷入了疯狂的活动。人们冲进教堂，忏悔，亲吻圣物，又冲回家去。数以百计的神父排成游行队伍，抬着圣体穿街过巷。黑衣兄弟会的人凶狠地鞭笞自己，拖着沉重的十字架到处给人看。破损的城墙没人去修；生锈的大炮也没有换。唐·胡安一场接一场地听弥撒，对狂乱的人们讲话，并提议让全城的神父举行一次大游行。

可怕的传说开始滋长——说那些海盗根本不是人，而是兽类，长着鳄鱼般的脑袋和狮子的爪子。庄重的人们在街头讨论着这种种可能。

===Original===
“The blessing of the day, Don Pedro.”

“The Virgin’s blessing, Don Guierrmo.”

“What is your thought of these robbers?”

“Ah! horrible, Don Guierrmo; horrible. They are said to be demons!”

“But do you think it possible, as I have heard, that Morgan himself has three arms and wields a sword in each?”

“Who can say, my friend! The devil has even greater powers than these, surely. Who can tell the limit of the Power of Evil? It is sacrilegious to consider it.”

And later:

“You say you had it of Don Guierrmo? Surely he would not tell a questionable thing﻿—a man of his wealth.”

“I repeat only what he said﻿—that Morgan could fire bullets from his fingertips﻿—that he breathed out sulphurous flames. Don Guierrmo was certain of it.”

“I must tell my wife of this, Don Pedro.”

===Chinese===
“唐·佩德罗（Don Pedro），愿今日的祝福与你同在。”

“愿圣母祝福你，唐·吉耶尔莫（Don Guierrmo）。”

“你怎么看这些强盗？”

“哎呀！可怕呀，唐·吉耶尔莫；可怕。据说他们是魔鬼！”

“可你觉得可能吗——我听人说——摩根本人长着三条胳膊，每只手都挥一把剑？”

“谁能说得准呢，我的朋友！魔鬼的本事比这还要大，那是肯定的。谁能道尽恶之力的尽头呢？去想这件事，本身就是亵渎。”

后来——

“你说这是听唐·吉耶尔莫说的？像他那样有身家的人，总不会讲不靠谱的话吧。”

“我不过转述他的话罢了——摩根能从指尖射出子弹——他嘴里吐出硫磺的火焰。唐·吉耶尔莫对此确信不疑。”

“我得把这事告诉我妻子，唐·佩德罗。”

===Original===
So the tales grew until the people were half mad. Stories of cruelty in other captured cities were recalled, and the merchants who had shrugged before, turned pale on remembering. They could not believe; and yet they must believe, for the pirates were already on their way to Chagres, and their stated purpose was the conquest and pillage of the Cup of Gold. At last, under pressure, Don Juan dragged himself from church long enough to send five hundred soldiers for an ambush on the road across the isthmus. A young officer craved audience.

“Well, young man,” the Governor began, “what is your wish?”

“If we had bulls, sir﻿—if we had great numbers of wild bulls,” the officer cried excitedly.

“Get them! Have the whole country scoured for bulls! Let the men gather a thousand of them! But what are we going to do with them?”

“We should stampede them on the enemy, sir.”

“Marvelous plan! Genius of an officer! Ah, my dear friend﻿—a thousand bulls? A thousand? I jested. Have them gather ten thousand of the wildest bulls.”

===Chinese===
传说就这样越传越凶，直到百姓们都半疯了。其他陷落之城里那些惨无人道的故事被一一记起，先前耸过肩的商人们，想起来便面无人色。他们无法相信；可又不得不信，因为海盗们已经在向查格雷斯（Chagres）进发的路上了，而他们公开宣告的目的，正是征服并洗劫金杯。最后，在重压之下，唐·胡安勉强从教堂里脱身了一小会儿，派出五百名士兵，去地峡那条横贯的大路上设伏。一名年轻军官求见。

“那么，年轻人，”总督开口道，“你有什么想法？”

“要是我们有公牛就好了，先生——要是有大群大群的野公牛，”那军官激动地喊道。

“去弄！把整个乡野搜个遍，把公牛都找来！叫人去凑一千头！不过，我们拿它们怎么办？”

“我们应该把它们朝敌人赶过去，先生。”

“绝妙的主意！天才的军官！啊，我亲爱的朋友——一千头公牛？一千头？我不过是说笑罢了。叫他们去凑一万头最野的公牛。”

===Original===
The Governor ordered out his soldiers﻿—two thousand of the King’s troops﻿—reviewed them, and then returned to kneel in the Cathedral. Don Juan was not afraid of fighting, but, like a prudent general, he was strengthening secondary defense. Besides, anything that cost as much as the masses he had paid for must have some effect.

The first creeping rumor grew to a monster. Quaking citizens began to bury the plate from their houses. The churchmen threw chalices and candlesticks into the cisterns for safety, and walled up their more precious relics in passages underground.

Balboa would have strengthened the walls and flooded the approach. Pizarro’s army would have been halfway across the isthmus, by this time, to meet the oncoming buccaneers. But those brave times were past. The merchants of Panama thought only of their possessions, their lives, and their souls﻿—in the order named. They never considered belting on swords or toiling at the disintegrated walls. That was for the soldiers of the King, who were paid good money to protect the citizens. The Governor must see to the defense.

Don Juan had reviewed his troops; that, to his mind, was all any general could do. The uniforms were proof against criticism, and his soldiers would have come with credit through any parade ground in Europe. Meanwhile, another mass would not hurt matters.

===Chinese===
总督调出了他的士兵——两千名国王的御林军——检阅了一番，然后又回去跪在大教堂里了。唐·胡安并非害怕打仗，而是像一位审慎的将军那样，在加强第二道防线。再者说，花了他那么多钱——跟他付弥撒的钱一样多的事，总该有点用处。

起初悄悄蔓延的谣言，长成了一头巨怪。瑟瑟发抖的市民开始把家里的银餐具埋进地下。教士们把圣杯和烛台扔进蓄水池里保全，把更珍贵的圣物砌进了地下的暗道。

要是巴尔沃亚，他会加固城墙、淹掉进城的通道。要是皮萨罗的军队，此刻早已穿过半个地峡，去迎击来犯的海盗了。可那些英勇的时代已经过去了。巴拿马的商人们只惦记着他们的财产、他们的性命、他们的灵魂——按这个顺序。他们压根儿没想过要佩上剑、去那坍圮的城墙下卖苦力。那是国王的士兵们该干的事，他们拿了好价钱，本就该保护市民。防御的事，得总督去操心。

唐·胡安检阅过他的军队了；在他看来，这已是一位将军所能做的一切。军服经得起任何挑剔，他的士兵放进欧洲任何一座阅兵场上都能体面过关。再者说，再多听一场弥撒，也无损大局。

### I / I

===Original===
While the buccaneers were throwing away the savings of plundered Maracaibo, Henry Morgan plunged deeply into plans for his new conquest. It would require more fighting men than had ever before been assembled. The messengers of Captain Morgan went out to the four corners of the Spanish Main. His words found their way to Plymouth, and Nieuw Amsterdam. Even to the wooded islands where men lived like apes, went his invitation to the great pillage.

“Every man will be rich if we succeed”; so ran the message. “This will be the most powerful blow ever struck by the Brotherhood. We will carry terror to the inmost heart of Spain. Our fleet gathers on the south side of Tortuga by October.”

And soon the ships and men poured to the place of rendezvous; enormous new vessels with white sails and carven prows, ships bristling with brass cannons, old rotting hulks, their bottoms so foul with weeds that they moved through the water like logs. There came sloops and long canoes and flatboats, forced through the water with sweeps. Even rafts drew to the meeting place, under woven palm sails.

===Chinese===
当海盗们还在挥霍着从劫来的马拉开波搜刮到的积蓄时，亨利·摩根已深深一头扎进了新一轮征服的谋划之中。这将需要召集前所未有之多的战斗人手。摩根船长的信使被派往西属大陆（the Spanish Main）的四隅八荒。他的话传到了普利茅斯（Plymouth），传到了新阿姆斯特丹（Nieuw Amsterdam）。甚至连那些林木葱茏、人如猿猴般栖居其上的岛屿，也收到了他赴这场大劫掠的邀约。

“只要事成，人人发财”——口信就是这样传的。“这将是兄弟会有史以来最沉重的一击。我们要把恐怖送进西班牙的心脏深处。十月里，我们的舰队在托尔图加（Tortuga）南岸集结。”

很快，船只和人马便涌向了集结之地：崭新的巨舰张着白帆、雕着船首像，船上铜炮林立；也有腐朽的废船，船底积满水草，在水中像圆木一样笨重地挪动。还有单桅快船、长长的独木舟和平底船，靠长桨硬撑着在水里前行。甚至还有木筏，张着编织的棕榈叶帆，也驶到了会合处。

===Original===
And the men﻿—all the blustering Brotherhood of Tortuga; the old, expert pirates of Goaves; Frenchmen, Netherlanders, English, and Portuguese﻿—the embattled outcasts of the world. Canoe loads of slaves who had escaped the Spaniards paddled in, drawn to this expedition by a thirsting for their master’s blood. The slaves were Caribs and Negroes and fevered whites. Little groups of hunters appeared on the beaches of jungle islands and took ship for the south side of Tortuga.

Among the major ships were tall frigates and galleons which had been captured in old engagements. When the time came for departure, Captain Morgan had thirty-seven ships under his hand, and two thousand fighting men in addition to the mariners and boys. In the crowd of shipping lay three slim, clean sloops from New England. They had not come to fight, but to trade﻿—powder for plunder, whisky for gold. Powder and whisky were the two great weapons of offense. And besides, these Plymouth men would buy old, useless ships for the iron and cordage they contained.

===Chinese===
至于人呢——全都是托尔图加那帮咋咋呼呼的兄弟会；戈阿夫（Goaves）那些老练的海盗；法兰西人、荷兰人、英格兰人、葡萄牙人——全天下被放逐的、枕戈待旦的亡命之徒。一舟舟从西班牙人手中逃脱的奴隶也划着桨来了，是被对主人鲜血的渴望吸引到这趟远征里来的。这些奴隶里有加勒比人（Caribs）、黑人和发着烧的白人。一小股一小股的猎人出现在丛林岛屿的滩头，登船驶往托尔图加南岸。

那些大船中间，有高耸的快速帆船和昔日战事中缴获的西班牙大帆船（galleon）。启航之时，摩根船长麾下已有三十七艘船，水手和船上童子之外，还有两千名战斗人员。在那一片樯橹之间，停着三艘来自新英格兰（New England）的纤细、干净的单桅快船。它们不是来打仗的，而是来做买卖的——以火药换战利品，以威士忌换黄金。火药和威士忌，是两大利器的进攻武器。再说，这些普利茅斯人还会买下那些没用的旧船，图的是船上的铁件和缆绳。

===Original===
Captain Morgan had sent hunters into the woods to shoot cattle, and ships to the mainland to steal grain. When all returned, there was food on hand for a voyage.

No one save Coeur de Gris and Henry Morgan, of all this polyglot press of men who had come at the call of conquest, knew where that conquest was to be. No one dreamed where they would be sailing and whom fighting at the journey’s end. The army of brave thieves had trooped to the name of Morgan, thirstily confident in his promise of unlimited plunder.

Henry Morgan had not dared to tell his destination. Potent though his name was, the buccaneers would have recoiled from such an impregnable objective. If they were given time to think of Panama, they would run home out of dread, for stories of the power and protection of the Cup of Gold had been told in all their islands for over half a century. Panama was a cloud city, an eerie, half-unearthly place, and armed with lightnings. Of course, there were those who believed the streets paved with golden cobbles, and certain church windows carved from single emeralds. These legends would draw them on, if only they had no time to think of the hazards as well.

===Chinese===
摩根船长派猎人进树林去猎牛，派船到大陆去偷粮食。等所有人都回来，手头就有了够一次航行的口粮。

在所有这些应征服之召而来的、操着各色语言的乌泱泱的人群里，除了柯尔·德·格里（Coeur de Gris）和亨利·摩根，没有一个人知道这一征服的目标究竟在何处。没有一个人梦想得到，旅程终了他们要驶向何方、与谁交战。这支由勇敢的窃贼组成的大军，是冲着摩根这个名字蜂拥而至的，他们渴切地信赖着他那掠夺无禁的承诺。

亨利·摩根没敢吐露他的目的地。尽管他的名号如雷贯耳，可这样一个坚不可摧的目标，海盗们也会望而却步。只要给他们时间去想巴拿马，他们就会吓得跑回家去，因为金杯的权势与庇护的故事，在他们的岛屿上已经流传了半个多世纪。巴拿马是一座云端之城，一个可怖的、半属尘外的所在，还披挂着闪电。当然，也有人相信那里的街道铺着金鹅卵石，相信教堂的某些窗子是用整块翡翠雕成的。这些传说会引诱他们向前——只要他们也无暇去想其中的凶险。

===Original===
When the ships had been careened and scraped, all the sails mended, the cannons scoured and tested, the holds filled with foodstuffs, then Henry Morgan called a meeting of his captains for the purpose of signing solemn articles and dividing the fleet into commands.

They gathered in the oaken cabin of the Admiral﻿—thirty captains who had brought ships to the mission. The frigate of Captain Morgan was a fine Spanish man-o’-war. It had been commanded by a Duke before it fell into the pirates’ hands. The cabin resembled a large drawing-room, paneled in dark oak, its walls drawing slightly inward at the top. Across the ceiling were heavy beams carved with vines and delicate, slim leaves. On one wall had been the painted arms of Spain, but a dagger had scraped and scratched it nearly out of sight.

Captain Morgan sat behind a broad table of which each leg was a curious carved lion, and around him, seated on stools, were the thirty leaders of his fleet and army. They waited impatiently for his communication.

===Chinese===
等船底都倾斜刮净了，所有帆都补好了，大炮都擦亮试过了，船舱都装满了口粮，亨利·摩根便召集他的船长们开会，为的是签署庄严的盟约，并把舰队划分成各个分遣队。

他们聚集在旗舰的橡木船舱里——来赴此役的三十位船长悉数到场。摩根船长的那艘快速帆船是一艘上好的西班牙战舰。落到海盗手里之前，它曾由一位公爵指挥。这间船舱像一间宽大的客厅，镶着深色橡木护壁，墙壁向顶端微微内收。天花板上横着粗重的房梁，雕着藤蔓和纤巧细长的叶子。一面墙上原本绘有西班牙的纹章，可一把匕首已把它刮划得几乎看不见了。

摩根船长坐在一张宽大的桌子后面，桌子的每一条腿都是一只雕工奇巧的狮子。围着他、坐在矮凳上的，是他舰队和陆军的三十位头领。他们不耐烦地等着他开口。

===Original===
There was the short, serious Captain Sawkins, whose eyes burned with the fervor of Puritanism. He justified his murders with Scripture and offered prayers of thanksgiving from a gun carriage after a successful rape.

Black Grippo was there, an old man now, and sagging under his unimportant infamies. He had come, finally, to regard his God as a patient policeman whom one might possibly outwit. Lately he had reasoned that he might flee his sins by a general confession and reconfirmation in his mother church, and this he intended to do when one more expedition should provide him with a golden candlestick to bear to the confessing priest by way of peace offering.

Holbert and Tegna, Sullivan and Meyther, sat on stools surrounding Captain Morgan. In a dark corner were two whom the whole Brotherhood knew as inseparables. They were called simply The Burgundian and The Other Burgundian. The first was a little fat man with a face like a red bloated sun. He was nervous and excitable. The slightest public attention threw him into a fit of embarrassment. When he was spoken to his face became redder, and he gave the impression of a bug frantically looking for a board under which to hide. His companion, The Other Burgundian, was his defender and guide. The Other Burgundian was taller and more powerfully made, although his left arm was gone at the elbow. These two might have been seen at any time walking together, sitting together. They seldom spoke, but always the good arm of The Other Burgundian would be about the shoulders of his dumpy friend in a gesture of protection.

===Chinese===
有矮个子、一脸严肃的索金斯船长（Captain Sawkins），他的双眼燃烧着清教徒的狂热。他用《圣经》为自己的杀戮辩护，又一次成功的强暴之后，他会站在炮车上献上感恩祷告。

黑皮肤的格里波（Grippo）也在场，他如今已是老人，被自己那些无关紧要的恶行压得有点佝偻。他终于把他的上帝看作一个耐心的警察，一个或许还能侥幸骗过去的人。近来他盘算着，靠一次总的告解和在母会里的重新受洗，兴许就能逃离自己的罪愆；他打算等再来一趟远征、弄到一支金烛台带去给听告解的神父作谢罪的礼物，便付诸行动。

霍尔伯特（Holbert）和特格纳（Tegna），沙利文（Sullivan）和梅瑟（Meyther），围坐在摩根船长四周的矮凳上。一个阴暗的角落里坐着两个全兄弟会都知道的形影不离之人。人们干脆叫他们“勃艮第人”（The Burgundian）和“另一个勃艮第人”（The Other Burgundian）。头一个是矮矮胖胖的男人，一张脸像一轮红肿的太阳。他神经质，容易激动。哪怕只有一丁点众人的注意落到他身上，他也会窘迫得手足无措。有人当面跟他说话，他的脸就更红了，活像一只发疯似地找木板往底下钻的甲虫。他的同伴，那“另一个勃艮第人”，是他的护卫与向导。另一个勃艮第人个子更高，体格也更壮，虽然他左臂从肘下没了。这两人随时随地都在一起走、一起坐。他们很少说话，但那另一个勃艮第人完好的那条胳膊，总搭在他那矮墩墩朋友的肩头，做出一副保护的姿态。

===Original===
Captain Morgan made his voice harsh and cold for his speech. There was a deep silence while he read the articles. A man who brought a ship might draw such and such rent; a carpenter with tools was to be paid so much; such amounts would be set aside for dependents of the slain. Then he came to the rewards to the first man to sight an enemy; the first to kill a Spaniard; the first into the city. The articles were finished.

“Now, sign,” demanded Captain Morgan, and the men shuffled to the table and inscribed their names or marks.

When they were seated again, Sawkins spoke out.

“The rewards are four times as great as custom demands. Why is that?” Sawkins’ training had made him abhor waste.

“The men will need bravery,” Henry Morgan said calmly. “They will need urging﻿—for we go to Panama.”

“Panama!” It was almost a groan that answered him.

“Yes, Panama. You have signed articles﻿—and I hang deserters. Look to the spirits of your men. You know something of the wealth of Panama﻿—enough to whet their tongues; and I know the dangers well enough to be sure they are surmountable.”

“But﻿—Panama﻿—” Sawkins began.

“I hang deserters,” Captain Morgan said, and he left the cabin. Coeur de Gris remained to listen. He would report the temper of the men.

===Chinese===
摩根船长把嗓音压得又粗又冷来念他的训词。他宣读盟约时，一片深沉的寂静。带船来的人可领某数目的租金；带工具的木匠付某数目的工钱；阵亡者的眷属拨出某数目的抚恤。接着他念到赏格——头一个发现敌人者；头一个杀掉一个西班牙人者；头一个冲进城者。盟约念完了。

“现在，签字，”摩根船长命令道。众人拖着步子挪到桌前，签下名字或画了押。

等他们重新坐下，索金斯开了口。

“这些赏格是惯例的四倍。这是为什么？”索金斯所受的训练使他痛恨浪费。

“弟兄们会需要勇气的，”亨利·摩根平静地说，“他们会需要点鼓劲——因为我们要去巴拿马。”

“巴拿马！”回答他的几乎是一声呻吟。

“是的，巴拿马。你们签了盟约——而我向来绞死逃兵。各自去稳住手下的心气。你们多少知道点巴拿马的富足——够吊起他们的胃口了；而那些凶险，我也清楚得很，我有把握它们都能克服。”

“可是——巴拿马——”索金斯才开口。

“我绞死逃兵，”摩根船长说罢，便离开了船舱。柯尔·德·格里留下来听动静。他要去禀报众人的情绪。

===Original===
There was long silence. Every man was recalling the things he had heard about Panama.

“It is dangerous,” said Sawkins, at last, “dangerous, but goodly rich. And the captain swore he knew the plan of the city and all the dangers of the fight.”

These words brought sudden reassurance. If Captain Morgan knew, then they need not fear. Morgan was infallible. The room filled with nervous, quick conversation.

“Money? They walk on it. I have heard that the Cathedral﻿—”

“But the jungle is impassable.”

“They have good wine in Panama. I tasted some that came from there.”

And all at once, every man seemed to think of the Red Saint.

“Why, that woman is there﻿—La Santa Roja.”

“Yes, that is right. She is there. Who do you suppose will get her?”

“The captain’s not a man for women at all. I think it will be Coeur de Gris, here. He is the most favored of us.”

===Chinese===
一阵长久的沉默。每个人都在回想自己听过的关于巴拿马的种种。

“危险是危险，”索金斯终于说，“危险，可也富得流油。再说船长发过誓，说他知道那城的布局，也知道这一仗的全部凶险。”

这几句话一下子带来了安慰。既然摩根船长知道，那他们就没什么好怕的了。摩根是不会错的。满屋立刻响起了紧张的、急切的交谈。

“钱？他们在钱上头走路呢。我听说那大教堂——”

“可那丛林根本过不去。”

“巴拿马有的是好酒。我尝过从那儿来的。”

忽然间，每个人似乎都想起了红圣女。

“嗳，那女人就在那儿——红圣女（La Santa Roja）。”

“没错，是这样。她就在那儿。你们猜谁能弄到手？”

“船长压根儿不是个沾女人的人。我看哪，会是这儿的柯尔·德·格里。他是我们里头最受宠的。”

===Original===
“Well enough. Coeur de Gris is fated to die on the poniard of some man’s jealousy. I would not mind killing him, because if I did not, someone else would. No, it might be my dagger.”

“What would you do with a woman like that? A rope’s end wouldn’t be the thing, I guess.”

“Well, to tell the truth, I have always found those fat doubloons the most perfect instruments of rape. They glitter so.”

“No, no. But see this. Nearly all women will repurchase their diamonds with their virtue. When you have the second, it is an easy thing to reacquire the first.”

“What does old One-arm say about it﻿—The Other Burgundian? Hey! will you be taking the Red Saint for your fat friend there?”

The Other Burgundian bowed.

“There would be no need,” he said. “My friend is very capable. Why, I could tell a tale﻿—” He turned to The Burgundian. “Have I your permission, Emil?”

The Burgundian seemed trying to get through the wall, but he did manage a nod.

===Chinese===
“那也好。柯尔·德·格里命中注定要死在哪个男人嫉妒的匕首下。我倒不介意亲手杀了他，因为我不杀，自然有别人杀。不，说不定就是我那把匕首。”

“弄到那样的女人，你打算怎么办呢？我猜，绳头鞭子怕是不顶用。”

“嗳，说老实话，我历来觉得那些胖墩墩的达布隆金币（doubloon）才是最称手的施暴利器。它们闪亮得很呢。”

“不，不。可你瞧。几乎所有的女人都肯拿她们的贞操换回她们的钻石。只要你拿到了第二样，再弄回头一样就容易了。”

“那老家伙独臂怎么说——另一个勃艮第人？嗨！你要替你那胖朋友把红圣女弄到手吗？”

那另一个勃艮第人欠了欠身。

“不必，”他说，“我这位朋友是很能干的。嗳，我能讲个故事——”他转向那勃艮第人。“我可以讲吗，埃米尔（Emil）？”

那勃艮第人看起来恨不得钻进墙里去，可他还是勉强点了点头。

===Original===
“Then I will tell you gentlemen a story,” The Other Burgundian began. “There were four friends in Burgundy; three who squeezed a little sour milk from the dugs of art, and one who had possessions. Also there was a lovely girl in Burgundy; beautiful, accomplished, a veritable Circe, most lovely in the country. And the four friends all fell in love with this sweet exquisite.

“Each one gave her the gifts which were most dear to himself. The first folded his soul in a sonnet and laid it at her feet. The second filled a viol with her name; and I﻿—the third, I mean﻿—painted the rosy image of her face. Thus did we artists bid for her in all friendliness to one another. But the last of the four was the true artist. He was quiet; he was subtle. What an actor! He won her with a superb gesture. He opened his hand﻿—so﻿—and there, on the cushion of his palm, lay a laughing rose pearl. They were married.

“Now, soon after this marriage, Delphine gave evidence of greater virtues than anyone had suspected. Not only was this paragon a perfect wife, but she was also the discreet and delightful mistress﻿—not to one, but to all three﻿—of the husband’s friends. And Emil, the husband, did not mind. He loved his friends. Why not? They were his true friends, but poor.

===Chinese===
“那我就给诸位讲个故事，”另一个勃艮第人开了口。“从前在勃艮第有四个朋友；其中三个从艺术的胸膛里挤一点酸牛奶度日，还有一个是有产业的。勃艮第还有一位可爱的姑娘；美丽、多才多艺，一个名副其实的喀耳刻（Circe），全乡最美的姑娘。这四个朋友全都爱上了这位娇滴滴的可人儿。

“每个人送她的，都是他自己最珍视的东西。头一个把自己的灵魂折进一首十四行诗，放在她脚下。第二个用她的名字注满了一把维奥尔琴；而我——我是说第三个——画下了她脸庞上玫瑰色的影像。我们这些艺术家就这样彼此友善地竞价。可四个人里最后那一个，才是真正的艺术家。他沉静；他精微。多好的演员啊！他以一个绝妙的手势赢得了她。他摊开手——就这样——于是，在他掌心的软垫上，躺着一颗含笑的玫瑰色珍珠。他们结了婚。

“婚后不久，德尔菲娜（Delphine）便展现出比任何人所预料的更多的美德。这位完人不仅是个贤妻，还是丈夫那三位朋友——不是一个，而是全部三个——审慎而迷人的情妇。而做丈夫的埃米尔，全不在意。他爱他的朋友。为什么不呢？他们是他真正的朋友，只是穷。

===Original===
“Ah, where is a force so blind, so idiotic, as public opinion! This time, two deaths and one banishment were born of it. This hydra of a Public Opinion﻿—consider to yourselves what it did! It forced Emil to challenge his three friends. Even then, all might have ended with the kiss, the embrace﻿—‘my honor is whole again, dear friend’﻿—if it had not been for Emil’s deplorable habit of leaving his rapier point in putrefying meat. Two of the men died, and I lost my arm.

“Now, here again comes this Public Opinion, like a blundering, powerful ox. Having forced the duels, it forced the victor out of France. Here is Emil, beside me﻿—lover, swordsman, artist, landowner. The Public Opinion﻿—But I have strayed from the tale in my hatred of this force. What I wanted to tell you is that Emil asks no consideration, no quarter at all.
